# Sentinel Codebase Audit — 2026-06-17

> Produced by a 20-lane multi-agent audit (Claude Opus 4.8). Each finding came from a subsystem finder and was then **independently adversarially verified** against the cited code — only findings a verifier rated `confirmed` or `uncertain` appear here; false positives were dropped. Severities are the verifier's **corrected** rating (`finalSeverity`).

## Remediation status — updated 2026-06-18

Fixes are landing on branch **`fix/audit-2026-06-17`** (off `main`), one commit per concern, `tsc --noEmit` + tests between phases, full suite green (747 tests). Phase ordering: P1s first, then P2 by concern, then P3 batches. Phase 3 (optimizer/backtest fidelity) is deferred pending an admin optimizer re-run + market-closed deploy.

| Severity | Fixed | Pending | Total |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | 8 | 0 | 8 |
| P2 | 24 | 6 | 30 |
| P3 | 22 | 26 | 48 |
| **Total** | **54** | **32** | **86** |

Raw findings: **108** → confirmed: **81**, uncertain: **5**, refuted: **22**.

**No P0 and no confirmed cross-tenant data leak.** Each finding below is tagged ✅ FIXED or ⬜ PENDING. Notable pending clusters: sector-exposure cap (needs validator + Trader-page UI), optimizer/backtest fidelity (Phase 3, high-risk), and the remaining P3 hardening themes (data-ingestion, schedulers, indicators/momentum, screener cache).

## Findings

### 1. ✅ FIXED · [P1] Take-profit graduation locks stop ABOVE current price when TP trigger < entry×1.30, forcing immediate exit and an invalid broker stop

- **Lane:** engine-exit-stops · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2211-2216, 5073-5133, 6552-6574`

**Description.** When graduation is enabled (optimized + tactical-smart, MODE_GRADUATION_DEFAULT lines 2142/2145) and price first crosses pos.takeProfit, runScan calls promoteToGraduationFloor() which unconditionally sets pos.stopLoss = entryPrice × 1.30 (GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT = 0.30, line 1956). promoteToGraduationFloor (2211-2216) only guards `if (pos.stopLoss >= lockStop) return false` — it NEVER checks that the lock price is below the current/trigger price. But the take-profit trigger is frequently far below entry×1.30: optimized mode sets takeProfit = currentPrice + atr_14 × tpAtrMult (line 5371; tpAtrMult range 3-15, optimizer.ts:97), so a $100 stock with ATR $2 and mult 5 gets takeProfit = entry×1.10. tactical-smart's preset fallback is takeProfitPct 0.06 → entry×1.06 (strategy-presets.ts:58). When price crosses, e.g., entry×1.10, graduation fires, locks stopLoss to entry×1.30, then the `if (!shouldExit)` chain immediately hits `if (currentPrice <= heldPosition.stopLoss)` (line 5104: 1.10×entry <= 1.30×entry = true) and sells at market labeled 'Stop loss hit'. The same fires in runExitCheck's 1-min poll (effectiveStop = max(fixedStop, trailStop), line 2821-2824). Additionally syncBrokerStops (targetStop = max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop), line 6566) then tries to place/replace a SELL STOP at entry×1.30 while market is at entry×1.10 — Alpaca rejects a sell stop whose stop_price is at/above the current price, so the broker stop placement also fails.

**Impact.** The entire graduation feature (documented intent: 'ride runners past take-profit') is defeated for the majority of positions — any stock whose ATR-based or preset take-profit lands below +30% is force-exited at the take-profit crossing instead of held. The position exits immediately on the next scan/poll, and the protective broker stop placement is rejected, briefly leaving the position broker-unprotected until the in-memory exit fires.

**Suggested fix.** In promoteToGraduationFloor, clamp the lock to below the current price (e.g., pass currentPrice and use `const lockStop = Math.min(pos.entryPrice * (1 + GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT), currentPrice * (1 - SMALL_BUFFER))`), or only graduate-lock when `pos.entryPrice * (1 + GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT) < currentPrice`. The graduation floor should never exceed the price that triggered graduation.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> All three legs of the finding hold up against the code. (1) promoteToGraduationFloor (trading-engine.ts:2211-2216) unconditionally sets pos.stopLoss = entryPrice × 1.30 (GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT = 0.30, line 1956) with only an `if (pos.stopLoss >= lockStop) return false` guard — it never checks the lock against the current/trigger price. (2) In runScan, graduation fires for ANY currentPrice >= heldPosition.takeProfit (line 5075), and the take-profit is frequently far below entry×1.30: optimized mode computes takeProfit = currentPrice + atr_14 × tpAtrMult (line 5371-5372), with tpAtrMult range 3-15 (optimizer.ts:97). A large-cap with ATR ~2% of price needs mult > ~15 just to reach +30%, so virtually all optimized positions get takeProfit < entry×1.30. tactical-smart's preset fallback is takeProfitPct 0.06 → entry×1.06 (strategy-presets.ts:46/58, used via resolveStrategy line 2705 when no optimizer run exists). entryPrice is set to currentPrice at buy time (line 5455) and takeProfit to the computed level (line 5458), so takeProfit-to-entry ratio is genuinely < 1.30. (3) After graduation locks the stop to entry×1.30 (above current price), the exit chain's `if (!shouldExit)` block at line 5102 runs the stop-loss check at line 5104 `if (currentPrice <= heldPosition.stopLoss)` UNCONDITIONALLY for graduated positions — there is no bypass — so currentPrice (e.g. entry×1.10) <= stop (entry×1.30) is TRUE and the position is force-sold labeled "Stop loss hit" on the very same scan that graduated it. The 1-min runExitCheck replicates this: effectiveStop = max(fixedStop=pos.stopLoss, trailStop) (line 2821) → currentPrice <= effectiveStop (line 2823) → exit. syncBrokerStops sets targetStop = max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop) (line 6566) and would submit a sell stop at entry×1.30 above market, which Alpaca rejects (broker-unprotected, though briefly since the in-memory exit fires first). The author's intended model is documented as graduation firing at +50% (ENGINE_RULESET.md:218 "worst case, you lock in +30%") and the unit tests only exercise the >+30% scenario (take-profit-graduation.test.ts:87-95 "position ran to +60%"), confirming the sub-30% case was overlooked. The feature ("ride runners past take-profit") is defeated for the majority of positions in optimized (the CLAUDE.md-recommended live mode) and fallback tactical-smart. Severity P1 is correct — it silently defeats a core feature and causes premature forced exits affecting live P&L. The only mild overstatement is the broker-unprotected window, which is brief because the market exit fires in the same scan; the dominant harm is the forced premature exit.

**Evidence:** trading-engine.ts:1956 (GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT=0.30); 2211-2216 (promoteToGraduationFloor only guards `pos.stopLoss >= lockStop`, never vs currentPrice); 5074-5095 (graduation fires when currentPrice >= takeProfit, calls promoteToGraduationFloor); 5102-5114 (`if (!shouldExit)` then 5104 `if (currentPrice <= heldPosition.stopLoss)` unconditionally for graduated positions → "Stop loss hit"); 5371-5373 (optimized takeProfit = currentPrice + atr×mult); 5455/5458 (entryPrice=currentPrice, takeProfit=takeProfitPrice); 2820-2842 (runExitCheck effectiveStop=max(fixedStop,trailStop), exit on currentPrice<=effectiveStop; comment lines 2832-2835 assumes stop locked at entry×1.30 protects); 6566 (syncBrokerStops targetStop=max(pos.stopLoss,trailStop) → sell stop above market); optimizer.ts:97 (takeProfitAtrMult range 3-15); strategy-presets.ts:46,58 (swing/tactical-smart takeProfitPct 0.06); trading-engine.ts:2705 (tactical-smart falls back to STRATEGY_PRESETS.swing); ENGINE_RULESET.md:218 (design assumes +50% trigger); tests/unit/take-profit-graduation.test.ts:87-95 (only tests >+30% scenario, never the sub-30% bug case)

### 2. ✅ FIXED · [P1] Tactical & tactical-smart strategy exits/swaps bypass daily-loss and consecutive-loss halt accounting

- **Lane:** engine-risk-halts · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3855-3870, 4165-4181, 4454-4462 (vs 2868-2880, 5189-5198)`

**Description.** Only two exit sites feed the risk counters: runExitCheck's per-position stop/take-profit (line 2870 `engine.dailyLoss += pnl<0?pnl:0`, 2874 `recordTradeResult`) and runScanInner's exits (5190/5193). The tactical mass-exit ('SPY below exit SMA -> sell everything', lines 3859-3869), the tactical-smart mass-exit (lines 4168-4180), and the tactical-smart swap-sell of the weak position (lines 4454-4462) all realize P&L (`bp.unrealizedPnl` / `pos.unrealizedPnl`, which can be deeply negative) but update only `realizedPnlThisScan` and `tradesThisScan`. They NEVER touch `engine.dailyLoss` and NEVER call `recordTradeResult()`. I grep-confirmed there is not a single reference to `dailyLoss`, `recordTradeResult`, or `consecutiveLosses` anywhere inside runTacticalSmartScanInner (4015-4640) or runTacticalScanInner's exit branch. Consequence: enforceDailyLossHalt (called at 3722/4048/4743) reads `engine.dailyLoss` which stays ~0 for these modes, so the realized daily-loss auto-halt is effectively dead for tactical/tactical-smart's own exits; and a string of losing rotations/flattens never increments the consecutive-loss streak, so tripSafeguardHalt('consecutive_losses') never fires from those paths.

**Impact.** In tactical and tactical-smart modes the realized daily-loss cap and consecutive-loss circuit breaker do not engage on the engine's own strategy exits/rotations. A regime-driven mass flatten or a churn of losing swap-sells can realize an arbitrarily large intraday loss without ever halting on the documented universal safeguards — exactly the runaway-loss scenario these halts exist to stop. CLAUDE.md describes daily-loss and consecutive-loss halts as universal (only the losing-reentry cooldown is documented as tactical-off).

**Suggested fix.** After each successful sell in the tactical/tactical-smart exit and swap-sell branches, mirror the runScanInner pattern: `engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0;` and `if (recordTradeResult(engine, pnl, riskLimits.maxConsecutiveLosses)) tripSafeguardHalt(engine, 'consecutive_losses', {...});`. Factor a shared `recordRealizedExit(engine, pnl, riskLimits)` helper so every exit site is covered uniformly.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> All three cited exit sites behave exactly as described, and the impact is real and not mitigated by other gates. TACTICAL mass-exit (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3855-3870): the loop sells every position passing pos.unrealizedPnl into logTrade and calls nothing else — no `engine.dailyLoss +=`, no `recordTradeResult`. Worse, line 3952 calls `upsertDailyPnl(today, 0, totalUnrealizedPnl, 0, ...)` with realized hardcoded to 0, so even the DB realized counter never reflects the flatten's loss. TACTICAL-SMART mass-exit (4168-4181) and swap-sell (4454-4462): both only do `realizedPnlThisScan += pos.unrealizedPnl` / `tradesThisScan++` (and the swap-sell `heldSymbols.delete`). Neither touches `engine.dailyLoss` nor calls `recordTradeResult`. `realizedPnlThisScan` is written to the DB row at 4593 but is NOT fed into any halt — it is purely a display/persistence accumulator. The contrast site runScanInner (5187-5199) and runExitCheck (2869-2880) do all three: realizedPnlThisScan, `engine.dailyLoss += pnl<0?pnl:0`, and `recordTradeResult(...) → tripSafeguardHalt('consecutive_losses')`. Halt accounting: `enforceDailyLossHalt` (1366) gates solely on `engine.dailyLoss > -threshold`, which stays ~0 for these modes' own exits → realized daily-loss auto-halt is dead for tactical/tactical-smart strategy exits. `recordTradeResult` (1033-1041) is the ONLY incrementer of `engine.consecutiveLosses` (grep confirms the sole `+= 1` is inside it; all other writes are resets to 0), so a churn of losing rotations/flattens never advances the streak and `tripSafeguardHalt('consecutive_losses')` can never fire from these paths. Mitigation check (the obvious refutation): `enforceUnrealizedLossHalt` IS called at scan-end in all three modes (3951 tactical, 4592 t-smart, 5699 runScan). But it does NOT save these paths: it computes `mtmLoss = engine.dailyLoss + totalUnrealizedPnl` (1471), and `totalUnrealizedPnl` is re-fetched from the broker AFTER the exit loop ran (3941-3946 / 4583-4589). On a mass-flatten the positions are gone, so totalUnrealizedPnl ≈ 0 and dailyLoss ≈ 0 → no halt. The MTM gate only catches a *slow bleed of still-held* positions, not the realize-and-exit/rotation scenario the finding targets. So the loss falls into a blind spot between both halts. CLAUDE.md documents daily-loss and consecutive-loss halts as universal live safeguards ("Safeguards on every live engine (independent of risk profile)... Consecutive-loss halt at threshold"), with only the losing-reentry cooldown documented as "Off in tactical mode only." The finding's universality claim is correct. P1 is appropriate, not inflated: two of four user-facing modes silently disable two documented universal circuit breakers on the engine's own exits/rotations.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3859-3870 (tactical mass-exit: logTrade(pos.unrealizedPnl) only, no dailyLoss/recordTradeResult); 3952 (upsertDailyPnl hardcodes realized=0 for tactical); 4168-4181 (t-smart exit: realizedPnlThisScan+=, tradesThisScan++ only); 4454-4462 (t-smart swap-sell: same, no dailyLoss/recordTradeResult); vs 5187-5199 and 2869-2880 (correct pattern with engine.dailyLoss += and recordTradeResult→tripSafeguardHalt); enforceDailyLossHalt gate at 1366 (engine.dailyLoss-only); recordTradeResult 1033-1041 (sole consecutiveLosses incrementer); enforceUnrealizedLossHalt 1458-1472 (mtmLoss=engine.dailyLoss+totalUnrealizedPnl) called at 3951/4592/5699 with totalUnrealizedPnl fetched post-exit at 3941-3946/4583-4589 → blind to flattened realized loss.

### 3. ✅ FIXED · [P1] stopEngine() does not stop an in-flight scan — engine keeps placing orders after Stop

- **Lane:** engine-scan-concurrency · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6267-6305, 4937-5510, 3879-3937, 4199-4573`

**Description.** stopEngine() clears the intervals, calls placeSafetyStops, sets engine.running=false, and schedules eviction — but it does NOT set engine.halted=true and does NOT bump engine.scanGeneration. The scan loop bodies (runScanInner line 4937+, runTacticalScanInner entry loop 3879+, runTacticalSmartScanInner loops 4199+/4343+/4509+) only re-check two conditions per iteration: `if (engine.halted) break;` (line 4942, present only in runScan) and `throwIfScanCancelled(engine, myGeneration)` (which only throws when engine.scanGeneration changed). engine.running is checked solely at the scan-wrapper entry (lines 3671, 3993, 4684), never inside the per-symbol loop. So if a user presses Stop while a scan is mid-iteration (a full universe scan runs 30-90s, and the tactical loops run up to TACTICAL_SCAN_SYMBOL_BUDGET_MS), the in-flight scan continues fetching, analyzing, and placing BUY limit orders / SELL market orders for the remainder of its symbol list and the post-loop swap-sell redeploy block. The 11-min ENGINE_EVICTION_DELAY_MS (line 6310) is explicitly designed to wait for the orphan to finish — confirming the design knows the scan keeps running, but it keeps TRADING, not just finishing reads. Contrast haltEngine() (6826-6828) which sets halted=true and is therefore honored by the loop break; Stop is the weaker control.

**Impact.** A user (or the system, on a broker switch / risk-profile change / going-to-cash decision) presses Stop expecting trading to cease immediately, but the engine can place additional live/paper orders for up to ~90s afterward (longer for tactical with the wall-clock budget). Orders placed after an explicit Stop are unexpected positions the user believes they prevented — wrong live orders, capital deployed against the user's intent.

**Suggested fix.** In stopEngine(), set engine.halted-equivalent gating for the in-flight scan: either set a dedicated cancellation by `++engine.scanGeneration` (so throwIfScanCancelled fires at the next yield) AND/OR add an `if (!engine.running) break;` check at the top of every scan loop body and before the post-loop swap-sell redeploy. Bumping scanGeneration is the cheapest fix that reuses the existing cooperative-cancellation plumbing.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> I tried to refute this and could not — every element of the finding checks out against the code. stopEngine() (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6267-6305) clears the three timers and sets engine.running=false (6287) but never sets engine.halted=true and never bumps engine.scanGeneration. By contrast haltEngine() sets engine.halted=true (6827), which is why the halt control is honored and Stop is not. throwIfScanCancelled() only throws when engine.scanGeneration!==myGeneration (87-91); since Stop doesn't bump it, cooperative cancellation never fires on a Stop. The scan is launched fire-and-forget by the interval callback: setInterval(...) calls runScanGuarded('tick') → scanFn() (6220-6223, 6183) WITHOUT awaiting. clearInterval in stopEngine only prevents FUTURE ticks — a scan promise already past the wrapper's engine.running entry check (3671/3993/4642-4649) keeps running to completion. engine.running is never re-checked inside any per-symbol loop body (the only in-loop engine.running checks at 6922/6956 are in pushExternalSignal/broadcastExternalSignal, iterating engines not symbols). Inside the loops, orders keep getting placed after Stop: runScanInner per-symbol loop (4937) only checks engine.halted (4942) and throwIfScanCancelled (4945); the post-loop swap-sell redeploy (5575) places BUY orders (5618) with NO cancellation check at all; runTacticalScanInner entry loop (3879) places BUY limits (3923) gated only by throwIfScanCancelled (3884); runTacticalSmartScanInner swap loop (4407) places SELL+BUY (4456/4488) with NO cancellation check, and the add loop (4509) places BUY (4558) with NO cancellation check. So after Stop the in-flight scan can place orders for the remainder of its symbol list (full-universe ~30-90s; tactical bounded by TACTICAL_SCAN_SYMBOL_BUDGET_MS). The 11-min ENGINE_EVICTION_DELAY_MS (6310) and its comment explicitly acknowledge an in-flight scan continues running after Stop — confirming the design expects the orphan to finish, but it keeps TRADING, not just finishing reads. This directly contradicts user/system intent on an irreversible (order-placing) operation and applies to live engines (gated by ALLOW_LIVE_TRADING). Orders are still bounded by canPlaceBuyOrder gates and protective/safety stops still fire, and exploitation requires the mid-scan timing window — so it's a clear P1 safety/correctness gap, not P0. The proposed fix (++engine.scanGeneration in stopEngine) is the cheapest correct fix, though note it would still NOT stop the tactical-smart swap/add loops, which lack throwIfScanCancelled entirely and would also need an explicit check.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6287 (stopEngine sets running=false, no halted/scanGeneration); 6826-6828 (haltEngine sets halted=true — the contrast); 87-91 (throwIfScanCancelled only fires on scanGeneration change); 6220-6223 + 6183 (interval fires scanFn() un-awaited; clearInterval stops only future ticks); 3671/3993/4642-4649 (engine.running checked only at scan-wrapper entry); 4937-4945 (runScan loop checks only halted + throwIfScanCancelled); 4407-4504 (tactical-smart swap loop: places SELL 4456 + BUY 4488, NO cancellation check); 4509-4573 (tactical-smart add loop: places BUY 4558, NO cancellation check); 5575-5625 (post-loop swap-sell redeploy: places BUY 5618, NO cancellation check); 3879-3933 (tactical entry loop: BUY 3923, only throwIfScanCancelled); 6307-6310 (ENGINE_EVICTION_DELAY_MS comment acknowledges in-flight scan keeps running after Stop)

### 4. ✅ FIXED · [P1] Stop-sync contract test pins the OLD (removed) halt gate — hides a real safety regression

- **Lane:** engine-snapshot-watchdog · **Category:** correctness · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:31-47, 82-86, 120, 124-131`

**Description.** checkSyncEligibility() in the test claims to be a 'contract test' that mirrors syncBrokerStopsForUser ('If the engine's checks change, update both'). But the real syncBrokerStopsForUser in src/lib/trading-engine.ts lines 7404-7446 deliberately REMOVED the halt gate — the comment at 7410-7416 states 'Halt does NOT gate this path' so a halted engine keeps refreshing its broker stops (peak ratchets / breakeven promotions reach Alpaca even after the engine halts). The test still has `if (engine.halted) return { ran: false, reason: "engine_halted" }` (line 38) and ASSERTS that a halted engine is skipped (lines 82-86, 120). The test passes (green) yet pins behavior that is the exact opposite of production, so it provides zero protection: if someone re-introduced a halt gate (the dangerous regression that freezes stops on a halted position — the scenario the code's comment says 'is the exact scenario where keeping stops fresh matters most'), this 'contract test' would happily still pass.

**Impact.** A safety-critical behavior (broker stops must keep refreshing while halted) is guarded by a test that asserts the wrong contract. A future edit that freezes stops on halt would not be caught, leaving live positions with stale broker-side stops during exactly the high-stress halt window — the original 2026-05-26 incident class.

**Suggested fix.** Update checkSyncEligibility() to match production: remove the halted gate (or make it a no-op), and delete/invert the 'skips with engine_halted' and 'evaluates each engine independently' halted assertions. Better: import and unit-test the real syncBrokerStopsForUser against a stubbed globalThis rather than re-implementing the gating in the test (re-implementation guarantees drift).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> The finding is accurate on every checkable point. The test's checkSyncEligibility (tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:31-47) is explicitly labeled a contract mirror of syncBrokerStopsForUser ("If the engine's checks change, update both. This is the contract test." lines 29-30), yet it diverges from production in a safety-relevant way. Production syncBrokerStopsForUser (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7404-7446) deliberately has NO halt gate: control flows from `if (!engine.running)` at 7409 straight to the positionMap check at 7417, and the comment block at 7410-7416 states "Halt does NOT gate this path ... it remains active while halted. Without this, the 5-min standalone scheduler stops refreshing broker stops for halted engines — peak-price ratchets and breakeven promotions ... never reach Alpaca, leaving positions protected by a stop that's stale relative to current peak." The test, by contrast, has `if (engine.halted) return { ran: false, reason: "engine_halted" }` at line 38 and ASSERTS a halted engine is skipped at lines 82-86 and 120 — the exact opposite of production. Git history proves drift, not coincidence: commit 7871a55 (#4) introduced both files together; commit ab83391 "fix(engine): keep protective paths alive while halted" later removed the halt gate from trading-engine.ts (20 lines changed) while producing an empty diff for the test file. The suite is green (verified: 10/10 pass), so it pins the removed, dangerous behavior as the desired contract. A future re-introduction of the halt gate — the precise 2026-05-26 incident class where 6 positions drifted with 9-hour-stale broker stops — would still pass this test, which is worse than no test because it documents the bug as correct. The scheduler (stop-sync-scheduler.ts:56-88) only delegates to syncBrokerStopsForUser with no halt gate of its own, so there is no mitigating layer. A second independent divergence reinforces the finding: the test returns scan_in_flight for ANY scanStartedAt (lines 43-45) and asserts a 6-hour-old scan still reports scan_in_flight (lines 133-146), whereas production runs the sync once scanAgeMs >= the 10-min STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS (lines 7432-7443) — so the test also fails to guard the stale-scan recovery path. Impact is real: a designated safety-regression guard for a documented financial-loss incident asserts the inverse contract. Not P0 because production code is currently correct (no live position is mis-protected today); clearly above P2 because this defeats the stated purpose of the only guard against a known loss-causing regression. P1 is correct.

**Evidence:** tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:29-30 (contract-mirror claim), :38 (halt gate present), :43-45 (no stale-scan override), :82-86 + :120 (assert halted engine skipped), :133-146 (asserts 6h scan still scan_in_flight); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7409 (running check) → :7410-7416 ("Halt does NOT gate this path" comment) → :7417 (positionMap check, no halt gate between), :7421-7443 (10-min STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS fall-through); src/lib/stop-sync-scheduler.ts:56-88 (delegates with no own halt gate); git: 7871a55 introduced test+func together, ab83391 ("keep protective paths alive while halted") changed trading-engine.ts (20 lines) with empty diff for the test; `npx vitest run` → 10 passed.

### 5. ✅ FIXED · [P1] Take-profit graduation locks stop ABOVE current price for optimized mode (TP < +30%), force-exiting winners as "stop loss hit"

- **Lane:** engine-swap-graduation · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5074-5114, 2211-2216, 5366-5373, 2672-2677`

**Description.** When graduation is enabled (optimized + tactical-smart) and currentPrice >= heldPosition.takeProfit, promoteToGraduationFloor() unconditionally sets pos.stopLoss = entryPrice * 1.30 (GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT=0.30) with no guard that the floor stays below currentPrice (line 2211-2215). For OPTIMIZED mode the take-profit is frequently far below +30%: the ATR-based TP at line 5371 is `currentPrice + atrVal*tpAtrMult` (typical ATR ~1-3% of price * mult ~3-8 = roughly +3% to +24%), and per-symbol GA-tuned `row.takeProfitPct` (line 2674) can be any value the optimizer found. When TP triggers at, say, entry*1.10, the floor is set to entry*1.30, which is ABOVE the current price (entry*1.10). shouldGraduateExit() typically returns null in the very momentum case graduation is meant to ride (hot RSI, rising volume = no weakness), so control falls to the regular exit chain at line 5102, where line 5104 `currentPrice <= heldPosition.stopLoss` is now `1.10*entry <= 1.30*entry` = TRUE, immediately force-selling and labeling it 'Stop loss hit'. The graduation feature (changelog PR-14: 'GA-tuned takeProfit becomes a graduation point so live runners aren't clipped') is completely defeated for optimized whenever TP < +30%, which is the common case for ATR-based TP.

**Impact.** Optimized-mode runners are force-closed the instant they cross take-profit instead of graduating to ride higher, and the exit is mislabeled 'Stop loss hit' in trade logs/audit, corrupting the post-trade record. The documented optimized graduation behavior never actually engages for the majority of symbols. (The default optimized preset takeProfitPct 0.369 happens to clear +30%, masking the bug in the no-per-symbol-params path, but ATR-based and most GA-tuned per-symbol params do not.)

**Suggested fix.** In promoteToGraduationFloor, clamp the lock so it never exceeds the price that triggered graduation: e.g. accept currentPrice and set `pos.stopLoss = Math.max(pos.stopLoss, Math.min(entryPrice*(1+GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT), currentPrice*(1 - SMALL_BUFFER)))`. Alternatively only apply the +30% floor when currentPrice > entry*1.30, otherwise lock to a fraction just below currentPrice. Either way the graduation stop must sit below the current price so the position actually continues to be held.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> Every link in the claimed chain is present and behaves as described, verified against the actual code. (1) GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT=0.30 (line 1956); promoteToGraduationFloor (2211-2216) unconditionally sets pos.stopLoss = entryPrice*1.30 — it takes no currentPrice arg and has no clamp, only refusing to LOWER an existing higher stop. The call site (5077) passes no price. (2) Optimized has graduation enabled (MODE_GRADUATION_DEFAULT.optimized = "enabled", line 2142) and routes through runScan→runScanInner (dispatch at 6126; everything except tactical/tactical-smart goes to runScan), which contains the graduation block at 5074-5095. (3) At buy, entryPrice===currentPrice (5455) and takeProfit===takeProfitPrice (5458). For optimized the ATR path fires (getActiveMode==="optimized" → tpAtrMult at 5369; takeProfit = currentPrice + atr_14*tpAtrMult at 5371-5372). takeProfitAtrMult ranges [3,15] (optimizer.ts:97); with typical ATR_14 ~1.5-2.5% of price this yields TP ≈ +6% to +24%, well below the +30% floor. New optimizer runs always use the ATR mult (2625-2626), so this is the live path, not a corner case. (4) When currentPrice>=takeProfit triggers graduation, the trigger price ≈ entry*(1+tpFraction) < entry*1.30, so promoteToGraduationFloor sets stopLoss = entry*1.30 > currentPrice. (5) shouldGraduateExit (2161-2202) returns null in the clean-momentum case it exists to ride: volumeDeclining=false (rising vol), plateaued=false (distFromPeak<0 on new highs), rsiRollover=false (hot RSI) → weakCount<min → null. So shouldExit stays false. (6) Control reaches line 5104: currentPrice <= heldPosition.stopLoss → entry*(1+tpFraction) <= entry*1.30 → TRUE → force-sell mislabeled "Stop loss hit" (5106). The optimized PRESET takeProfitPct 0.369 (>30%, strategy-presets.ts:34) and the tactical-smart/tactical buy sites (takeProfit: price*1.5, lines 3930/4310/4495/4565) clear the floor and mask the bug there — exactly as the finding notes. The unit tests (take-profit-graduation.test.ts) only ever model +50%/+60% triggers (lines 88-100) and never a sub-30% trigger, confirming this case was unconsidered. Severity P1 is correct: a shipped feature (PR-14 graduation) is silently defeated for the majority of optimized symbols and trade exits are mislabeled in audit/journal/Tax Center, but the degraded behavior is "exit at take-profit" (a profitable exit), not a loss or data-loss event, so it is not P0.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:1956 (GRADUATION_FLOOR_PCT=0.30); :2211-2216 (promoteToGraduationFloor sets stopLoss=entryPrice*1.30, no clamp/no currentPrice); :2142 (optimized graduation "enabled"); :6126 (optimized → runScan); :5074-5095 (graduation block, calls promoteToGraduationFloor + shouldGraduateExit); :5369-5373 (optimized ATR-based TP = currentPrice + atr_14*tpAtrMult); :5455,:5458 (entryPrice=currentPrice, takeProfit=takeProfitPrice at buy); :5102-5106 ("Stop loss hit" via currentPrice<=stopLoss when graduation returns null); :2161-2191 (shouldGraduateExit returns null on no-weakness momentum); src/lib/optimizer.ts:97 (takeProfitAtrMult range 3-15); src/lib/strategy-presets.ts:34 (optimized preset takeProfitPct 0.369 masks bug on preset path); tests/unit/take-profit-graduation.test.ts:88-100 (tests only model +50%/+60% triggers, never sub-30%)

### 6. ✅ FIXED · [P1] EMA crossover detection compares fast/slow EMAs from different bars when history arrays differ in length

- **Lane:** indicators · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:68-100, 154-175`

**Description.** detectCrossover/detectCrossunder take minLen = min(fastHistory.length, slowHistory.length) and index BOTH arrays by the same i counted from the front (start = max(0, minLen-lookback-1); compare fastHistory[i] vs slowHistory[i]). The two EMA history arrays (emaFast window 9, emaSlow window 21) begin recording at different bars (fast first pushes at bar 9, slow at bar 21) and are capped at maxHistory=50 (ema.ts:36-41). When the arrays have different lengths — which happens for any analyzeBars call with roughly 22-57 daily bars, or 5m windows, and for ALL optimizer/backtester windows of 60-89 bars — index i from the front points at different absolute bars in each array. Worked example at a 60-bar optimizer window: emaFast.history() = 50 entries (bars 11..60), emaSlow.history() = 40 entries (bars 21..60), minLen=40; at i=38 it compares emaFast at bar 49 against emaSlow at bar 59 (10-bar skew). The loop only iterates up to minLen-1, so it NEVER inspects the fast array's most recent values (indices minLen..fastLen-1) — it tests stale fast EMAs against current slow EMAs. This fabricates and misses 'Fresh bullish/bearish EMA crossover' which each add a point to bullScore/bearScore (analyzer.ts:168-175), tipping BUY/STRONG_BUY thresholds (bullScore>=4 && bullScore>bearScore+2, lines 237-254).

**Impact.** Corrupted crossover signals on every short-window analysis: the GA optimizer (analyzeSignalOnly on 60-89 bar windows, optimizer.ts:571) selects strategy params against a buggy signal function, then those params drive LIVE trading; the daily backtester (windows 30-57, backtester.ts:341,383) and multi-timeframe (5m bars >=20, multi-timeframe/route.ts:31) emit wrong BUY/SELL decisions. Wrong-direction or phantom crossovers can flip a HOLD into a BUY or vice-versa.

**Suggested fix.** Align the arrays by the END before comparing: slice each history to its last K = min(len) entries (const f = fast.slice(fast.length-minLen); const s = slow.slice(slow.length-minLen)) and index f[i]/s[i], or compute per-array offsets so equal i maps to the same bar. The engine's 90-bar daily path happens to be unaffected only because both arrays are coincidentally capped to 50 (bars 41..90); fix it regardless since shorter-window callers are corrupted.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> The bug is real and reaches live trading. detectCrossover/detectCrossunder (analyzer.ts:73-99) front-index BOTH EMA history arrays by the same i in [max(0,minLen-lookback-1), minLen-1). The two arrays do NOT start at the same bar: EMA.update pushes to recentValues only after emaValue becomes non-null at count===window (ema.ts:27-41), so emaFast(9) first records at bar 9 and emaSlow(21) at bar 21, and both cap at maxHistory=50 (ema.ts:38-39). For an N-bar window, emaFast holds min(N-8,50) entries and emaSlow holds min(N-20,50); they are unequal length whenever N<70, so equal i points at different absolute bars (a skew of up to 10 bars near N=60), and indices minLen..fastLen-1 (the freshest fast EMAs) are never inspected. This tests stale fast EMAs against current slow EMAs, fabricating and missing 'Fresh ... crossover' which each add a point to bull/bearScore (analyzer.ts:168-175) feeding the BUY/STRONG_BUY thresholds at lines 237-254. Confirmed call sites: optimizer rolling window grows 60..90 and calls analyzeSignalOnly at length>=60 (optimizer.ts:561,571) so windows 60-69 are skewed and the resulting GA params drive live trading; the daily backtester calls analyzeBars on windows >=30 during ramp-up (backtester.ts:341-343,383-385); multi-timeframe daily path uses fetchBars(symbol,90,'1d'). Crucially the finding UNDERSTATES the impact: fetchBars 'days' is CALENDAR days (market-data.ts:97 period1=period2-days*86400), so the live engine's and screener's fetchBars(symbol,90,'1d') (trading-engine.ts:4213,4357; screener.ts:228) return ~60-62 trading bars, giving emaFast=50 vs emaSlow~42 — i.e. the live signal pipeline is ALSO skewed, not coincidentally safe as the finding assumed. analyzeSignalOnly (analyzer.ts:386-413) and analyzeBars share evaluateSignal, so all paths inherit the defect. Magnitude per call is data-dependent (a fabricated cross requires the misaligned EMAs to actually intersect), so not every call flips, but the comparison is genuinely wrong and runs on every short-window analysis including the live signal feed. P1 (trading-correctness, reaches live params) is appropriate; the proposed end-alignment fix (slice last minLen of each) is correct.

**Evidence:** src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:73-78 (minLen=min(fast,slow); loop i from max(0,minLen-lookback-1) to minLen-1; compares fastHistory[i] vs slowHistory[i]); analyzer.ts:90-95 (crossunder, same pattern); ema.ts:27-41 (push only when emaValue!=null, first at count===window; cap at maxHistory=50 via shift); analyzer.ts:155-175 (history() arrays fed to detectors; each cross adds to bull/bearScore); analyzer.ts:237-254 (bullScore>=4 && bullScore>bearScore+2 BUY/STRONG_BUY thresholds); config.ts:50 (crossoverLookback=3); optimizer.ts:123 (WINDOW_SIZE=90), 440 (w.shift at >WINDOW_SIZE), 561 (w.length<60 skip), 571 (analyzeSignalOnly(symbol,w)); backtester.ts:140 (windowSize default 100), 341-343 and 383-385 (analyzeBars on windowBars where length>=30); multi-timeframe/route.ts:28,31-32 (fetchBars 90 1d, analyzeBars); market-data.ts:97 (period1=period2-days*86400 — days are calendar days); trading-engine.ts:4213,4357 (fetchBars(symbol,90,'1d')→analyzeHybrid); screener.ts:228,234 (fetchBars(symbol,90,'1d')→analyzeHybrid); hybrid/pipeline.ts:47 (analyzeHybrid→analyzeBars).

### 7. ✅ FIXED · [P1] Form 8949 CSV export silently drops every manual_close disposal, omitting realized gains and leaving lots perpetually open

- **Lane:** tax · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts:75-87`

**Description.** The export route pulls all FILLED rows with a non-null fillPrice (SQL filter, lines 66-72) then maps them to TaxTradeEvent with `rows.filter((r) => r.fillPrice !== null && (r.action === "BUY" || r.action === "SELL"))` (line 76). But the engine writes disposals via the Trader UI Flatten command as `action: "manual_close"` with `status:"FILLED"` and `fillPrice` set (src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts line 161-173), confirmed as a real action value across the codebase (trading-engine.ts uses `inArray(traderTrades.action, ["SELL", "manual_close"])`). manual_close rows pass the SQL `isNotNull(fillPrice)` filter but are excluded by the strict `action === "SELL"` JS check, so the disposition never reaches computeFifoClosedLots. The matching BUY lot is therefore never consumed: the position appears perpetually open and its realized gain/loss is completely absent from the Form 8949 CSV and the summary.

**Impact.** User downloads a Form 8949 / tax summary that understates realized proceeds and gains by the full amount of every manually-flattened position. Filing from this report under-reports income to the IRS (or hides realized losses the user could claim). Unlike the documented wash-sale over-conservatism, this is a hard omission, not a conservative over-report.

**Suggested fix.** Normalize the action before the FIFO pass, mirroring /api/tax/report line 86: treat any non-BUY FILLED row with a positive fillPrice as a SELL. e.g. `const action = r.action.toUpperCase() === "BUY" ? "BUY" : "SELL"` and drop the `action === "SELL"` exclusion. Also widen the SQL `action` predicate to include 'manual_close' (and any lowercase variants) rather than relying on exact-case match.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> The finding is real and the mechanism is exactly as described. (1) The buggy filter is present: src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts line 76 maps rows with `rows.filter((r) => r.fillPrice !== null && (r.action === "BUY" || r.action === "SELL"))`, which silently excludes any `manual_close` row even though the SQL query (lines 66-72) only filters on userId + status="FILLED" + isNotNull(fillPrice). (2) The engine genuinely writes such rows: src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts lines 161-173 insert `action: "manual_close"`, `status: "FILLED"`, `fillPrice: pos.currentPrice` (non-null) for every position closed via the Trader UI Flatten command. These pass the SQL filter but are dropped by line 76. (3) The impact propagates as claimed: src/lib/tax-report.ts only consumes a BUY OpenLot when it encounters a SELL event, and its own comment (line 100) states "Open lots (BUYs not fully consumed by SELLs) are dropped." So the matching BUY lot is never consumed, produces no ClosedLot, and its realized gain/loss is absent from both the CSV and the summary. (4) The contrast confirming this is a bug, not intended behavior: the companion Tax Center route src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts line 86 explicitly normalizes `const action = t.action.toUpperCase() === "BUY" ? "BUY" : "SELL"`, and CLAUDE.md documents that BUY/SELL/manual_close should normalize to BUY/SELL. The export route is the outlier. Severity is corrected from P0 to P1: this is a tax-reporting accuracy bug affecting only manually-flattened positions (engine scan-driven exits write action="SELL" and are unaffected), the output carries an explicit "NOT a tax professional, verify with your CPA before filing" disclaimer (line 109), and it moves no money / leaks no tenant data. It is a genuine correctness/compliance defect deserving a fix, but the P0 "understates IRS income" framing overstates blast radius given the disclaimer and the manual-only trigger.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts:66-72 (SQL filter: userId + status=FILLED + isNotNull(fillPrice), no action predicate); src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts:76 (JS filter `(r.action === "BUY" || r.action === "SELL")` drops manual_close); src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:161-173 (inserts action:"manual_close", status:"FILLED", fillPrice:pos.currentPrice); src/lib/tax-report.ts:98-100 (FIFO consumes BUYs only via SELL events; unconsumed BUY lots dropped); src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:85-86 (companion route correctly normalizes manual_close→SELL); CLAUDE.md:84,229 (documented intent: BUY/SELL/manual_close normalize to BUY/SELL)

### 8. ✅ FIXED · [P1] /api/tax/report filters trades to the tax year BEFORE FIFO matching, dropping realized gains whose buy lot is in a prior year

- **Lane:** tax · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:44-95,116`

**Description.** Both the portfolio query (gte/lte executedAt within yearStart..yearEnd, lines 51-54) and the engine loop (`if (executedAt < yearStart || executedAt > yearEnd) continue;`, line 83) restrict the trade set to the requested tax year, then feed only those in-year trades to calculateTaxSummary → generateForm8949 (line 116), which performs FIFO BUY→SELL matching. A position bought in (say) Dec 2024 and sold in 2025 has its BUY excluded from the 2025 trade set; in generateForm8949 the 2025 SELL hits an empty `symbolLots` queue (tax-engine.ts line 278: `while (remaining > 0 && symbolLots.length > 0)`), emits zero Form8949 lines, and the entire realized gain/loss silently disappears. The opposite error also occurs: an early-year sale can FIFO-match a later same-year buy incorrectly because the true earlier lot was filtered out. The correct pattern — pull full history, FIFO-match, then filter closed lots by sale date — is implemented in /api/export/tax-report (computeFifoClosedLots over all rows, then filterByTaxYear) but NOT here.

**Impact.** The unified Tax Center realized-gains view (the primary user-facing tax number) omits the gain/loss of any position whose acquisition crossed a calendar-year boundary, and mis-computes cost basis when same-symbol lots span years. Users under- or over-report capital gains based on this figure.

**Suggested fix.** Fetch all FILLED engine trades + all portfolio trades across full history (no executedAt year predicate), run generateForm8949 over the complete set, then filter the resulting Form8949Line[] by `dateSold` falling in the tax year (matching the export route's filterByTaxYear approach). Keep the per-year predicate only as an index hint, never as the FIFO input boundary.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P1.

> Year filter is applied before FIFO matching, so a SELL whose BUY lot is in a prior year hits an empty lot queue and its realized gain is silently dropped from the user-facing Tax Center summary with no error logged. Both the portfolio query and the engine loop restrict to the year, then feed only in-year trades to generateForm8949. The export route implements the correct pattern (full history, FIFO, then filter by sale date). The future-buy sub-claim is imprecise since FIFO is time-sorted, but the cross-year drop is real. P1 not P0 because the filed Form 8949 CSV uses the correct export route.</parameter> </invoke> 

**Evidence:** route.ts lines 49-54 portfolio year filter, line 83 engine-loop year continue, line 116 calculateTaxSummary; tax-engine.ts line 274 empty symbolLots default, line 278 while-loop skips empty queue, line 238 time-sort; export tax-report route.ts lines 50-88 full-history FIFO then filter; tax-report.ts lines 211-218 filterByTaxYear on dateSold; dashboard tax-center page.tsx line 71 consumes broken summary

### 9. ⬜ PENDING · [P2] Intrabar look-ahead in trailing stop: same-bar high promotes the trail that the same-bar low is then checked against

- **Lane:** backtester · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/backtester.ts:252-327`

**Description.** On each in-position bar the code first raises peakPrice to bar.high (line 253-255), computes the trailing stop off that just-raised peak (dynTrailPct from profitPct of the new peak, line 263-266; trailingStop = peakPrice*(1-dynTrailPct), line 282), then checks `bar.low <= effectiveStop` (line 324) to fire a trailing-stop exit on the SAME bar. This assumes the intrabar high occurred before the low. For a bar that gapped down (low first) then rallied to a new high, the model fires a trailing-stop exit at a level that only existed AFTER the low — an exit that could not have happened in real life. The optimizer's portfolioBacktest has the identical pattern (optimizer.ts 451 then 492).

**Impact.** Backtest trailing-stop exits are optimistically (and sometimes pessimistically) mispriced on volatile bars, inflating apparent trailing-stop effectiveness and biasing reported returns/Sharpe used to pick live engine params. Systematic because it fires on every bar where high and low straddle the trail.

**Suggested fix.** Resolve stop hits against the trail computed from the PRIOR bar's peak (peak as of bar i-1), and update peakPrice to bar.high only after the stop check; or model worst-case intrabar ordering (check low against the pre-update trail). Keep both backtesters consistent.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code behaves exactly as described in both backtesters. In src/lib/backtester.ts the in-position block first raises the peak (line 253-255: `if (bar.high > position.peakPrice) position.peakPrice = bar.high`), then derives the dynamic trail from that just-raised peak (profitPct line 263 → dynTrailPct line 264-266 → trailingStop = peakPrice*(1-dynTrailPct) line 282 → effectiveStop = max(stopLoss, trailingStop) line 283), then fires the exit by checking the SAME bar's low against it (line 324: `if (!exitPrice && bar.low <= effectiveStop)`, exit reason "trailing_stop" line 326, fill at effectiveStop line 325). Because raising the peak raises the trail level, the same-bar low is checked against a trail that only existed AFTER the intrabar high — a textbook intrabar look-ahead that assumes high-before-low ordering. optimizer.ts replicates it identically: peak raise at line 451, trailStop off new peak at line 458-460, and `bar.low <= Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)` at line 492 with fill at that max (line 493). The impact path into live params is real: portfolioBacktest IS the GA fitness function (optimizer.ts:1006-1007 `riskAdjust(portfolioBacktest(...,"train",...))`), and the GA tunes the per-symbol params used by `optimized` mode in live trading, so the bias systematically distorts the fitness landscape used to pick live params. Backtests run on daily bars (CLAUDE.md confirms daily-only; gap-down-then-rally within a daily bar is common), so the trigger is frequent, not edge-case. Direction is mainly optimistic (a higher peak yields a higher trail, so the spurious exit books a better-than-reality price, inflating trailing-stop effectiveness), with occasional pessimistic early exits — matching the finding's nuance. P2 is appropriate: real and systematic, but confined to offline research/optimizer tooling (no live money path, no crash, no data leak); the live engine's own scan/exit logic is not implicated. The proposed fix (resolve stops against the prior-bar peak or model worst-case intrabar ordering, applied to both backtesters) is the correct remediation.

**Evidence:** src/lib/backtester.ts:253-255 (peak raised to bar.high), :263-266 (dynTrailPct off new peak), :282 (trailingStop = peakPrice*(1-dynTrailPct)), :283 (effectiveStop = max), :324-326 (bar.low <= effectiveStop fires trailing_stop, fills at effectiveStop); src/lib/optimizer.ts:451 (peak raise), :458-460 (trailStop off new peak), :492-493 (bar.low <= max(stopLoss,trailStop) fill); src/lib/optimizer.ts:1006-1007 (portfolioBacktest is GA fitness, tunes live optimized-mode params)

### 10. ⬜ PENDING · [P2] mode-compare reads optimizer params via takeProfitPct, which GA runs never write — Optimized config silently lost

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** correctness · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/app/api/backtest/mode-compare/route.ts:109-126`

**Description.** The fallback that loads the latest completed optimizer run requires `typeof p.takeProfitPct === 'number'` (line 113) before returning a config. But the GA optimizer's OptimizableParams uses `takeProfitAtrMult` (optimizer.ts line 61), and bestParams written at optimizer.ts line 1120 is exactly that object — there is no takeProfitPct key on any new run. So for every modern run the guard fails and the function returns null, dropping the Optimized strategy's tuned config in the mode-compare page (it then uses some default/no params).

**Impact.** The mode-comparison page does not actually compare the user's optimized params for any run produced after the ATR-multiplier migration; it silently substitutes defaults, giving users a misleading Optimized-vs-others comparison. Not money loss, but wrong analytics driving strategy choice.

**Suggested fix.** Accept takeProfitAtrMult as the modern key (mirror trading-engine.ts _loadOptimizedParams which already falls back: `takeProfitPct: p.takeProfitPct ?? 5.0`), or branch on whichever key is present, instead of hard-requiring takeProfitPct.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate on both premise and impact. (1) The GA optimizer's OptimizableParams (src/lib/optimizer.ts:59-69) defines takeProfitAtrMult and has no takeProfitPct; a grep for "takeProfitPct" across optimizer.ts returns zero matches, so the optimizer never writes that key. (2) The completed run persists bestEver.params verbatim at optimizer.ts:1120 (set bestParams: bestEver.params), and bestParams is a raw jsonb column (src/lib/db/schema/optimization.ts:65) with no transform on read — so a modern run's bestParams object has takeProfitAtrMult and lacks takeProfitPct. (3) The mode-compare fallback at route.ts:111-115 hard-requires typeof p.takeProfitPct === "number"; for every modern run this is false, so the block is skipped and the function returns null (route.ts:131). (4) At the call site (route.ts:237) a null optimizedParams makes config = {} for the optimized mode — which is precisely the pre-PR-22 regression the PR-22 comments (route.ts:56-58 and 211-214) claim to have fixed: the "Optimized (GA)" row silently runs with backtester defaults, misrepresenting the live engine. So the analytics are wrong, matching the claimed impact. The engine's own loader (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2625-2631) proves the mode-compare loader is the one out of sync: it explicitly comments "New runs have takeProfitAtrMult; old runs have takeProfitPct" and falls back with takeProfitPct: p.takeProfitPct ?? 5.0. The companion unit test (tests/unit/mode-compare-params.test.ts:95-128) only passes because it feeds fabricated bestParams objects that contain takeProfitPct, never the real takeProfitAtrMult shape, so it masks the production mismatch rather than catching it. One scope-limiting nuance (not a refutation): the bug only triggers on the global-run fallback path; the per-symbol path (route.ts:64-87) reads symbol_strategies.take_profit_pct, a real column (src/lib/db/schema/strategies.ts:34), and works. So users with a per-symbol GA row for the compared symbol are unaffected, but anyone comparing a symbol they haven't individually tuned (the common case) gets the defaults-substituted Optimized row. Impact is wrong analytics, not money loss; P2 is correct.

**Evidence:** src/lib/optimizer.ts:59-69 (OptimizableParams has takeProfitAtrMult, no takeProfitPct); optimizer.ts:1120 (bestParams: bestEver.params written verbatim); grep takeProfitPct in optimizer.ts = no matches; src/lib/db/schema/optimization.ts:65 (best_params jsonb, no transform); src/app/api/backtest/mode-compare/route.ts:111-115 + 131 (hard-requires takeProfitPct, returns null); route.ts:237 (null → config = {} for optimized mode); route.ts:56-58,211-214 (PR-22 comments describing the exact regression this re-introduces); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2625-2631 (correct dual-key handling: takeProfitAtrMult = p.takeProfitAtrMult ?? null; takeProfitPct: p.takeProfitPct ?? 5.0); src/lib/db/schema/strategies.ts:34 (symbol_strategies.take_profit_pct real, so path 1 unaffected); tests/unit/mode-compare-params.test.ts:95-128 (test uses fabricated takeProfitPct-bearing bestParams, masking the real takeProfitAtrMult mismatch)

### 11. ⬜ PENDING · [P2] Trailing-stop exit uses same-bar peak then checks same-bar low (intrabar look-ahead bias)

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/optimizer.ts:446-494`

**Description.** In the per-bar exit loop, line 451 first ratchets pos.peakPrice up to the CURRENT bar's high (`if (bar.high > pos.peakPrice) pos.peakPrice = bar.high`). Line 460 then derives the trailing stop from that just-updated peak (`trailStop = pos.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrail)`), and line 492 checks the trail/stop against the CURRENT bar's low (`bar.low <= Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)`). This assumes the bar's high always occurs before its low, so a position is credited with riding up to the intrabar high and only then stopping out. When the low actually preceded the high (dip-then-rally bars), the backtest both (a) gives the trail a higher anchor than was achievable and (b) fills the exit at a level the real path would not have produced. The bias is systematically favorable to tight trailing stops, which is exactly the parameter the GA tunes (trailingStopPct, PARAM_RANGES line 98).

**Impact.** GA fitness over-rewards tight trailing stops that capture phantom intrabar highs. Persisted bestParams.trailingStopPct (read by the live engine via _loadOptimizedParams, trading-engine.ts 2632) is tuned on inflated backtest returns and will underperform / stop out earlier than modeled on real live bars.

**Suggested fix.** Decide intrabar ordering conservatively: evaluate the stop/trail BEFORE updating peakPrice with the current bar's high (use the prior-bar peak for this bar's trail check), or model worst-case ordering (assume low before high) for exit detection. At minimum, do not let the same bar's high raise the peak that the same bar's low is tested against.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. The bars are DAILY ("1d") — fetched via provider.fetchBars(symbol, ..., "1d") at optimizer.ts:229 and :257 — and portfolioBacktest iterates one bar per calendar date (loop over data.dates, di from startIdx, bar = barLookup.get(symbol).get(date), :430-449). Within the per-position exit loop: :451 ratchets pos.peakPrice up to the CURRENT daily bar's high; :458-460 derive trailStop from that just-raised peak (dynTrail tightens as profitPct rises, so a higher peak both raises the anchor AND tightens the %); :492 then tests bar.low <= max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop) against the SAME bar's low. This is the textbook intrabar look-ahead artifact: it implicitly assumes the day's high preceded the day's low, crediting the position with riding to the intraday high before stopping out — a path that is not guaranteed on dip-then-rally bars and is systematically favorable to tight trailing stops. trailingStopPct is exactly what the GA tunes (PARAM_RANGES :98, range 0.01–0.15), so fitness over-rewards tight trails. The persisted bestParams.trailingStopPct IS read by the live engine via _loadOptimizedParams (trading-engine.ts:2632). Crucially, the live engine does NOT reproduce this artifact: it updates peakPrice from a single observed currentPrice per poll tick (trading-engine.ts:2767 `if (currentPrice > pos.peakPrice) pos.peakPrice = currentPrice`) and tests `currentPrice <= effectiveStop` against that same single price (:2820-2823) — there is no within-observation high-then-low, so the peak can only rise to actually-observed prices and the stop fills at the observed price. Hence the backtest model genuinely overstates achievable live performance in the direction the finding claims. The finding is correctly scoped: entry, sizing, and equity all use bar.close (:573, :602, :597, :624), so the bias is confined to the stop/trail exit path. It does not overclaim. Severity correction: this is an offline backtest-fidelity / parameter-tuning bias, not a runtime correctness, money-loss, crash, or security bug. It is real and systematic and worth fixing, but it manifests only as gradual live-vs-modeled underperformance and is partly inherent to daily-bar backtesting (the live 1-min poll has the same coarse-observation limitation in reverse). P2 is appropriate; P1 is slightly inflated for a research-only fidelity defect.

**Evidence:** src/lib/optimizer.ts:451 (peakPrice ratchets to current bar.high), :458-460 (dynTrail + trailStop derived from just-updated peak), :492-493 (bar.low <= max(stopLoss, trailStop) tested same bar); :229 and :257 (fetchBars ... "1d" — daily bars); :430,448 (per-date loop, one daily bar per position per iteration); :98 (PARAM_RANGES.trailingStopPct min 0.01 max 0.15 — the tuned param); :573/:602/:597/:624 (entry/sizing/equity use bar.close, confirming the bias is exit-path-only). Live engine divergence: src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2767 (peakPrice = currentPrice from single quote), :2820-2823 (effectiveStop tested against currentPrice), :2632 (live reads persisted trailingStopPct from optimizer bestParams).

### 12. ⬜ PENDING · [P2] excessReturn fitness subtracts incomparable bases (portfolio compounded return minus average per-symbol simple return)

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/optimizer.ts:332-364,644-676,988-1008`

**Description.** buyHoldReturn is computed as the simple average of each symbol's single-name simple percent return over the segment (buildPortfolioData lines 333-354: `((lastTrain-firstTrain)/firstTrain)*100`, averaged across symbols). totalReturn is the PORTFOLIO-level return of a 10-position, cash-constrained, compounded account (lines 644-645: `(finalEquity-INITIAL_CASH)/INITIAL_CASH*100`). excessReturn = totalReturn - buyHold (line 676) and is the core of the GA fitness (riskAdjust line 1002 multiplies excessReturn by the risk multipliers; fitness() line 1007 returns it). Subtracting an equal-weight average-single-name simple return from a compounded concentrated-portfolio return mixes two different mathematical bases, so 'excess' over buy-and-hold is not an apples-to-apples comparison.

**Impact.** The optimization objective is distorted: in strong bull universes the average-single-name benchmark can be far larger or smaller than a 10-name compounded portfolio's achievable return, biasing the GA's notion of 'beating buy-and-hold' and therefore which params win. Reported bestTestReturn/excess shown to users (optimizer page, mode-compare) is built on the same mismatched basis.

**Suggested fix.** Compute buy-and-hold on the SAME basis as the strategy: simulate an equal-weight (or same-sizing) buy-and-hold PORTFOLIO over the segment with the same INITIAL_CASH and compounding, and take its totalReturn as the benchmark. Then excessReturn is a like-for-like difference.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> All cited mechanics are present and behave exactly as described. (1) buyHoldReturn is an equal-weight simple average of each symbol's single-name simple percent return over the segment: buildPortfolioData accumulates `bhTrainSum += ((lastTrain - firstTrain) / firstTrain) * 100` per symbol (lines 347, 351) and returns `bhTrainSum / bhTrainN` (line 362). (2) totalReturn is a portfolio-level compounded return: `((finalEquity - INITIAL_CASH) / INITIAL_CASH) * 100` (line 645) where INITIAL_CASH=10000 (line 134) and the portfolio is cash-constrained, capped at 10 positions (line 113; 1.5x for STRONG_BUY line 121), sized at 10% of marked portfolio value per entry (BACKTEST_POSITION_PCT=0.10 line 112, used line 603), compounding through buys/sells. (3) excessReturn = totalReturn - buyHold (line 676), where buyHold is the avg-single-name value (line 410). (4) excessReturn is the core of GA fitness: riskAdjust returns it directly for losers (line 991) and excessReturn × softSharpe × softDrawdown for winners (line 1002); fitness() returns riskAdjust(portfolioBacktest(...)) (line 1007). (5) The mismatched values are surfaced to users — testExcess/trainExcess in logs (lines 1136-1137) and excessReturn/buyHoldReturn returned in PortfolioResult; bestTestReturn/baselineTestReturn persisted to optimizationRuns are the totalReturn side of the same comparison (lines 1121-1124). The two bases are genuinely incomparable, not coincidentally equal: an equal-weight average of every symbol's full-window simple hold vs. a ≤10-name, signal-selected, cash-gated, compounded portfolio that does not hold all names, holds them unequally, and exits before window end. In a strong-bull universe the all-symbol average diverges systematically from what a 10-slot compounding strategy can achieve, biasing the GA's 'beat buy-and-hold' objective and which params win. This matches the project's own 'don't fabricate a basis / signed-ratio' numeric-correctness guidance. Severity stays P2: it distorts the research/selection objective and a user-displayed metric, but does not affect live position sizing (explicitly separate per line 111 comment), money math, or runtime safety — so not P0/P1, and too central to the objective and user-facing reporting to drop to P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/optimizer.ts:347,351,362 (avgBuyHold = equal-weight avg of per-symbol simple return); :645 (totalReturn = compounded portfolio return from INITIAL_CASH); :134 INITIAL_CASH=10000; :112-113,121 cap=10/15 + 10% sizing; :603 posSize = portfolioValue × BACKTEST_POSITION_PCT; :410 buyHold = avgBuyHoldTrain/Test; :676 excessReturn = totalReturn - buyHold; :991,1002,1007 fitness built on excessReturn; :1121-1124,1136-1137 totalReturn/excess surfaced to users and persisted

### 13. ⬜ PENDING · [P2] Engine loads globally-latest optimizer run with no userId scope when no run is marked active

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** data · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2605-2623`

**Description.** _loadOptimizedParams first tries the run with isActive=true (no userId filter), then falls back to `status='complete' ORDER BY completedAt DESC LIMIT 1` — again with NO userId predicate (lines 2613-2618). The result is cached in a module-level singleton shared by every optimized-mode engine. So if no run is explicitly saved as active, every user's optimized engine adopts whichever user most recently completed an optimization run. Optimization runs are per-user rows (optimization_runs.userId, schema line 16) created by startOptimization(userId,...).

**Impact.** Cross-tenant parameter bleed: user B's optimized live/paper engine can trade with user A's GA-tuned stop/take-profit/EMA/RSI params purely because A finished a run more recently, with no user consent or visibility. Wrong risk params on live orders.

**Suggested fix.** Scope the params lookup to the engine's own userId (pass userId into _loadOptimizedParams and add `eq(optimizationRuns.userId, userId)` to both queries), and key the cache by userId rather than a single module-level slot. If a global shared preset is genuinely intended, gate it behind the explicit isActive flag only and never fall back to an arbitrary user's latest run.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present and behaves as described. `_loadOptimizedParams` (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2605-2651) first queries the explicitly-active run with NO userId predicate (lines 2608-2612, status='complete' AND isActive=true LIMIT 1), then falls back to `status='complete' ORDER BY completedAt DESC LIMIT 1` — again with NO userId predicate (lines 2613-2618). The result lands in a module-level singleton `_optimizedParamsCache` (line 2576, written at 2646) shared by every per-user engine. It is consumed by all optimized-mode engines via resolveStrategy() (line 2690), getOptimizedSignalParams() (line 4778), and getOptimizedTpAtrMult() (line 5369) — i.e. it sets stop-loss, take-profit, EMA, RSI, and hold-period params on real (paper/live) orders. The fallback path is the NORMAL state: completed runs default to isActive=false (schema line 47), and optimizer.ts:1118-1131 sets status='complete' WITHOUT setting isActive, so unless someone explicitly calls /api/optimize/save-preset, every optimized engine adopts whichever user most recently finished a run. optimization_runs.userId exists per-row (schema line 16) and runs are created per-user by startOptimization(auth.userId,...). Two caveats temper but do not refute the finding: (1) The shared/global preset is INTENTIONAL by design — the save-preset route (lines 53-59) and CLAUDE.md both document that the active optimizer preset is a single global slot shared across engines, and bestParams are tuning constants (stops/EMAs), not PII/account state, so the 'cross-tenant data leak / IDOR' framing is overstated — this is a correctness/nondeterminism defect, not a privacy breach. (2) But the finding is AMPLIFIED by the fact that optimization is NOT admin-only as CLAUDE.md claims: /api/optimize POST (route.ts:20) gates only on checkTier('trader'), so any trader-tier user can create a completed run that the global fallback then silently adopts for everyone. The proposed fix direction (gate strictly on the explicit isActive flag, or scope+cache by userId) is sound. P2 is appropriate: real, affects live order params, but not active-exploitable data exfiltration and partly intended-as-global.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2613-2618 (fallback query: .where(eq(optimizationRuns.status,'complete')).orderBy(desc(completedAt)).limit(1) — no userId); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2576,2646 (module-level _optimizedParamsCache singleton); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2690,4778,5369 (consumed by every optimized-mode engine for strategy/signal/TP params); src/lib/db/schema/optimization.ts:16,47 (userId per-row, isActive default false); src/lib/optimizer.ts:1118-1131 (completion sets status='complete' but not isActive=true); src/app/api/optimize/save-preset/route.ts:53-59 (comment confirms global shared cache + nondeterministic LIMIT 1); src/app/api/optimize/route.ts:20 (POST gated only on checkTier('trader'), not admin)

### 14. ⬜ PENDING · [P2] Harvesting savings always computed at the ordinary marginal rate, overstating savings for long-term losers

- **Lane:** tax · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/tax-engine.ts:431-461`

**Description.** suggestHarvesting computes `marginalRate = getMarginalRate(ORDINARY_BRACKETS[filingStatus], ordinaryIncome)` once (line 437) and applies it to every position's loss as `potentialSavings = currentLoss * marginalRate` (line 443), regardless of how long the position has been held. A long-term position's harvested loss offsets long-term gains (and beyond that, ordinary income only up to the $3,000/yr limit) — its real tax benefit is generally the LTCG rate (0/15/20%), not the ordinary rate (often 22-37%). The TaxPosition input carries no acquisition date (lines 178-184), and the harvesting route feeds it raw broker positions with no holding-period field (src/app/api/tax/harvesting/route.ts lines 56-69), so the function cannot even distinguish ST from LT. It also ignores the $3,000 net-loss deduction cap, so users with no offsetting gains see a savings number far larger than the IRS will allow this year.

**Impact.** Users see inflated 'potential tax savings' for harvesting long-term losers (e.g. a 37% number when the real benefit is 15%), and inflated savings when they have no gains to offset (cap ignored). Drives sub-optimal tax-loss-harvesting decisions and erodes trust in the Tax Center numbers.

**Suggested fix.** Add an acquisition date / holding-period flag to TaxPosition, populate it from broker lot data where available, and select the ordinary vs LTCG marginal rate accordingly. Cap the ordinary-income offset portion at the annual $3,000 ($1,500 MFS) net-loss limit when the user has no offsetting realized gains, or clearly label the figure as 'gross loss × top rate, before the $3k cap'.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> Confirmed. suggestHarvesting computes one ordinary marginal rate at line 437 and applies it to every loss at line 443 with no holding-period info. TaxPosition lacks an acquisition date at lines 178 to 184 and the route supplies none at lines 46 to 67, so short-term versus long-term cannot be distinguished. LTCG_BRACKETS exists and is used correctly in calculateTaxSummary at lines 367 to 375 with the 3000 or 1500 cap at line 360, but harvesting ignores both the LTCG rate and the cap. The route calls with defaults single and 50000 where ordinary is 22 percent versus LTCG 15 percent, so long-term losers are overstated, and the UI shows it as a green Save figure with no caveat at tax-center page line 392. Real numeric defect, bounded, in an advisory feature with disclaimers, so P2.

**Evidence:** tax-engine.ts lines 437 443 178-184 57-78 360 367-375; harvesting route.ts lines 46-67 and 78; tax-center page.tsx line 392

### 15. ✅ FIXED · [P2] price_above_sma silently checks SMA-20 for every threshold except exactly 50 (wrong indicator)

- **Lane:** alerts · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/alert-engine.ts:63-65`

**Description.** checkIndicatorRule's price_above_sma branch is `if (threshold === 50) return ...sma_50...; return ...sma_20...`. The threshold/value is the user-supplied rule `value` (an unconstrained z.number() in src/app/api/alerts/route.ts line 16, free-text operator at line 15). A user who creates a 'price crossed above SMA 200' rule with value=200 — or any value other than the magic literal 50 (e.g. 100, 200, even 50.0 vs 49.9) — silently falls through to the SMA-20 comparison. The rule evaluates an entirely different moving average than the user configured.

**Impact.** Indicator alerts fire (or stay silent) against the wrong moving average. An SMA-200 trend-break alert actually tracks SMA-20, producing alerts that have no relationship to what the user set up — a wrong trading signal delivered as if it were the requested one.

**Suggested fix.** Map the threshold to the intended SMA explicitly (e.g. switch on 9/20/50/200 to the matching indicator field) and reject unsupported periods at rule-creation in the zod schema, rather than defaulting any non-50 value to SMA-20. Better: store the SMA period in a dedicated column instead of overloading `value`.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. In src/lib/alert-engine.ts:63-65 the price_above_sma branch is `if (threshold === 50) return ind.sma_50...; return ind.sma_20...`, so the only threshold that selects SMA-50 is the exact float literal 50; every other value (100, 200, 49.9, etc.) silently falls through to the SMA-20 comparison. The threshold IS the user-supplied rule value: it flows from rule.value (alert-engine.ts:111) which is created via the API with `value: z.number()` (no period constraint, src/app/api/alerts/route.ts:16), stored in a `real` column (src/lib/db/schema/alerts.ts:19), and entered through an unconstrained `type="number"` input in the UI whose label literally reads "SMA Period (20 or 50)" (src/app/dashboard/alerts/page.tsx:76, 241-246) — so arbitrary periods are reachable. The live path is real: src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:56 calls evaluateAlertRules → checkIndicatorRule. The bug is compounded by buildMessage (line 157) which announces any non-50 value as "SMA 20", masking the misconfiguration. The existing test (tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts:110-111) only exercises 50 and 20, so it never catches the unsupported-period fallthrough. So the claim is factually correct and the impact (wrong moving average evaluated) is genuine. Severity is corrected from P1 to P2: the blast radius is narrow (one of 10 rule types, only when value ≠ 50), and alerts are advisory notifications — they do not auto-execute trades or move money. Notably the analyzer only computes sma_9/sma_20/sma_50 (analyzer.ts:267-333, no sma_200), so the finding's "SMA 200" example could never be served correctly under any fix anyway; the realistic harm is a user who types 200/100 expecting a long-term SMA and gets a short-term SMA-20 alert mislabeled as such.

**Evidence:** src/lib/alert-engine.ts:63-65 (price_above_sma: `if (threshold === 50) return ind.sma_50...; return ind.sma_20...`); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:111 (checkIndicatorRule(rule.operator, rule.value, context)); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:157 (buildMessage labels non-50 as "SMA 20"); src/app/api/alerts/route.ts:16 (value: z.number() — no period constraint); src/lib/db/schema/alerts.ts:19 (value real() — float, so 50.0 vs 49.9 equality is exact); src/app/dashboard/alerts/page.tsx:76,241-246 (unconstrained type="number" input, label "SMA Period (20 or 50)"); src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:56 (live caller); src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:267-333 (only sma_9/sma_20/sma_50 computed — no sma_200); tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts:110-111 (tests only 50 and 20, misses the fallthrough).
