### 16. ✅ FIXED · [P2] First evaluation of macd/ema/sma crossover rules fires a false 'crossover detected' when the level is already true

- **Lane:** alerts · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/alert-engine.ts:40-47, 59-65`

**Description.** macd_crossover/ema_crossover/price_above_sma are pure level checks (macd_histogram>0, ema_9>ema_21, price>sma). decideAlert turns a level into an 'edge' using lastConditionMet, which the schema (src/lib/db/schema/alerts.ts line 26) defaults to `false`. So the first cron evaluation after a rule is created (or re-enabled) sees conditionMet=true, lastConditionMet=false → risingEdge → fires and emits buildMessage 'MACD bullish crossover detected' / 'EMA 9/21 bullish crossover detected' — even though the cross may have happened days ago and the indicator is merely still above. This is exactly the 'still above since days ago' false-cross the edge-trigger design claims to eliminate; it only eliminates re-fires, not the initial spurious cross.

**Impact.** Every newly created or re-enabled crossover/SMA alert on a symbol that is already in the bullish state fires a misleading 'crossover detected just now' notification, prompting the user to act on a cross that did not occur. Re-enabling toggles repeatedly can replay the false fire.

**Suggested fix.** Initialize lastConditionMet to the current condition state at rule creation (seed it from a one-time evaluation), or treat the first observation as a baseline (persist state without firing when lastTriggered is null and the rule has never been evaluated). Alternatively detect a true cross using the prior bar's indicator value rather than a level + edge wrapper.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The described mechanism is real and present exactly as claimed. (1) macd_crossover/ema_crossover/price_above_sma are pure level checks in checkIndicatorRule (src/lib/alert-engine.ts:59-65: macd_histogram>0, ema_9>ema_21, price>sma) — no prior-bar comparison, so they cannot distinguish a fresh cross from "still above." (2) decideAlert (lines 40-47) converts that level into an edge using lastConditionMet: risingEdge = conditionMet && !lastConditionMet. (3) The schema defaults last_condition_met to false (src/lib/db/schema/alerts.ts:26), and the POST creator (src/app/api/alerts/route.ts:74-82) inserts only userId + parsed fields, never seeding lastConditionMet from a one-time evaluation, so a brand-new rule starts at false. (4) The cron (src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:47-64) fully populates real indicators from a live fetch. Therefore on the first evaluation of a rule whose symbol is already bullish: conditionMet=true, lastConditionMet=false, lastTriggered=null → risingEdge=true, cooldown not engaged → fire=true → buildMessage emits "MACD bullish crossover detected" / "EMA 9/21 bullish crossover detected" / "price crossed above SMA" (lines 155-157), asserting a cross that may have happened days ago. The unit test at tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts:18-20 codifies this exact case (decideAlert(true,false,null,NOW) → fire:true) as expected behavior, confirming there is no seeding mitigation. This directly contradicts the stated design goal in alerts.ts:23-25 ("signal the actual cross, not 'still true since days ago'") — the edge-trigger eliminates re-fires but not the spurious initial fire. The proposed fix (seed lastConditionMet from a one-time eval at creation, or skip firing on the first-ever observation) is sound. The re-enable-replay sub-claim is narrower than stated: PATCH (lines 100-108) doesn't reset lastConditionMet and disabled rules are excluded from the cron (route.ts:38-39), so a toggle only replays the false fire if the condition flipped false→true during the disabled window while lastConditionMet was frozen at false — a real but conditional path. Severity corrected from P1 to P2: this is a genuine user-facing correctness defect in alert semantics, but it is bounded (one misleading notification per newly created/re-enabled rule, advisory only, no automated trade is placed, no data exposure, no engine effect).

**Evidence:** src/lib/alert-engine.ts:40-47 (decideAlert risingEdge logic); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:59-65 (pure level checks for macd/ema/sma); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:155-157 (buildMessage "crossover detected" strings); src/lib/db/schema/alerts.ts:26 (lastConditionMet .notNull().default(false)); src/app/api/alerts/route.ts:74-82 (POST inserts without seeding lastConditionMet); src/app/api/alerts/route.ts:100-108 (PATCH does not reset lastConditionMet on re-enable); src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:38-39 (cron only selects enabled rules) and 47-64 (populates real indicators); tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts:18-20 (codifies first-eval true→fire as expected)

### 17. ✅ FIXED · [P2] executeTrade: non-atomic read-modify-write corrupts portfolio cash and positions under concurrency

- **Lane:** backtester · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/portfolio-sim.ts:45-149`

**Description.** executeTrade() reads the portfolio row (line 52-56) and the position row (line 73-82 / 104-113), computes a new currentBalance / quantity in JS, then writes it back with plain UPDATEs — no db.transaction() and no atomic SQL (e.g. `set balance = balance - cost`). Every step is an independent statement. Two concurrent POSTs to /api/portfolio/[id]/trade (or a BUY racing a SELL) both read the same currentBalance (line 69 sets `portfolio.currentBalance - cost`, line 124 sets `portfolio.currentBalance + proceeds` — both off the SAME stale snapshot). The last UPDATE wins and the other trade's cash delta is silently lost, yet BOTH trades are still recorded via the insert at line 142-148. The position upsert (line 84-101) has the same shape: concurrent BUYs both read `existing.quantity`, both write `existing.quantity + shares` off the stale read, losing one buy's shares from the position while both fills are recorded. The 'Insufficient cash' / 'Insufficient shares' guards (line 62, 115) read the same stale snapshot, so they can both pass and overdraw the account into a negative balance.

**Impact.** Paper-portfolio cash balance and position quantities desync from the recorded trade ledger: lost cash deltas, phantom or missing shares, balances driven negative. Net-worth (/api/portfolio/summary, NetWorthWidget) and harvestable-loss messaging built on these values become wrong. Trivially triggered by a double-click or two browser tabs.

**Suggested fix.** Wrap the whole operation in db.transaction(); inside it use atomic SQL deltas (`currentBalance = currentBalance - ${cost}`) with a guard predicate (`WHERE currentBalance >= ${cost}`) and an ON CONFLICT upsert for the position row, so the balance/quantity checks and mutations are a single serialized read-modify-write. Re-check rows-affected to detect the insufficient-funds case rather than trusting the pre-read snapshot.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is technically accurate in every claimed mechanism. executeTrade (src/lib/portfolio-sim.ts:45-149) performs a non-atomic read-modify-write with no db.transaction() and no atomic SQL delta: it SELECTs the portfolio (52-56), guards on the stale snapshot (cost > portfolio.currentBalance at 62; position.quantity < shares at 115), then writes back an ABSOLUTE computed value (currentBalance: portfolio.currentBalance - cost at 69; portfolio.currentBalance + proceeds at 124 — both off the same pre-read snapshot), and unconditionally inserts the trade row (142-148). The position update (84-93) likewise writes existing.quantity + shares computed from a stale read. db (src/lib/db/index.ts:26-31) is a 20-connection pool with no implicit transaction wrapping — executeTrade uses bare db, not withTimeout's db.transaction(). Node's await yields between each of the 6 statements, so two concurrent POSTs (double-click / two tabs, same authenticated owner) interleave: both read the same currentBalance, both pass the insufficient-cash guard, both UPDATE to an absolute value → last-writer-wins silently drops one trade's cash delta while BOTH ledger rows persist. Same shape for the position upsert (shares lost) and the guards (account can be overdrawn negative — currentBalance is a plain real with no DB CHECK, schema line 18). The downstream-impact claim is also verified: getPortfolioValue() reads currentBalance (164) and pos.quantity (170/172) and feeds /api/portfolio/summary's manualTotal → net-worth total (summary/route.ts:49,114). The route's CSRF + ownership checks (trade/route.ts:23,35) are irrelevant to same-user concurrency. This directly violates the project's own 'Atomic upsert on accumulator rows', 'Single-Owner Transaction Discipline', and 'Claim Before You Await' rules. Severity corrected from P1 to P2: the blast radius is confined to the PAPER simulator (manual portfolios) — no real money moves (Alpaca/live engine is a separate path and is the source of truth for real positions), and ownership is enforced so there is no cross-tenant leak. It is a genuine, trivially-triggered data-integrity/concurrency defect, but paper-only impact and the lack of money movement make P1 slightly inflated.

**Evidence:** src/lib/portfolio-sim.ts:52-56 (read portfolio), :62 (stale BUY guard), :69 (absolute write currentBalance - cost), :84-93 (stale position read-modify-write existing.quantity + shares), :115 (stale SELL guard), :124 (absolute write currentBalance + proceeds), :142-148 (unconditional trade insert); no db.transaction()/FOR UPDATE/atomic delta anywhere in 45-149. src/lib/db/index.ts:17-24 (postgres pool max:20), :26-31 (bare db proxy, no implicit tx). src/lib/db/schema/portfolio.ts:18 (currentBalance real, no CHECK), :28 (quantity integer), :33 (unique index portfolioId,symbol). src/app/api/portfolio/[id]/trade/route.ts:23,35,59 (CSRF+ownership, then executeTrade). src/lib/portfolio-sim.ts:164,170-172 + src/app/api/portfolio/summary/route.ts:49,114 (currentBalance/quantity feed net-worth total).

### 18. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Raw Alpaca error message is echoed to clients as userMessage (upstream-error leak)

- **Lane:** brokers · **Category:** security · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/brokers.ts:442-456`

**Description.** On a failed Alpaca order POST, placeOrder parses the raw broker body (`JSON.parse(errorText)`) and assigns `errorData.message` directly to the BrokerError `userMessage`. That `userMessage` is returned verbatim to the HTTP client at src/app/api/broker/orders/route.ts:233 (`{ error: err.userMessage }`) and account/test routes. Every other broker failure path in this file uses a curated generic userMessage ("Failed to place order", "Invalid API credentials", etc.), but this one path forwards whatever Alpaca says. The CLAUDE rules state "Never expose upstream errors to clients — sanitize via API Error envelope, log full server-side, return generic." Alpaca rejection messages can disclose account-level/internal details (buying-power figures, account restrictions, internal reason codes) and are not part of a stable machine contract.

**Impact.** Information disclosure of upstream/account internals to the browser; also couples the client UX to unstable broker prose. Not a money-loss bug but a confirmed sanitization-policy violation on a live-order path.

**Suggested fix.** Log the raw Alpaca message server-side only; return a curated generic userMessage (e.g. map known codes like insufficient buying power / wash-trade / qty-not-available to fixed strings) instead of forwarding `errorData.message`. Keep the full text in the log line that already exists at line 437-440.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate as described. In src/lib/brokers.ts the Alpaca placeOrder failure handler (lines 442-456) does `JSON.parse(errorText)` on the raw broker response body and assigns `errorData.message` directly into the BrokerError's `userMessage` (userError var, line 446 -> 455). BrokerError (lines 149-158) exposes userMessage as a public field. At src/app/api/broker/orders/route.ts:233 the POST handler returns `{ error: err.userMessage }` with err.statusCode verbatim to the HTTP client, with no API-error-envelope wrapping and no re-sanitization. This violates the documented rule "Never expose upstream errors to clients — sanitize via API Error envelope, log full server-side, return generic." The leak is the lone outlier: every other broker failure path in the file uses a curated generic userMessage (e.g. lines 254-258 "Invalid API credentials", 294-298 "Failed to fetch positions", 334-338 "Failed to fetch orders"), and the IBKR place-order path at 847-851 deliberately returns "Failed to place order on IBKR" instead of forwarding errorText — proving the intended pattern that the Alpaca path breaks. The full raw text is already logged server-side at lines 437-440, so the proposed fix is trivial. Impact is information disclosure / unstable-contract coupling (Alpaca rejection prose can include buying-power figures, account restrictions, internal reason codes), not money loss; access is gated to an authenticated user acting on their own broker connection. P2 is appropriate — a real sanitization-policy violation on a live-order path, but bounded in sensitivity. (Aside: cancelOrder line 495 and replaceOrder line 523 forward raw broker text into userMessage too, so the same leak class exists beyond the cited path, reinforcing rather than weakening the finding.)

**Evidence:** src/lib/brokers.ts:442-456 (userError = errorData.message from JSON.parse(errorText), thrown as BrokerError userMessage); src/lib/brokers.ts:149-158 (BrokerError.userMessage public field); src/lib/brokers.ts:437-440 (raw errorText already logged server-side); src/app/api/broker/orders/route.ts:231-235 (returns { error: err.userMessage } verbatim with err.statusCode); contrast curated paths src/lib/brokers.ts:254-258, 294-298, 334-338, and IBKR generic 847-851 ("Failed to place order on IBKR"); additional same-class leaks at src/lib/brokers.ts:495 and 523.

### 19. ✅ FIXED · [P2] brokerFetch ignores HTTP 429 / Retry-After on every broker call

- **Lane:** brokers · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/brokers.ts:160-186, 287-324, 326-353, 378-457`

**Description.** brokerFetch has no awareness of rate-limit responses. Alpaca enforces ~200 req/min per account and returns 429 with a Retry-After header when exceeded. A 429 propagates through as `!res.ok` and is mapped to a generic BrokerError; getPositions turns it into a 502 the engine counts toward its 5-failure broker-unreachable halt (trading-engine.ts:4804-4815), and getOrders/placeOrder turn it into a hard failure. Nothing in this client (the only network layer to the broker) parses or honors Retry-After or X-RateLimit-Remaining, and there is no backoff — repeated scans keep hammering the same throttled endpoint. CLAUDE rules: "Respect upstream rate-limit signals... Don't blind-retry on 429."

**Impact.** Under load (many users / dense scan cadence) a transient 429 burst can (a) be miscounted as broker-unreachable and trip a spurious engine halt, and (b) prolong the throttle because the client keeps retrying immediately instead of waiting Retry-After. Degrades reliability of live trading exactly when the account is busiest.

**Suggested fix.** In brokerFetch, special-case res.status===429: read Retry-After, surface it on a dedicated retryable BrokerError (retryable=true, statusCode 429), and have the engine treat 429 distinctly from connectivity failures (don't increment consecutiveBrokerFailures; back off for Retry-After seconds). Centralize via a fetchWithRetry-style helper.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The code-level claim is fully accurate. brokerFetch (src/lib/brokers.ts:160-186) is the sole network layer to Alpaca and has no 429 / Retry-After / X-RateLimit awareness — a grep for those tokens in brokers.ts returns zero matches. Any non-ok status, 429 included, falls through `if (!res.ok)` and is mapped to a generic BrokerError with no backoff and no honoring of Retry-After. The per-endpoint mappings are exactly as described: getPositions → BrokerError(502) (brokers.ts:292-299), getOrders → BrokerError(502) (brokers.ts:332-339), placeOrder → BrokerError(400, non-retryable) (brokers.ts:432-457). A 429 on placeOrder thus becomes a terminal 400-class hard failure rather than a retryable signal, which is a concrete reliability defect under load. The engine-halt path also behaves as claimed but is narrower than the framing implies. In runScan, a getPositions() throw is caught at trading-engine.ts:4803-4816, increments engine.consecutiveBrokerFailures (4804), and trips tripSafeguardHalt(engine, "broker_unreachable") once it reaches BROKER_FAILURE_HALT_THRESHOLD = 5 (719, 4809). So a 429 IS miscounted toward the broker-unreachable halt — confirming the spurious-halt mechanism. However: (1) only the runScan path increments the counter — the tactical and tactical-smart paths catch getPositions failures and abort the scan WITHOUT incrementing (3805-3809, 4103-4107), and runExitCheck (the 1-min poll) never calls getPositions() so it can't count failures; (2) runScan runs at SWING_SCAN_MS = 15 min (382), so tripping the 5-failure halt requires ~5 consecutive scans (≈60-75 min) of sustained 429s, not a brief transient burst. The "keeps hammering immediately" concern is also mild for the positions path at a 15-min cadence. The defect is real and violates the documented "respect upstream rate-limit signals / don't blind-retry on 429" rule, but the worst-case impact requires sustained throttling rather than a momentary spike. P2 (reliability) is appropriate and not inflated.

**Evidence:** src/lib/brokers.ts:160-186 (brokerFetch — no 429/Retry-After handling); src/lib/brokers.ts:292-299 (getPositions → 502); src/lib/brokers.ts:332-339 (getOrders → 502); src/lib/brokers.ts:432-457 (placeOrder 429 → non-retryable 400); grep for 429/Retry-After/X-RateLimit in brokers.ts = no matches; src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4798-4816 (runScan getPositions failure → consecutiveBrokerFailures++ → broker_unreachable halt); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:719 (BROKER_FAILURE_HALT_THRESHOLD = 5); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:382 (SWING_SCAN_MS = 15 min); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3805-3809 and 4103-4107 (tactical/tactical-smart abort on getPositions failure WITHOUT incrementing the counter); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2714-2743 (runExitCheck uses in-memory positionMap + fetchQuote, never getPositions).

### 20. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Sector-exposure cap is computed against a pre-scan snapshot, so multiple same-sector buys in one scan all bypass the cap

- **Lane:** engine-buy-gates · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:849-864, 928-956, 4934, 5405`

**Description.** buildSectorExposureContext snapshots positionMarketValues from the position map ONCE before the scan loop (scanSectorCtx at line 4934; tsSectorCtx 4067; tacticalSectorCtx 3750) and the Map is never updated as buys are placed during the loop. canPlaceBuyOrder sums sectorExposureContext.positionMarketValues (line 936) which stays frozen at the pre-scan view. So the first buy into a fresh sector sees sectorMv=0 and passes; the second, third, ... buys into that same sector in the SAME scan also see sectorMv=0 and pass. By contrast the daily-notional cap (engine.dailyNotional) IS incremented in-loop via recordOrderPlacement, so the two caps behave inconsistently. The code comment (lines 4928-4933) calls the staleness 'acceptable' but it lets the cap be exceeded by N positions per scan rather than bounding cumulative sector exposure.

**Impact.** When the cap is enabled (via DB), the engine can open many positions in one sector within a single scan, concentrating well past maxSectorExposurePct — exactly the concentration the cap exists to prevent. Worst case in a single scan, an entire fresh sector can be loaded up to the per-symbol notional each.

**Suggested fix.** Mutate the context Map as buys are placed (positionMarketValues.set(symbol, buyNotional) right after a successful placement, mirroring the dailyNotional accumulation), or rebuild scanSectorCtx after each buy. The cap must accumulate within-scan like the notional cap does.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate and reproducible from the code. buildSectorExposureContext (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:849-864) builds a fresh `positionMarketValues` Map (a `new Map()` at line 856, NOT a reference to the live position map) once before the scan loop. In runScan this is `scanSectorCtx` at line 4934, with a comment (4928-4933) explicitly calling the staleness "acceptable." canPlaceBuyOrder sums this frozen Map (lines 935-938) to compute existing sector exposure. After a successful BUY (line 5432-5440) the code calls recordOrderPlacement (mutating engine.dailyNotional, line 1025) and positionMap.set (line 5464, mutating the LIVE map) — but it never updates scanSectorCtx.positionMarketValues. Grep confirms `positionMarketValues.set` appears only at line 861 (initial build); there is no in-loop mutation anywhere. So a second/third same-sector buy in the same scan re-reads the identical pre-scan sum and passes again. The inconsistency the finding draws is real: the daily-notional cap (line 989) reads the in-loop-mutated engine.dailyNotional, and the portfolio exposure cap (lines 5347-5349) reads the live positionMap via Array.from(positionMap.values()), so BOTH accumulate correctly within a scan — only the sector cap reads a frozen snapshot. getSymbolSector (sectors.ts:196) returns real, granular buckets (Technology ~80 symbols), and the universe is top-50 by confidence, so multiple same-sector buys per scan are realistic in a sector-led move. The loop permits multiple buys (gated only by positionMap.size < positionCap at line 5300). All three call sites cited (tacticalSectorCtx 3750, tsSectorCtx 4067, scanSectorCtx 4934) share the bug. The comment's rationalization ("not reflected on the broker until next sync") is the flawed reasoning the finding identifies — the cap depends on the engine's own intra-scan placements, which are knowable. Severity stays P2 (not inflated): the cap is opt-in and OFF by default (maxSectorExposurePct: 0 = disabled, line 779), the over-concentration is bounded by positionCap and the portfolio-wide exposure cap, and the snapshot is rebuilt next scan so the window is a single scan — but when a user enables the cap, it genuinely fails to enforce the documented control for the principal position-adding path within each scan.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:856 (`const positionMarketValues = new Map<string, number>()` — independent snapshot, not a live reference); :861 (only `.set` call, during build); :928-956 (sector cap sums sectorExposureContext.positionMarketValues, never re-derived in-loop); :4928-4933 (comment rationalizing staleness as "acceptable"); :4934 (scanSectorCtx built once before loop); :5300 (loop allows multiple buys, gated by positionCap); :5347-5349 (portfolio exposure cap reads LIVE positionMap — accumulates correctly, contrast with sector cap); :5440 (recordOrderPlacement after buy) + :1022-1026 (mutates engine.dailyNotional — accumulates correctly); :5464 (positionMap.set updates live map but not scanSectorCtx); :989 (notional cap reads in-loop engine.dailyNotional); :779 (maxSectorExposurePct default 0 = disabled). Other call sites: :3750, :4067. sectors.ts:196 (getSymbolSector returns real buckets).

### 21. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Sector-exposure cap, earnings blackout, and adaptive mode are unreachable through the supported config path (missing from validator and UI)

- **Lane:** engine-buy-gates · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/validators.ts:182-199`

**Description.** updateRiskProfileSchema (the only schema the /api/risk-profile PATCH accepts, and Zod .object() strips unknown keys) omits maxSectorExposurePct, earningsBlackoutDays, and adaptiveModeEnabled. The Trader-page riskForm state (src/app/dashboard/trader/page.tsx lines 248-258) likewise never includes maxSectorExposurePct or earningsBlackoutDays, and the /api/trader/command 'risk' case is a no-op echo (command/route.ts line 218-224). A repo-wide grep shows maxSectorExposurePct and earningsBlackoutDays appear ONLY in the engine, the DB schema, and tests — never in any route/validator/UI. loadRiskLimits therefore always reads NULL → defaults to 0 (disabled) for every user who configures via the app. The recent commit 'enforce sector-exposure cap on tactical/tactical-smart buy paths' guards a cap that no user can actually turn on; same for the earnings-blackout gate.

**Impact.** Two documented live-trading safeguards (sector concentration cap and earnings blackout) and adaptive-mode auto-enable are dead through the product. Users believe these protections exist (they are documented in CLAUDE.md as Trader-page risk settings) but they can only be enabled by a manual DB write, so live engines run with no sector concentration limit and no earnings protection.

**Suggested fix.** Add maxSectorExposurePct (fraction 0..1), earningsBlackoutDays (int 0..N), and adaptiveModeEnabled to updateRiskProfileSchema, and surface them in the Trader-page risk form. Until then, the enforcement code is unreachable.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The core claim is verified. updateRiskProfileSchema (validators.ts:182-199) is a Zod .object() and omits maxSectorExposurePct, earningsBlackoutDays, and adaptiveModeEnabled. The ONLY write path to userRiskProfiles is /api/risk-profile PATCH (route.ts:51 safeParse → lines 67/89 write parsed.data); since .object() strips unknown keys, a client posting these three fields has them silently dropped before the DB write — they can never be persisted via the app. The Trader-page riskForm state (page.tsx:248-258) contains none of them, and a full-file grep returns no matches. The /api/trader/command 'risk' case (route.ts:218-224) is a no-op echo that performs no DB write. The only other userRiskProfiles consumer, strategy-resolver.ts:53-54, is read-only. loadRiskLimits (trading-engine.ts:779-806) defaults NULL columns to 0/false (disabled). The enforcement added by the recent commit is real (earnings blackout gated on earningsBlackoutDays > 0 at line 907; sector cap gated on maxSectorExposurePct > 0 at line 929) but is a permanent no-op for any user who configures through the product — so two documented buy-order safeguards (CLAUDE.md line 213 lists them in canPlaceBuyOrder gate ordering) are dead unless an admin hand-writes the DB row. Tests (engine-safeguards.test.ts:1016+) only exercise the logic by passing non-zero values directly, masking the gap. One correction to the finding's framing: the 'adaptive-mode auto-enable' impact is overstated. Adaptive MODE is fully reachable via the mode picker (USER_FACING_MODES includes 'adaptive'; the switch command accepts it at trading-engine.ts:7082). The adaptiveModeEnabled risk-profile boolean is a separate vestigial flag that is loaded into RiskLimits (line 805/828) but consumed nowhere in engine logic, so it is dead code regardless of reachability — not a safeguard users are deprived of. That overstatement does not change the verdict, because the sector-cap and earnings-blackout reachability gaps independently and clearly hold. P2 is appropriate: real config-reachability/safety gap (advertised live-trading protections silently disabled for app-configured users), but not a crash/data-loss or cross-tenant issue, and paper trading is unaffected.

**Evidence:** src/lib/validators.ts:182-199 (schema omits the three fields); src/app/api/risk-profile/route.ts:51,67,89 (safeParse + write parsed.data — only write path, Zod strips unknown keys); src/app/dashboard/trader/page.tsx:248-258 (riskForm lacks the fields; full-file grep returns no matches); src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:218-224 ('risk' case is no-op echo, no DB write); src/lib/strategy-resolver.ts:53-54 (read-only userRiskProfiles consumer); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:779-806 (NULL→0/false defaults), :907-923 (earnings gate on >0), :928-956 (sector gate on >0), :805/828 + grep showing adaptiveModeEnabled is loaded but never consumed, :108-112 and :7082 (adaptive mode reachable via picker/switch); src/lib/db/schema/strategies.ts:63-67 (columns exist); CLAUDE.md:213 (documents earnings blackout + sector exposure as canPlaceBuyOrder gates)

### 22. ✅ FIXED · [P2] placeDisasterStops can place a sell stop above current price on a gapped-down position at engine restart (broker rejects → unprotected)

- **Lane:** engine-exit-stops · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6724-6760`

**Description.** placeDisasterStops computes stopPrice = Math.max(disasterStop, trailStop, fixedStop).toFixed(2) (line 6746) where disasterStop = avgEntryPrice×0.82, fixedStop = avgEntryPrice×(1-stopLossPct), trailStop = peakPrice×(1-dynTrailPct) with peakPrice = max(currentPrice, avgEntryPrice) (line 6740). If the engine restarts while a held position is underwater beyond the fixed stop — e.g., optimized stopLossPct 0.085 gives fixedStop = entry×0.915 but currentPrice has gapped to entry×0.90 — Math.max selects entry×0.915, which is ABOVE the current market price. There is no comparison of the computed stopPrice against pos.currentPrice. Alpaca rejects a sell stop with stop_price >= current price; the catch at 6757 only logs the error.

**Impact.** On engine restart against a position that gapped down through its stop level (common after an overnight/weekend move), the protective broker stop is rejected and never placed, leaving the position with no broker-side protection. The error is swallowed to a log line, so the operator gets no clear signal that the position is unprotected.

**Suggested fix.** Clamp the disaster/fixed stop to below current price before placing: `const stopPrice = Math.min(Math.max(disasterStop, trailStop, fixedStop), pos.currentPrice * (1 - 0.001))`, or if the computed stop is at/above current price, fire an immediate market sell (the protective intent is already breached) and surface an audit/unprotected flag instead of letting the broker silently reject.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. In placeDisasterStops (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6724-6764) the stop price is `Math.max(disasterStop, trailStop, fixedStop).toFixed(2)` (line 6746) with disasterStop = avgEntryPrice×0.82 (DISASTER_STOP_PCT=0.18 at line 6316), fixedStop = avgEntryPrice×(1-stopLossPct) (6743), and trailStop = peakPrice×(1-dynTrailPct) where peakPrice = Math.max(currentPrice, avgEntryPrice) (6740). For an underwater position, peakPrice collapses to avgEntryPrice, so both fixedStop and trailStop are computed off entry, independent of how far currentPrice has fallen. There is no comparison of the computed stop against pos.currentPrice. The scenario is concrete: optimized stopLossPct=0.085 → fixedStop = entry×0.915; if currentPrice has gapped to entry×0.90, Math.max selects entry×0.915, which is ABOVE market. placeEngineOrder (1520-1551) forwards stopPrice unmodified to client.placeOrder, and the Alpaca adapter (brokers.ts:420) sets payload.stop_price raw with no clamp — Alpaca rejects a sell stop at/above the last trade price, returning a BrokerError (brokers.ts:432-452). The catch at 6757-6758 only runs log.error: it does NOT add the symbol to engine.unprotectedSymbols and does NOT write an audit row. This is a demonstrated inconsistency with the sibling initial-stop path (6585-6614) which, on the analogous PDT rejection, explicitly engine.unprotectedSymbols.add(symbol) + log.warn + writeAudit(ORDER_REJECTED) so the trader-page banner surfaces the unprotected position. placeDisasterStops runs on every engine boot/restart (startEngine at 5865), and the disaster stop's documented purpose (6316 comment: "only fires if server is down for hours") is precisely the server-down case the engine's 1-min runExitCheck cannot cover — so a swallowed rejection genuinely leaves the position with no broker-side protection and no operator signal. Impact is real but conditional (requires restart + underwater-past-stop + currentPrice between disaster and fixed/trail stop), and the engine's own poll still manages exits while it stays up. P2 is appropriate and matches the codebase's own treatment of the analogous rejection class (the 2026-06-09 P2 audit note at 6627).

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6740 (peakPrice = Math.max(currentPrice, avgEntryPrice)); :6743 (fixedStop = avgEntryPrice×(1-stopLossPct)); :6746 (stopPrice = Math.max(disasterStop, trailStop, fixedStop).toFixed(2) — no clamp to currentPrice); :6757-6758 (catch only log.error, no unprotectedSymbols.add, no audit); :6316 (DISASTER_STOP_PCT=0.18, "only fires if server is down for hours"); :5865 (placeDisasterStops invoked in startEngine on every boot/restart); src/lib/brokers.ts:420 (payload.stop_price = params.stopPrice — raw, unclamped); src/lib/brokers.ts:432-452 (non-ok → throw BrokerError); sibling correct-handling path src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6585-6606 (rejection → unprotectedSymbols.add + log.warn + writeAudit ORDER_REJECTED) shows the codebase already knows this failure mode and surfaces it elsewhere but not in placeDisasterStops.

### 23. ✅ FIXED · [P2] syncBrokerStops ignores the delayed-trail activation gate, pushing the dormant trail stop to the broker anyway

- **Lane:** engine-exit-stops · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6538-6566, 2814-2821, 5030-5038`

**Description.** The delayed-trail feature (isTrailActive, lines 2380-2391; trailActivationProfitPct/trailActivationBars) is meant to keep the trailing stop dormant until the position has gained a configured % (recommended 0.05) or aged N bars — during that window the position is protected only by the fixed disaster stop + breakeven ladder, giving it 'room to find its footing'. Both runExitCheck (line 2814-2821) and the main runScan exit (5030-5038) honor this gate: `trailStop = trailActive ? peak*(1-pct) : 0`. But syncBrokerStops, which writes the actual resting stop to the broker, computes `trailStop = pos.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrailPct)` unconditionally (line 6558) and uses `targetStop = Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)` (6566). It never calls isTrailActive. When the trail level is tighter (higher) than the fixed stop early in the position's life, the broker gets a stop at the trail level even though the engine's own in-memory exit logic considers the trail disabled.

**Impact.** Defeats the delayed-trail risk knob at the broker level: a user who set trailActivationProfitPct to delay trailing until +5% still gets stopped out at the broker by the trail (e.g., on a normal early pullback) the feature was specifically designed to ride through. The in-memory and broker-side stop behavior diverge, contradicting the documented purpose of the feature.

**Suggested fix.** Thread the risk limits + position age/peak-profit into syncBrokerStops and gate trailStop on isTrailActive() exactly as runExitCheck/runScan do (set trailStop to 0 / fall back to pos.stopLoss when the trail is dormant), so the broker stop matches the engine's effective stop.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate. Both in-memory exit paths gate the trail on isTrailActive(): runExitCheck computes `const trailStop = trailActive ? pos.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrailPct) : 0` (trading-engine.ts:2820) and the main runScan exit computes `const trailingStopPrice = trailActiveScan ? heldPosition.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrail) : 0` (5036-5038), both feeding isTrailActive() with the user's trailActivationBars/trailActivationProfitPct (2814-2818, 5030-5034). isTrailActive (2380-2391) returns false while peakProfitPct < trailActivationProfitPct (recommended 0.05) or positionAgeBars < trailActivationBars. syncBrokerStops, in contrast, computes `const trailStop = pos.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrailPct)` unconditionally (6558) and `const targetStop = Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)` (6566), never calling isTrailActive and never loading riskLimits (its signature is `syncBrokerStops(userId)` at 6506; it only pulls regimeVix from the engine). The divergence is real and harmful: during the dormant window peakPrice sits between entry and entry*1.05, while the calm-market trail floor is just 1% (TRAIL_FLOOR_VIX_CALM=0.01, 1839), so trailStop can compute to roughly entry*1.04 — far ABOVE the disaster floor of entry*0.88 (tactical, 3930/4310/4495/4565) or entry*(1-stopLossPct) (optimized, 3371/5365). targetStop therefore equals the trail level, and syncBrokerStops pushes it to Alpaca as a GTC resting stop-sell (placeEngineOrder type:'stop', timeInForce:'gtc', 6571-6575) or ratchets an existing stop up to it (priceRatchet replaceOrder, 6631-6653). That broker-resting stop fires server-side on a normal early pullback that the engine's own in-memory logic would ride through (trail dormant, only the far-below disaster stop active) — exactly the behavior the delayed-trail knob was designed to prevent. syncBrokerStops runs on every scan (3963/4604/5714) and on the independent stop-sync scheduler (7444), so the divergence is continuously live. Severity stays P2 rather than P1: the feature is opt-in (both knobs default to 0 = always-active per isTrailActive, so only users who explicitly set trailActivationProfitPct/Bars are exposed), and the proposed fix (load riskLimits via loadRiskLimits like runExitCheck at 2750 and gate trailStop on isTrailActive) is straightforward.

**Evidence:** trading-engine.ts:6558 (`const trailStop = pos.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrailPct)` — unconditional, no isTrailActive); 6566 (`const targetStop = Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)`); 6506 (`async function syncBrokerStops(userId: string | null)` — no riskLimits param); 6571-6575 + 6631-6655 (targetStop pushed/ratcheted to broker as GTC stop-sell); 2820 (runExitCheck: `trailActive ? pos.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrailPct) : 0`); 5036-5038 (runScan: `trailActiveScan ? heldPosition.peakPrice * (1 - dynTrail) : 0`); 2380-2391 (isTrailActive); 1839 (TRAIL_FLOOR_VIX_CALM=0.01); 3930/4310/3371 (entry disaster stops ~0.88 / 1-stopLossPct); 7444 + 3963/4604/5714 (syncBrokerStops call sites)

### 24. ✅ FIXED · [P2] User emergency halt (haltEngine) is never persisted to trader_daily_pnl — autoStartIfNeeded's user_emergency_halt suppression is dead code, restart can silently resume

- **Lane:** engine-lifecycle-regime · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6805-6914, 6991-7048, 1095-1124, 1416-1424`

**Description.** haltEngine() (6805-6914) sets engine.halted=true and engine.haltReason="user_emergency_halt" only in memory, liquidates broker positions, then returns. Unlike tripSafeguardHalt (which calls upsertDailyPnl(...,true,reason,...) at line 1121) and enforceDailyLossHalt (upsertDailyPnl(...,true,...) at 1416), haltEngine writes NO halted=true / halt_reason row to trader_daily_pnl. autoStartIfNeeded (6991-7048) gates auto-resume on the persisted traderDailyPnl.halted column (query at 7000-7005, check at 7006) and its comment at 7008-7011 explicitly lists "user_emergency_halt" as a reason that "persists and requires an explicit user Start." Because that reason can never appear in the DB, the gate is unreachable for emergency halts. The halt route (trader/engine/route.ts case "halt") only writes an audit row (metadata reason "user_requested_flatten_all" — itself inconsistent with the in-memory "user_emergency_halt"), no PnL persistence. Since haltEngine also clears all three interval handles, no subsequent scan fires to write the halted row either.

**Impact.** After a user emergency-halts the engine and the container later restarts/deploys, bootEngines→autoStartIfNeeded sees no persisted halt, passes the safeguard gate, and silently resumes automated trading. This only stays benign if liquidation fully succeeded (positions==0 → early return at 7071), but haltEngine swallows per-symbol liquidation failures (catch at 6892-6902), so any position that failed to close — or any position the user manually re-acquired — gets auto-managed/traded again by an engine the user explicitly emergency-halted. The DB-backed dashboard halt indicator also under-reports the emergency halt until/unless a later write occurs.

**Suggested fix.** In haltEngine, before returning, persist the halt the same way tripSafeguardHalt does: await/void upsertDailyPnl(getETDateString(), 0, 0, 0, true, "user_emergency_halt", engine.userId) (and set engine.haltContext for symmetry). Align the audit metadata reason with the in-memory haltReason. This makes the autoStartIfNeeded integrity-halt suppression actually fire for emergency halts.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate on every load-bearing claim. haltEngine (trading-engine.ts:6805-6914) sets engine.halted=true and engine.haltReason="user_emergency_halt" purely in memory (6826-6828), clears all three interval handles (6809-6824), liquidates broker positions while swallowing per-symbol close failures (catch at 6892-6902), then returns at 6913 — with NO upsertDailyPnl call anywhere in the function and no engine.haltContext set. By contrast tripSafeguardHalt persists the halt at 1121 (upsertDailyPnl(..., true, reason, ...)) and enforceDailyLossHalt at 1416, and the MTM halt path also persists. autoStartIfNeeded (6987-7048) gates auto-resume solely on the persisted traderDailyPnl.halted column (query 7000-7005, check 7006), and its own comment (7010) lists "user_emergency_halt" as an integrity halt that "persist[s] and require[s] an explicit user Start" — but a grep for user_emergency_halt across the whole repo shows it appears ONLY as an in-memory assignment (6828), in comments (1054/1071/7010), in docs, and in an in-memory-only rollover test (engine-safeguards.test.ts:872, which drives synthetic engine state, not a DB write). It is never written to the DB. The engine snapshot payload (engine-snapshot.ts:61-77, 86-100) carries no halted/haltReason field, so the snapshot hydration path provides no alternative persistence. Because haltEngine also clears every interval (6809-6824), no subsequent scan runs to persist engine.halted via the scan-end upsertDailyPnl calls (2891/3952/4593/5702, all reason=undefined and all inside scans). Therefore the autoStartIfNeeded suppression branch is unreachable for emergency halts: on restart it reads no halt row, falls through, and calls startEngine at 7122 whenever broker positions > 0 (only the positions.length===0 early return at 7071 blocks it). The halt route (trader/engine/route.ts case "halt", 143-163) writes only an audit row with metadata reason "user_requested_flatten_all" — inconsistent with the in-memory "user_emergency_halt" and persisting no PnL row; the admin halt route (admin/engine/route.ts:181) shares the gap. Impact is real but bounded: silent resume only re-manages positions that liquidation failed to close (errors are swallowed) or that the user manually re-acquired, and protective stops still fire on resumed positions, so it is not a catastrophic-loss P1. P2 is correct.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6826-6828 (in-memory halt set, no persist); 6892-6902 (swallowed liquidation failure); 6809-6824 (all intervals cleared); 6913 (return with no upsertDailyPnl); 1121 (tripSafeguardHalt persists), 1416 (enforceDailyLossHalt persists); 7000-7006 (autoStartIfNeeded reads traderDailyPnl.halted), 7010 (comment lists user_emergency_halt as persisting), 7071 (positions==0 early return), 7122 (startEngine called after gate passes); src/app/api/trader/engine/route.ts:149-156 (audit-only, reason "user_requested_flatten_all", no PnL persist); src/app/api/admin/engine/route.ts:181 (same haltEngine call); src/lib/engine-snapshot.ts:61-77,86-100 (snapshot payload lacks halted/haltReason); grep user_emergency_halt = only 6828 assignment + comments + docs + tests/unit/engine-safeguards.test.ts:872 (in-memory-only test, never a DB write)

### 25. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Daily-loss halt and MTM halt use gross losing-trade sum, not net realized P&L, causing false halts on net-profitable days

- **Lane:** engine-risk-halts · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2870, 5190, 1365-1366, 1471-1472`

**Description.** `engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0` (lines 2870, 5190) accumulates ONLY losing trades and never credits realized winners. enforceDailyLossHalt compares `engine.dailyLoss > -dailyLossThreshold` (1366) and enforceUnrealizedLossHalt computes `mtmLoss = engine.dailyLoss + totalUnrealizedPnl` (1471). So a day with, e.g., +$5000 of realized wins and -$2100 of realized losses on a $100k / 2% account ($2000 threshold) reports `dailyLoss = -2100` and halts, despite the account being net +$2900 realized. Meanwhile the dashboard's `realizedPnlThisScan` written to trader_daily_pnl is NET, so the displayed P&L and the halt basis diverge. The realized daily-loss limit is documented as 'daily losses exceed this %', which a trader will read as net loss.

**Impact.** Engine auto-halts on net-profitable, high-turnover days, sidelining the strategy and missing entries; the MTM halt inherits the same inflated loss basis. Conversely a user reading the (net) dashboard P&L cannot reconcile why the engine halted. Wrong metric basis for a money-gating control.

**Suggested fix.** Accumulate net realized P&L for the day (`engine.dailyLoss += pnl`) so the cap reflects net intraday drawdown, OR rename/redocument the field as 'gross realized losses' and confirm that is the intended semantics. If gross is intended for conservatism, keep it but make the dashboard surface the same gross-loss figure the halt uses to avoid the reconciliation gap.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. `engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0` at trading-engine.ts:2870 (runExitCheck) and :5190 (tactical-smart scan) accumulates ONLY losing trades; a grep of every `dailyLoss` mutation confirms there is no path that credits realized winners (only resets to 0 at the daily rollover, lines 1065 and 5888, plus snapshot hydration at 6032). enforceDailyLossHalt compares `engine.dailyLoss > -dailyLossThreshold` where `dailyLossThreshold = equity * dailyLossPct` (1365-1366), so the gate trips on cumulative GROSS losses regardless of offsetting wins. enforceUnrealizedLossHalt inherits the same gross basis via `mtmLoss = engine.dailyLoss + totalUnrealizedPnl` (1471). Meanwhile the very same exit at line 5189 does `realizedPnlThisScan += pnl` (NET, winners included), and that net value is what gets written to trader_daily_pnl.realizedPnl through upsertDailyPnl's atomic add (4593/5704 -> 3099/3109). So the dashboard-displayed realized P&L and the halt basis genuinely diverge, and the worked example (net +realized but gross losses past the cap -> false halt) is mechanically valid. CLAUDE.md documents the field as 'auto-halts when daily losses exceed this %', which reads as net. Impact is real but bounded: false halts only occur on net-profitable high-turnover days where gross losing-leg sum alone breaches the threshold; gross-only is also a defensibly conservative brake, and a halt blocks new BUYs without flattening. Note the consecutive-loss safeguard (recordTradeResult) resets on any winning trade, so this gate is inconsistent with the engine's other net-aware streak logic. This is a wrong-metric-basis money-gating control plus a documented-semantics and dashboard reconciliation mismatch — genuine but not a P1 loss/security issue. P2 stands.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2870 `engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0` (gross, runExitCheck); :5189-5190 `realizedPnlThisScan += pnl;` (NET) immediately followed by `engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0;` (gross) — same exit, two divergent accumulators; :1365-1366 `const dailyLossThreshold = equity * riskLimits.dailyLossPct; if (engine.dailyLoss > -dailyLossThreshold) return false;`; :1471 `const mtmLoss = engine.dailyLoss + totalUnrealizedPnl;`; :4593 / :5702-5704 `upsertDailyPnl(today, realizedPnlThisScan, ...)` writes the NET realized figure to trader_daily_pnl; :3099/:3109 atomic add of realizedDelta; :1065 `engine.dailyLoss = 0` daily reset (only mutation besides the two += and hydration at :6032). No code path credits positive pnl to engine.dailyLoss.

### 26. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Broker-side stop fills never update engine.dailyLoss — realized daily-loss halt is bypassable

- **Lane:** engine-scan-concurrency · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:1346-1366, 3401-3489, 3512-3663, 2870, 5190`

**Description.** engine.dailyLoss is a purely in-memory accumulator and the realized daily-loss halt (enforceDailyLossHalt, line 1366: `if (engine.dailyLoss > -dailyLossThreshold) return false`) reads only that field. engine.dailyLoss is incremented at exactly two sites — runExitCheck (line 2870) and runScan's own exit (line 5190) — i.e. only when the ENGINE places the sell. When a position is closed broker-side (a disaster/protective stop fires on Alpaca, manual sell, or any external close), syncPositionMapFromBroker → reconcileBrokerSideExit (3401) writes the loss to trader_trades.pnl in the DB, and reconcilePendingTrades (3512) corrects PENDING rows in the DB, but NEITHER adds the realized loss to engine.dailyLoss. So losses realized by broker-side stops are invisible to the realized daily-loss halt. The MTM unrealized halt (enforceUnrealizedLossHalt) is a partial backstop while the position is open, but once the stop fires the loss is realized and disappears from both the unrealized total and the realized counter.

**Impact.** On a day where protective broker stops do most of the selling (precisely the bad-day scenario the halt exists for), engine.dailyLoss can stay near 0 while real realized losses pile up in the DB, so the realized daily-loss auto-halt never trips. The engine keeps opening new positions past the configured daily loss limit.

**Suggested fix.** When reconcileBrokerSideExit / reconcilePendingTrades resolve a SELL to a realized loss that the engine did not already account for, add the negative pnl to engine.dailyLoss (guard against double-counting engine-initiated exits by tagging engine-placed broker_order_ids, or by only crediting rows whose signal indicates a broker-side/auto-reconciled exit).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The mechanism is exactly as described and I confirmed every load-bearing claim by reading the code. (1) The realized daily-loss halt reads ONLY the in-memory accumulator: enforceDailyLossHalt gates on `if (engine.dailyLoss > -dailyLossThreshold) return false` (line 1366), and it is the sole realized-loss gate — canPlaceBuyOrder (line 888) enforces earnings/sector/reentry/wash-sale/notional/rate-limit but NOT daily loss, so blocking new buys past the daily limit depends entirely on enforceDailyLossHalt firing, which runs at each scan top (3722, 4048, 4743). (2) engine.dailyLoss is incremented at exactly two sites, both immediately after an engine-placed sell via placeEngineOrder: runExitCheck line 2870 and runScan line 5190 (`engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0`). A full grep of `.dailyLoss` shows the only other writes are resets (1065, 5888) and snapshot hydration (6032) — no broker-side path adds to it. (3) When a position closes broker-side, syncPositionMapFromBroker (3270-3293) writes an ENGINE_POSITION_DISAPPEARED audit, calls reconcileBrokerSideExit (fire-and-forget), and deletes the position from the map — it never touches engine.dailyLoss. reconcileBrokerSideExit (3401) and reconcilePendingTrades (3512) write the realized pnl ONLY to trader_trades in the DB. So a broker-side/manual/protective-stop fill realizes a loss that never reaches the realized halt's counter, and simultaneously leaves totalUnrealizedPnl when the position is removed from the map. The MTM unrealized halt (enforceUnrealizedLossHalt, 1471, scan-end, using engine.dailyLoss + totalUnrealizedPnl at 1.5× threshold) is a genuine but partial backstop: it covers the bleed only while the position is still open and tracked; the moment the broker stop fires, the loss exits the unrealized total and never enters the realized counter, so the MTM total falls back toward zero exactly when the loss becomes permanent. This is the precise gap the finding names. Severity stays P2, not higher: engine-initiated exits (the common 1-min/15-min exit path) DO count, and the MTM halt catches the worst in-progress bleed days, so full bypass requires the specific scenario where broker-side fills dominate the day's selling. Not lower than P2 because it defeats a money-protecting auto-halt under exactly the bad-day scenario it exists for, in a live-trading path.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:1366 (enforceDailyLossHalt reads only engine.dailyLoss); :2870 and :5190 (only two increment sites, both right after placeEngineOrder engine-placed sells); :1065,:5888,:6032 (other writes are resets/hydration only); :3270-3293 (syncPositionMapFromBroker on broker-side disappearance: audit + reconcileBrokerSideExit + positionMap.delete, no dailyLoss update); :3401-3489 (reconcileBrokerSideExit inserts pnl into traderTrades DB only); :3512-3663 (reconcilePendingTrades updates traderTrades.pnl DB only); :888-948 (canPlaceBuyOrder has no daily-loss check); :1471 (enforceUnrealizedLossHalt uses engine.dailyLoss + totalUnrealizedPnl); :5687-5699 (totalUnrealizedPnl summed only over surviving positionMap entries, then enforceUnrealizedLossHalt — once a position is removed at 3293 its realized loss is in neither total)

### 27. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Partially-filled orders are stuck at PARTIAL_FILLED forever and dropped from realized P&L / tax

- **Lane:** engine-scan-concurrency · **Category:** data · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3524-3535, 3596-3606, 3622-3630`

**Description.** reconcilePendingTrades only selects rows WHERE status = 'PENDING' (line 3530). When a broker order is partially_filled it is updated to status='PARTIAL_FILLED' (line 3597) with the partial fill price and a pnl scaled to the partial filled qty (3601-3605). Because the row is no longer PENDING, it is never re-selected by reconcile, so when the order subsequently fully fills the final fill price and the P&L on the remaining shares are never captured. The Tax Center / 8949 routes filter on status = 'FILLED' exactly (src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:75, src/app/api/tax/form8949/route.ts:94), so a PARTIAL_FILLED row is excluded entirely from realized P&L and tax reporting.

**Impact.** Any order that goes through a partially_filled state before fully filling permanently records an understated P&L (only the partial slice) and is omitted from realized-gains and Form 8949 tax output. Silent under-reporting of gains/losses; tax figures wrong.

**Suggested fix.** Include PARTIAL_FILLED rows in the reconcile query (status IN ('PENDING','PARTIAL_FILLED')) so they re-reconcile to FILLED on completion, OR keep the placeholder PENDING until the order reaches a terminal state. Ensure the final FILLED row carries the full-quantity fill price and pnl.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The described mechanism is present and behaves exactly as claimed. reconcilePendingTrades selects only rows WHERE status = 'PENDING' (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3530), so once a row is updated to 'PARTIAL_FILLED' (line 3597) on a partially_filled broker state, it permanently falls out of the reconcile selection. I confirmed PARTIAL_FILLED is written in exactly one place (line 3597) and read by NO other code path (grep for PARTIAL_FILLED returns only the doc comment at 3503 and the assignment at 3597) — there is no separate sweep that promotes PARTIAL_FILLED to FILLED. Every engine order is logged as 'PENDING' at submission (logTrade calls at 2882, 3863, 3926, 4172, 4306, 4456, 4491, 4561, etc.), and reconcile is the only path that resolves them. So a SELL that the broker reports as partially_filled during one reconcile cycle, then fully fills later, is orphaned at PARTIAL_FILLED with pnl scaled only to the partial slice (lines 3601-3605), and the final-fill price/remaining-share P&L are never captured. Both tax routes filter status = 'FILLED' exactly (tax/report/route.ts:75, tax/form8949/route.ts:94) with no PARTIAL_FILLED fallback (verified the report route has a single status filter and no other status branch), so PARTIAL_FILLED rows are dropped from realized gains and Form 8949 — silent, permanent under-reporting. The data-integrity and tax-correctness claims are accurate. Severity stays P2 rather than higher because realistic frequency is bounded: engine exits (the P&L-bearing SELL leg) are market orders (placeEngineOrder type:'market' at 2867/3862/4171/4456) that fill near-atomically in liquid names and rarely dwell in partially_filled long enough for a reconcile cycle to observe the transient state; the larger partial-fill exposure is the limit BUY entries, which carry pnl=null and affect cost basis rather than realized P&L. Real, silent, permanent when triggered, but not high-frequency — P2 is correct. Proposed fix (status IN ('PENDING','PARTIAL_FILLED')) is sound.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3530 (reconcile selects only status='PENDING'); :3596-3606 (partially_filled → status='PARTIAL_FILLED', pnl scaled to filledQty only); :3621-3630 (single UPDATE keyed by row.id, no re-enqueue); grep PARTIAL_FILLED → only :3503 (doc) and :3597 (sole writer), zero readers; :2882/:3862/:3863/:3926/:4172/:4306 (all engine fills logged as 'PENDING'); src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:75 and src/app/api/tax/form8949/route.ts:94 (status='FILLED' exact filter, no PARTIAL_FILLED branch); src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:51 (status default 'PENDING')

### 28. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Orphan scan can place one extra order after being superseded (gate-check / record-placement straddle an await)

- **Lane:** engine-scan-concurrency · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5405-5441, 988-1000, 1002-1016, 1022-1027, 6162-6201`

**Description.** When the 10-min STALE_SCAN override fires (runScanGuarded, 6162), the previous scan promise is abandoned but keeps running; the new scan bumps engine.scanGeneration so throwIfScanCancelled eventually kills the orphan. However throwIfScanCancelled only runs at the top of each loop iteration (line 4945) and before swap-sell (5520). Inside one iteration the sequence is: gate via canPlaceBuyOrder (5405, which reads engine.dailyNotional and engine.recentOrderTimestamps), then `await placeEngineOrder` (5432), then recordOrderPlacement (5440, which mutates dailyNotional + recentOrderTimestamps). The cancellation check is NOT re-evaluated between the gate and the order, so a superseded orphan that is already inside an iteration places exactly one more real order, and the daily-notional/rate-limit counters it shares with the live scan are checked-then-incremented across the placeEngineOrder await — a check-then-act TOCTOU on shared engine counters. This is the concurrency hazard the code comments at 6150-6177 explicitly mark as 'deferred to a focused concurrency PR'.

**Impact.** After a wedged-scan override, the orphan and the new scan run concurrently sharing engine.dailyNotional and the rate-limit window; both can pass the notional cap / rate-limit check before either increments, so the day's notional cap and the 30-orders/60s live-trading rate limit can be exceeded, and an extra unintended order can be placed.

**Suggested fix.** Re-check throwIfScanCancelled immediately before placeEngineOrder in every buy/sell branch, and claim the rate-limit slot / notional synchronously at gate time (increment recentOrderTimestamps + dailyNotional before the await, roll back on placement failure) rather than after placeEngineOrder resolves.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> Every element of the finding is verified in the actual code. In runScanInner's buy branch the sequence is exactly as claimed: line 5405 `await canPlaceBuyOrder(...)`, line 5432 `await placeEngineOrder(...)`, line 5440 `recordOrderPlacement(engine, "buy", buyNotional)`. canPlaceBuyOrder READS the shared counters (engine.dailyNotional at line 989, engine.recentOrderTimestamps at lines 1005-1006), and recordOrderPlacement MUTATES those same counters AFTER the placeEngineOrder await (recentOrderTimestamps.push at 1023, dailyNotional += at 1025). This is a genuine check-then-act TOCTOU across an await on shared engine state. The per-iteration cooperative cancellation check (throwIfScanCancelled at line 4945) sits at the TOP of each symbol iteration; the next check is at line 5520 (before swap-sell). A full grep of throwIfScanCancelled confirms NO check exists between 4945 and 5520 — so within the symbol iteration the orphan is processing when superseded, it passes the gate and places exactly one real order before throwing at the next iteration's 4945. The orphan/override mechanism is real: runScanGuarded (6162) fires a fresh scan when scanInFlight age exceeds STALE_SCAN_MS (10 min, line 6161/6165) while the old promise keeps running; runScan bumps engine.scanGeneration (line 4649) and the orphan's captured myGeneration no longer matches, so throwIfScanCancelled (87-91) eventually kills it — but only at yield points, not between the gate and the order. The in-code comments at 56-72 and 6150-6177 explicitly acknowledge this exact race ("racing on engine state ... dailyNotional, broker order placement") and mark it "deferred to a focused concurrency PR." Impact is real but appropriately bounded as the finding states: one extra order after a wedged-scan override, and a single-order overshoot of the 30-orders/60s live rate limit (ORDER_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN=30, line 716) and the daily notional cap. It is NOT mischaracterized — the finding correctly scopes it to "exactly one more real order," not an unbounded breach. Severity P2 is correct: a genuine concurrency hazard on the live order-placement (money-moving) path, but gated behind a rare precondition (a scan wedged >10 min; comment notes 95th-pct scan ~30s, worst ~90s) and bounded to a one-order blast radius. Not P1 (not routinely triggerable, limited overshoot), not below P2 (it is a real live-order TOCTOU that can place an unintended order and exceed a risk gate).

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5405 (await canPlaceBuyOrder gate), :5432 (await placeEngineOrder), :5440 (recordOrderPlacement after the await); :989 + :1005-1006 (canPlaceBuyOrder READS engine.dailyNotional / engine.recentOrderTimestamps); :1022-1027 (recordOrderPlacement MUTATES recentOrderTimestamps.push + dailyNotional +=); :4945 and :5520 are the only throwIfScanCancelled checks bracketing the loop body (grep shows none between them); :87-91 (throwIfScanCancelled throws on generation mismatch); :4649 (++engine.scanGeneration); :6162-6178 (runScanGuarded override at STALE_SCAN_MS=10min, :6161/:6165) with the deferred-PR comment; :56-72 (cancellation-model comment naming dailyNotional/order-placement as the raced state); :716 (ORDER_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN=30).

### 29. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Exit P&L uses analysis/quote price as actual fill, so engine.dailyLoss and consecutive-loss halt run on a fabricated price

- **Lane:** engine-scan-concurrency · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5179-5198, 2867-2882, 3586-3595`

**Description.** On an engine-placed market SELL, pnl is computed as (currentPrice - entryPrice) * qty where currentPrice is the analyzeHybrid price (yesterday's daily close in swing modes — line 4957 / analysis.price) or the 1-min fetchQuote price (runExitCheck 2869), NOT the broker fill. That placeholder pnl immediately drives engine.dailyLoss += pnl (5190 / 2870) and recordTradeResult → consecutive-loss halt (5193 / 2874). reconcilePendingTrades later corrects the DB row's pnl via delta math (3593: newPnl = row.pnl + (actualFill - placeholderFill)*qty), but it never corrects engine.dailyLoss or engine.consecutiveLosses. In swing mode the placeholder is a full day stale, so on a gap-down market sell the in-memory realized loss can be materially smaller (or a loss recorded as a gain) than the true fill.

**Impact.** The realized daily-loss halt and the consecutive-loss halt key off a stale/fabricated exit price. A trade that actually filled at a loss can be recorded in-memory as a smaller loss or a win, resetting consecutiveLosses and under-counting dailyLoss — both safeguards can be delayed or skipped relative to true fills.

**Suggested fix.** Compute exit pnl from the broker fill (poll the order or use reconcile's corrected value) before mutating engine.dailyLoss / consecutiveLosses, or have reconcilePendingTrades apply the corrected delta back into engine.dailyLoss and re-evaluate the consecutive-loss counter.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The structural defect is real and present exactly as described. In runScan's exit path (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5179-5198), the engine places a market SELL via placeEngineOrder, captures the returned BrokerOrder only for .id, then computes pnl = (currentPrice - heldPosition.entryPrice) * sellQty (5188) where currentPrice = analysis.price = lastBar.close (4957; analyzer.ts:324). That placeholder pnl immediately drives engine.dailyLoss += pnl<0?pnl:0 (5190) and recordTradeResult -> consecutive-loss halt (5193). The 1-min runExitCheck path (2867-2882) does the same with currentPrice from fetchQuote (2760). reconcilePendingTrades corrects only the DB row's pnl via delta math (3593-3594) and writes it to traderTrades (3628); it never writes back to engine.dailyLoss or engine.consecutiveLosses, and no other path recomputes those counters from the broker-fill-corrected DB values (only resets at 1065/5888 and snapshot rehydration at 6032/6049, which restores the same placeholder accumulation). The realized daily-loss halt (checkDailyLoss:1366), MTM-drawdown halt (1471), and consecutive-loss halt (recordTradeResult:1040) all gate on these never-corrected in-memory counters. So a safeguard does key off an unreconciled exit price. The finding overstates magnitude in one respect: Yahoo's interval=1d fetch with period2=now returns the current in-progress daily bar during market hours, so analysis.price is ~live (cached up to 15 min via BAR_CACHE_MAX_AGE_OPEN_MS), not literally yesterday's close; runExitCheck uses an even fresher fetchQuote. In normal operation the placeholder-vs-fill gap is quote/cache-to-fill slippage, not a full trading day, so the sign-flip / halt-skip outcome is an edge case (fast gap during the cache window) rather than routine. The defect is genuine in safety-critical halt logic but bounded in magnitude — P2 is correctly calibrated, neither inflated nor a false positive.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5179-5198 (placeEngineOrder result used only for .id; pnl from currentPrice; dailyLoss+= at 5190; recordTradeResult at 5193); 4957 (currentPrice = analysis.price); src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:324 (price = lastBar.close); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2867-2882 (1-min path: pnl at 2869, dailyLoss+= 2870, recordTradeResult 2874) with currentPrice from fetchQuote at 2757-2760; 3582-3595,3628 (reconcile corrects only DB row pnl, no engine-counter writeback); checkDailyLoss gate at 1366; recordTradeResult at 1033-1041; MTM gate at 1471; engine.dailyLoss only mutated at 1065/5888/6032 and 5190/2870; src/lib/market-data.ts:33 (BAR_CACHE_MAX_AGE_OPEN_MS = 15 min), 96-99 (period2=now, interval=1d), 156-170 (fetchQuote regularMarketPrice)

### 30. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Stop-sync contract test asserts a 6-hour hung scan is skipped — contradicts the STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS recovery it's meant to verify

- **Lane:** engine-snapshot-watchdog · **Category:** correctness · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:43-46, 133-146`

**Description.** The test's checkSyncEligibility() returns { ran:false, reason:'scan_in_flight' } whenever engine.scanStartedAt is set, with NO age check (lines 43-46). The 'a hung scan (scanStartedAt set for hours) still reports scan_in_flight' test (lines 133-146) sets scanStartedAt to 6 hours ago and asserts the sync is SKIPPED. But the real syncBrokerStopsForUser (trading-engine.ts 7421-7443) implements STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS = 10 min: when scanAgeMs >= 10 min it falls THROUGH and RUNS syncBrokerStops anyway (it logs 'Stop-sync running past stale-scan override'). CLAUDE.md and docs/changelog.html (P0 #2) both document this hung-scan recovery as the whole point of the PR. So for a 6-hour-old scan production RUNS, the test asserts SKIP. The test's own comment even narrates the override but its mirror omits it. This is the highest-value gating logic in the module and the test pins the pre-fix (broken) behavior.

**Impact.** The exact recovery path that resolves the original incident (a chronically hung tactical-smart scan freezing broker stops all session) is 'verified' by a test that asserts it stays frozen. A regression that drops the 10-min override (reverting to the bug) would pass CI, and broker stops would silently freeze for the whole session again — positions look protected on the dashboard but their Alpaca stops are stale, the precise 9-hour-stale-stop loss event the PR was built to prevent.

**Suggested fix.** Add the scanAgeMs vs STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS branch to checkSyncEligibility(): if scanStartedAt age >= 10 min, return { ran: true }. Change the 6-hour test to assert { ran: true }, and add a separate test for a fresh (<10 min) scan asserting scan_in_flight. Or test the real function directly to eliminate mirror drift.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is factually correct in every particular. The test's mirror function checkSyncEligibility (tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:43-46) returns { ran:false, reason:'scan_in_flight' } for ANY non-null scanStartedAt with no age check. The real syncBrokerStopsForUser (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7421-7445) does the opposite: it computes scanAgeMs, returns scan_in_flight ONLY when scanAgeMs < STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS (10*60*1000), and for older scans falls through, logs 'Stop-sync running past stale-scan override', calls syncBrokerStops(userId), and returns { ran:true }. The 6-hour test (lines 133-146) sets scanStartedAt to Date.now() - 6*60*60*1000 and asserts { ran:false, reason:'scan_in_flight' } — but production returns { ran:true } for that input. So the test pins the pre-fix (broken) behavior, exactly inverting the recovery the PR was built to provide. The override is documented as the load-bearing fix in CLAUDE.md:70, docs/ENGINE_RULESET.md:472, and public/docs/changelog.html:403 (P0 #2). The scheduler (stop-sync-scheduler.ts:63-70) genuinely skips the broker call when ran:false, confirming the stale-stop-freeze failure mode is real. The test even narrates the override in its comment ('The 10-min override... clears scanStartedAt eventually') while the mirror omits it — clear drift. I could not refute any element of the claim. I downgrade severity to P2 only because this is a test-only defect: production code is correct, no user is harmed today, and the harm is purely future regression-masking / false CI confidence on a critical path. P1 implies a live correctness defect; this is a broken contract test that would let a real regression slip through — serious but a notch below an active production bug.

**Evidence:** tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:43-46 (mirror has no age check: `if (engine.scanStartedAt) return { ran:false, reason:'scan_in_flight' }`); tests/unit/stop-sync-scheduler.test.ts:133-146 (6-hour scan asserts `{ ran:false, reason:'scan_in_flight' }`); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7421-7445 (real: `scanAgeMs = Date.now() - engine.scanStartedAt.getTime()`, `STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS = 10*60*1000`, `if (scanAgeMs < STALE_SCAN_OVERRIDE_MS) return { ran:false, reason:'scan_in_flight' }`, else falls through to `await syncBrokerStops(userId); return { ran:true }`); src/lib/stop-sync-scheduler.ts:65-69 (ran:false => sync skipped); CLAUDE.md:70, docs/ENGINE_RULESET.md:472, public/docs/changelog.html:403 (override documented as the PR's purpose).

### 31. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Swap-sell redeploy checks buying power against the scan-start snapshot, ignoring buying power already consumed by in-loop buys this scan

- **Lane:** engine-swap-graduation · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4715-4723, 5337-5343, 5540-5561`

**Description.** `account` (and account.buyingPower) is fetched once at runScanInner start (line 4715) and never refreshed. The in-loop entry path consumes buying power on each placed BUY but does not decrement account.buyingPower. The post-loop swap-sell planner is then fed `buyingPower: account.buyingPower` (line 5558) — the stale scan-start value — so its insufficient_buying_power gate (planner line 2108) under-counts already-committed capital. currentExposure is correctly recomputed from positionMap (line 5537), so the exposure cap is fine, but the buying-power gate is not. The planner can green-light redeploy BUYs that exceed actual remaining buying power.

**Impact.** Swap-sell can attempt redeploy orders the broker rejects for insufficient buying power (caught by the try/catch at 5655, so no crash), producing failed orders, audit/log noise, and wasted order-rate-limit budget. In a margin account it could also over-commit relative to the user's intended cash-on-hand if the broker honors it on margin.

**Suggested fix.** Track committed notional during the scan (decrement a running buyingPower as each in-loop BUY is placed via recordOrderPlacement, or re-fetch account before the swap-sell block) and pass the residual buying power into the planner.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> Every code-level claim in the finding is accurate. `account` is fetched once at runScanInner start (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4715) and never refreshed within the scan — the only later getAccount() call (4822) writes a separate local `currentAccount` used solely for account-switch/equity-collapse detection, never reassigning `account`. The in-loop entry path checks `orderCost > account.buyingPower` (5339) but on a successful buy calls only `recordOrderPlacement` (5440), which (1022-1027) appends a timestamp and increments `engine.dailyNotional` — it does NOT decrement any buying-power figure. The post-loop swap-sell block then feeds the planner `buyingPower: account.buyingPower` (5558) — the unchanged scan-start snapshot. Inside planSwapSellRedeploy the insufficient_buying_power gate (2108) compares each candidate's orderCost to the static input.buyingPower and never decrements a running buying-power total across its own multiple attempts, so even within one plan multiple redeploys each test against the full scan-start figure. The finding's contrast claim is also correct: currentExposure is recomputed from positionMap (5537) and the planner accumulates runningExposure (2083, 2122), so the exposure cap correctly accounts for committed capital, while the buying-power gate does not. Impact is real but bounded, matching the finding's own claimed-impact text. The swap-sell block only runs when exitsThisScan>0 (5528) — i.e. positions were just sold, freeing cash — so the stale snapshot frequently understates current buying power on the exit path; the genuine over-count comes from in-loop buys placed earlier in the same scan that aren't reflected. Worst case is broker-rejected redeploy orders for insufficient buying power, caught by the try/catch at 5655 (no crash), producing failed-order audit/log noise and wasted order-rate-limit budget; on margin the over-commit is still bounded by the correctly-implemented exposure cap (default 1.5x equity) plus the per-attempt canPlaceBuyOrder re-gate (5605) and notional cap. No crash, no cross-tenant leak, no money-math display bug. This is a legitimate low-impact correctness/hygiene gap — P2 is defensible (arguably P3 given the exit-freed-cash mitigation and exposure-cap backstop), so I leave severity at P2 rather than inflating or deflating.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4715 (account = await client.getAccount(), single fetch); 4822 (separate currentAccount, detection-only); 5339-5343 (in-loop buyingPower gate); 1022-1027 (recordOrderPlacement does not touch buyingPower); 5440 + 5626 (recordOrderPlacement calls after buys); 5537-5538 (currentExposure recomputed — correct); 5558 (stale account.buyingPower passed to planner); 2083/2108/2122 (planner: runningExposure cumulative but buyingPower static, no running decrement); brokers.ts:18,277 (buyingPower is real BrokerAccount field from Alpaca buying_power)
