### 32. ✅ FIXED · [P2] AI scoring layer can promote a HOLD into BUY/STRONG_BUY, fabricating a trade signal from nothing

- **Lane:** hybrid-ai · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/hybrid/ai-scoring-layer.ts:30, 40-45, 177-192`

**Description.** isDirectionFlip() only blocks buyish<->sellish transitions (BUY->SELL, SELL->BUY). It returns false when the original signal is HOLD, because HOLD is neither buyish nor sellish. The SYSTEM_PROMPT (line 30) likewise only forbids 'BUY->SELL or SELL->BUY'. In pipeline.ts the signal handed to the AI layer is the already-reevaluated adjustedSignal (pipeline.ts line 130/146-149): a technical BUY whose adjusted confidence fell below 0.40 is downgraded to HOLD by reevaluateSignal, then passed to the AI layer as HOLD. The LLM can return signal='STRONG_BUY'; isDirectionFlip('HOLD','STRONG_BUY')===false, so the raw LLM signal is accepted (line 188) and overwrites the pipeline signal (pipeline.ts line 159). The engine buys purely on signal === BUY/STRONG_BUY (trading-engine.ts line 5262), with no independent confidence floor at entry, and a STRONG_BUY additionally bypasses the position cap by 50% (line 5267-5268) and gets swap-sell redeploy priority.

**Impact.** A symbol the deterministic technical engine declined (HOLD) can be turned into a live STRONG_BUY by a single LLM response, placing a real bracket order — and even exceeding the user's max-position cap. The LLM-decoupled confidence clamp (+/-0.20) does not constrain the signal class, so the safety rule ('cannot flip direction') is unenforced for the most dangerous transition: no-position -> aggressive buy.

**Suggested fix.** Treat HOLD as a protected origin: in isDirectionFlip (or a new guard) block HOLD->buyish and HOLD->sellish, OR cap the AI-adjusted signal so it can only down-rank, never introduce a directional signal the technical layer didn't already produce. Re-run reevaluateSignal(adjustedSignal, adjustedConfidence) after AI scoring so signal and confidence stay coupled.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The described code defect is real and present exactly as claimed. (1) isDirectionFlip (ai-scoring-layer.ts:40-45) only blocks buyish<->sellish via isBuyish/isSellish; HOLD is neither, so a HOLD->STRONG_BUY adjustment is NOT blocked at line 189-191. The SYSTEM_PROMPT (line 30) likewise only forbids BUY->SELL/SELL->BUY. (2) pipeline.ts confirms the data flow: line 130 reevaluateSignal downgrades a technical BUY with adjustedConfidence<0.40 to HOLD; line 146-149 passes that HOLD as the AI layer input; line 159 overwrites adjustedSignal with aiScoring.adjustedSignal raw. reevaluateSignal is called exactly once (only at line 130, never re-run after AI scoring), so signal and confidence decouple — even though the AI ±0.20 clamp keeps confidence below ~0.60, the signal CLASS can become STRONG_BUY. (3) The engine entry (trading-engine.ts:5262) keys purely on signal===BUY/STRONG_BUY with no confidence floor anywhere in the entry block (5300-5405 gate only qty/buying-power/exposure/cooldown/drift/canPlaceBuyOrder); a STRONG_BUY additionally raises the position cap by 50% (5266-5268) and gets swap-sell defer priority (5281-5289). So the full chain (HOLD technically declined -> LLM STRONG_BUY -> real bracket order exceeding the position cap) is logically valid. The severity is reduced from P1 to P2 because the AI scoring layer is OFF by default: HYBRID_CONFIG.aiScoringEnabled = process.env.HYBRID_AI_SCORING_ENABLED === "true" (config.ts:128), the var is absent from .env.example and any deploy config, and the engine only ever sets enableAiScoring:false (never force-on; trading-engine.ts:4787-4793). The in-code comment at 4784 ("HYBRID_CONFIG which has AI scoring on") is stale/incorrect. The flow is also driven by an internal admin-keyed LLM, not directly attacker-controlled input. It is a genuine dormant safety defect that fires the moment the documented Premium AI feature flag is enabled — real, but currently latent in default deployments, hence P2 not P1.

**Evidence:** src/lib/hybrid/ai-scoring-layer.ts:32-45 (isBuyish/isSellish/isDirectionFlip leave HOLD unprotected); :30 (SYSTEM_PROMPT only forbids BUY<->SELL); :188-191 (flip-block no-ops for HOLD origin). src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:130 (reevaluateSignal downgrades BUY->HOLD at conf<0.40, only call site); :146-149 (AI layer fed adjustedSignal); :159 (adjustedSignal = aiScoring.adjustedSignal, no re-coupling). src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5262 (shouldBuy on signal class only); :5266-5268 (STRONG_BUY positionCap = maxPositions*1.5); :5300-5405 (entry gates contain no confidence floor). Mitigation: src/lib/config.ts:128 (aiScoringEnabled defaults false); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4787-4793 (engine only ever overrides enableAiScoring to false, never on); HYBRID_AI_SCORING_ENABLED absent from .env.example and deploy config.

### 33. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Sentiment heuristic uses substring matching, mislabeling unrelated headlines and skewing the confidence adjustment

- **Lane:** hybrid-ai · **Category:** logic · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:163-183, 195-216`

**Description.** scoreSentimentHeuristic lowercases all headlines into one string and tests `joined.includes(word)` for each keyword (lines 169-176) with no word boundary. Bullish term 'high' matches 'highlight'/'higher'; 'gain' matches 'against'; 'rise' matches 'comprise'; 'low' (bearish) matches 'below'/'follow'/'flow'/'allow'; 'cut' (bearish) matches 'circuit'/'execute'. It also counts distinct keyword *presence* (bullCount/bearCount incremented once per term regardless of frequency), not headline-weighted occurrences. The resulting bullishPercent-bearishPercent delta feeds a +/-0.15 confidence adjustment (computeAdjustment lines 201/208) that can move a BUY's confidence enough to trip the reevaluateSignal STRONG_BUY (>0.75) or HOLD (<0.40) thresholds.

**Impact.** Spurious bullish/bearish classification from incidental substrings biases the sentiment confidence adjustment in a way that can up/down-rank a signal class, with no relation to actual news tone. Note the repo already ships a correct word-boundary classifier (headline-sentiment.ts SINGLE_WORD_RE + PHRASE_TERMS) that this layer does not use.

**Suggested fix.** Replace substring includes() with the word-boundary regex approach already implemented in headline-sentiment.ts (reuse scoreHeadline or its SINGLE_WORD_RE), and weight by per-headline occurrences rather than term presence.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. scoreSentimentHeuristic (sentiment-layer.ts:166-183) joins all headlines into one lowercased string and tests bare joined.includes(word) with no word boundary, incrementing bullCount/bearCount once per term-presence (lines 172/175), not by occurrence frequency. The keyword lists (lines 163-164) contain short substrings that match inside unrelated words: bearish 'low' matches 'below'/'below estimates'/'follow'/'cash flow'/'allow' (all high-frequency in finance headlines), bullish 'high' matches 'higher'/'highlights', bearish 'cut' matches 'execute', bullish 'gain' matches 'against', bullish 'rise' matches 'enterprise'/'comprise'. These confounders are common, not contrived. The impact is real and reachable, and it is NOT a rare fallback: scoreSentimentWithAI (lines 103-159) is dead code — never called anywhere (Grep confirms only the heuristic is invoked at line 79). So the buggy heuristic is the SOLE scoring path. The result feeds computeAdjustment (lines 201/208): adjustment = sentimentDelta * 0.15 * strength, where strength = min(newsScore,1)*min(headlineCount/5,1) ≈ 0.7 with ≥5 headlines, giving up to ~±0.105 confidence shift. pipeline.ts:117 adds sentiment.adjustment to adjustedConfidence, then reevaluateSignal (pipeline.ts:21-25) applies the >0.75 STRONG_BUY and <0.40 HOLD thresholds. analyzer.ts:244 puts a plain BUY in [0.55,0.85], so a BUY at ~0.65-0.74 can be spuriously up-ranked to STRONG_BUY by an incidental-substring bullish delta. The layer is enabled by default (config.ts:125 sentimentEnabled defaults true; no prod override) and runs in the live engine scan (trading-engine.ts:4956; hybridOpts at 4787-4793 only toggles enableAiScoring, never sentiment). The note about the existing word-boundary classifier is accurate: headline-sentiment.ts ships SINGLE_WORD_RE (lines 69-76) + PHRASE_TERMS (line 67), and its own comment (lines 10-13) states the hybrid layer 'powers signal-adjustment math where accuracy matters' — yet that layer uses the buggy substring matcher instead. Severity stays P2 (not inflated, not deflated): genuine logic bug with a reachable signal-class effect, but bounded — a single ±0.105 adjustment among several layers, it re-ranks within a direction rather than flipping direction, and the HOLD downgrade is hard to reach for a BUY (floor 0.55 − 0.105 = 0.445 > 0.40 threshold). The STRONG_BUY up-rank is the clearly reachable path.

**Evidence:** src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:163-164 (short-substring keyword lists), :169-176 (joined.includes(word) substring match, presence-counted), :78-83 (heuristic is the only scoring call), :103-159 (scoreSentimentWithAI defined but never called — dead code, confirmed by Grep), :196 (strength≈0.7), :201/:208/:216 (±0.15*strength adjustment, clamp); src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:117 (adjustedConfidence += sentiment.adjustment), :21-25/:130 (reevaluateSignal 0.75/0.40 thresholds); src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:244 (BUY confidence 0.55-0.85); src/lib/config.ts:125 (sentimentEnabled default true); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4956 + 4787-4793 (engine runs analyzeHybrid with sentiment never disabled); src/lib/headline-sentiment.ts:67,69-76,82-106 (correct word-boundary classifier the layer fails to reuse), :10-13 (comment asserting the hybrid layer is the accuracy-critical one).

### 34. ✅ FIXED · [P2] VWAP never resets per session and is cumulated across the entire 90-day daily window, making 'Price vs VWAP' meaningless

- **Lane:** indicators · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/indicators/vwap.ts:17-32`

**Description.** VWAP is anchored — it must reset at the start of each trading session. Here a fresh VWAP is created per analyzeBars call (analyzer.ts:55, createIndicators) and fed ALL bars with no daily reset (vwap.ts has reset() but it is never called from analyzer.ts). In the engine's primary path the bars are 90 DAILY bars (trading-engine.ts:743 BARS_FOR_ANALYSIS=90), so 'VWAP' becomes a 90-day volume-weighted average price — not a VWAP at all. For the 5m path it cumulates across ~5 sessions instead of resetting daily. The resulting value is then used as a hard scoring input: 'Price above/below VWAP' adds a full point to bullScore/bearScore (analyzer.ts:122-130) and feeds STRONG_BUY/STRONG_SELL thresholds.

**Impact.** A core scoring component is computed on a quantity that does not represent VWAP. On daily data it systematically biases the price-vs-VWAP vote (long-run VWMA sits far below current price in any uptrend, near-always voting bullish), inflating BUY scores and distorting both live signals and GA fitness.

**Suggested fix.** Either reset VWAP at each session boundary (detect date change in the bar stream and call reset()), or — since VWAP is intrinsically intraday — exclude the VWAP vote from scoring on daily-bar analyses and only use it on intraday (5m) windows that are anchored to the current session.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding's mechanics are fully verified. VWAP (src/lib/indicators/vwap.ts:17-23) accumulates cumulativeTPV/cumulativeVolume monotonically with no per-session reset; value() = cumulativeTPV/cumulativeVolume over EVERY bar fed since construction. analyzer.ts constructs a fresh VWAP per call (createIndicators, line 55) and the bar loop (lines 284-321 in analyzeBars, lines 393-408 in analyzeSignalOnly) calls ind.vwap.update(bar) on every bar with NO reset() and NO date-change detection. A Grep for .reset() across the indicators dir returns only MACD's internal EMA resets — VWAP.reset() (lines 34-39) is dead code, never invoked from any analyzer/backtester/optimizer path. The primary engine scan path is daily: runScan defaults barResolution="1d" (trading-engine.ts:4642, set "1d" at 6108), days = BARS_FOR_ANALYSIS = 90 (line 743, used at 4948), fetchBars(symbol, 90, "1d") → analyzeHybrid → analyzeBars (pipeline.ts:47 passes bars straight through). So the quantity labeled "VWAP" is a 90-trading-day cumulative volume-weighted average price (a VWMA), not a session-anchored VWAP. On the 5m path it spans ~5 sessions (days=5) rather than resetting at each session open. That value drives a real scoring vote: evaluateSignal lines 122-130 add exactly +1 to bullScore (price>vwap) or bearScore (price<vwap), and bullScore feeds the STRONG_BUY threshold (bullScore>=4 && bullScore>bearScore+2, line 237) and the symmetric STRONG_SELL (line 246). The directional-bias claim holds: a 90-bar VWMA sits below current price through any sustained uptrend, so the vote is near-always +1 bull in uptrends and +1 bear in downtrends — a persistent, label-misrepresenting bias rather than a genuine intraday mean-reversion signal. The same defect propagates to GA fitness (backtester.ts:309/343/385 and optimizer.ts:478 all call analyzeBars; no vwap reset anywhere). Severity stays P2 (not elevated): the biased vote is heavily correlated with the existing 'Price above 20-period SMA' (lines 144-152) and EMA-trend votes, so it largely duplicates trend information already counted rather than injecting an independent error; it is one vote among ~7, and it does not corrupt position sizing, stops, or money math. It degrades signal-distribution accuracy and the meaning of the displayed 'VWAP' indicator, which is a real logic defect worth fixing, but not P0/P1.

**Evidence:** src/lib/indicators/vwap.ts:17-32 (cumulative update, value=cumTPV/cumVol, no internal reset); vwap.ts:34-39 (reset() exists, unused); src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:55 (new VWAP() per createIndicators); analyzer.ts:291 & 400 (ind.vwap.update(bar) inside bar loops, no reset); analyzer.ts:122-130 (price>vwap → +1 bull / else +1 bear); analyzer.ts:237,246 (bullScore>=4 STRONG_BUY threshold); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:743 (BARS_FOR_ANALYSIS=90), 4948-4949 (days=90 for "1d", fetchBars), 6108 (barResolution="1d" default), 4956 (analyzeHybrid); src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:47 (analyzeBars passthrough); Grep .reset() in src/lib/indicators → only macd.ts:70-72 (no VWAP reset); backtester.ts:309/343/385 + optimizer.ts:478 (GA path also uses analyzeBars, no reset).

### 35. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Sector-exposure cap mis-buckets ~30 tradeable S&P 500 symbols into a synthetic "Other" sector

- **Lane:** screener-sectors · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/sectors.ts:196-198 (getSymbolSector) vs sp500.ts:9-71 (FALLBACK_SYMBOLS / tradeable universe)`

**Description.** getSymbolSector() returns "Other" for any ticker not in the symbolSectors map (sectors.ts:197 `?? "Other"`). The engine's actual tradeable universe is SCAN_UNIVERSE = getSP500Symbols() (trading-engine.ts:24, refreshed at 3697/4667), i.e. the sp500.ts list. That list contains many symbols that are ABSENT from sectors.ts: confirmed missing via Grep — BLK, WBA, AAL, AMP, MRO, PVH, QRVO, RHI, SJM, TECH, XRAY, BIO, CMA (and more: ACGL, DAY, FBHS, FISV, OGN, RE, ROL, SEE). The sector-exposure cap in canPlaceBuyOrder (trading-engine.ts:925-956) computes `newSector = getSymbolSector(symbol)` and sums market value of existing positions where `getSymbolSector(posSymbol) === newSector`. Because all unmapped names collapse to "Other", unrelated companies (BlackRock=Financials, Walgreens=Staples, Marathon Oil=Energy) are treated as ONE concentrated sector, while a genuinely concentrated pair of mapped same-sector names is evaluated correctly. Separately, every ETF (SPY, QQQ, XLF, XLE, UVXY, ...) maps to a single "ETF" bucket (sectors.ts:183-193), so broad-market SPY and leveraged-vol UVXY count as the same sector.

**Impact.** When a user enables the sector-exposure cap (maxSectorExposurePct > 0), diversified BUYs across distinct real sectors can be falsely blocked as 'over-concentrated' (all lumped in Other), and conversely real concentration in the Other/ETF bucket is mis-measured. Wrong trade gating: either missed entries or unguarded concentration. Default is 0/disabled (trading-engine.ts:779) so only opt-in users are affected.

**Suggested fix.** Make the sector map cover the full tradeable universe, or fail loud: assert at boot that every getSP500Symbols()/SCAN_UNIVERSE symbol resolves to a non-"Other" sector; add the missing S&P 500 names to symbolSectors. For ETFs, classify by underlying sector (XLF→Financials, XLE→Energy) rather than one "ETF" bucket, or exclude pure-ETF positions from per-sector concentration math.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate on every checkable point. (1) Code present and behaves as claimed: src/lib/sectors.ts:196-198 returns "Other" for any symbol absent from symbolSectors; src/lib/sectors.ts:185-193 maps every ETF (SPY, QQQ, XLF, XLE, UVXY, ...) to a single "ETF" bucket. (2) The sector-exposure cap in canPlaceBuyOrder (trading-engine.ts:933-955) computes newSector = getSymbolSector(symbol) and sums market value of existing positions where getSymbolSector(posSymbol) === newSector, exactly as described, then blocks the BUY when sectorPct > cap. (3) The cap is wired into all three real buy paths — tactical (line 3912), tactical-smart (lines 4292/4477/4547), and scan/optimized (line 5405) — each passing a sectorExposureContext built by buildSectorExposureContext (lines 849-863) from the live position map. (4) The tradeable universe is SCAN_UNIVERSE = SP500_SYMBOLS / getSP500Symbols() (trading-engine.ts:19,24), i.e. the sp500.ts list. (5) I independently confirmed via Grep that none of BLK, WBA, AAL, AMP, MRO, PVH, QRVO, RHI, SJM, TECH, XRAY, BIO, CMA, ACGL, DAY, FBHS, FISV, OGN, RE, ROL, SEE exist in sectors.ts, while all appear in sp500.ts FALLBACK_SYMBOLS — so a dozen-plus genuinely distinct-sector tradeable names (BlackRock=Financials, Walgreens=Staples, American Airlines=Industrials, Ameriprise=Financials, etc.) collapse into one synthetic "Other" sector. When a user enables the cap, that conflation can falsely block a diversified BUY across distinct real sectors and conversely under-measure true concentration within those unmapped sectors. The ETF lumping is a parallel correctness gap (broad SPY vs leveraged UVXY treated as the same sector). The only mild overstatement: a few of the cited "missing" tickers (FBHS, FISV, RE, OGN) are stale/delisted names that the daily Wikipedia refresh would drop from the live universe, so the live count of mis-bucketed current constituents is somewhat lower than the raw list — but well over a dozen real names remain, so the substance holds. Severity P2 is correct, not inflated: it is a genuine wrong-gating bug in a risk control, but it is opt-in (loadRiskLimits default maxSectorExposurePct: 0 = disabled, trading-engine.ts:779) and the failure mode is mis-measured concentration (missed entries or unguarded concentration), not direct capital loss or a security hole.

**Evidence:** src/lib/sectors.ts:196-198 (getSymbolSector ?? "Other"); src/lib/sectors.ts:185-193 (all ETFs → "ETF"); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:933-955 (cap groups by getSymbolSector equality, blocks on sectorPct > cap); trading-engine.ts:19,24 (SCAN_UNIVERSE = SP500_SYMBOLS); trading-engine.ts:849-863 (buildSectorExposureContext); trading-engine.ts:3912,4292,4477,4547,5405 (canPlaceBuyOrder call sites across tactical/tactical-smart/scan); trading-engine.ts:779 (maxSectorExposurePct default 0 = disabled); src/lib/sp500.ts:9-71 (FALLBACK_SYMBOLS contains BLK l16, WBA l67, AAL l11, AMP l13, MRO l47, PVH l54, QRVO l56, RHI l56, SJM l58, TECH l61, XRAY l69, BIO l16, CMA l20 — all confirmed ABSENT from sectors.ts via Grep "No matches found").

### 36. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Non-atomic daily-P&L accumulator in flatten loses realized P&L under concurrency

- **Lane:** security-auth-tenant · **Category:** data · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:180-192`

**Description.** The flatten handler updates trader_daily_pnl with a select-then-(insert|update): it reads the (date,userId) row, and if absent INSERTs, else UPDATEs realizedPnl = existing.realizedPnl + realizedPnl. trader_daily_pnl has a UNIQUE index on (date, userId) (schema/trader.ts:81). Two concurrent writers to the same day's row (two rapid flatten calls, or a flatten racing the in-process engine's own daily-PnL writer) both read 'no row', both attempt INSERT; the unique index rejects one. That rejection is swallowed by `catch { /* best effort */ }` (line 192), so the losing flatten's realized P&L delta and trade count are silently dropped. The UPDATE branch is also a lost-update read-modify-write (both read existing.realizedPnl, both add their own delta, last write wins). This is exactly the 'atomic upsert on accumulator rows' anti-pattern called out in the project guidance.

**Impact.** Daily realized P&L and trade-count shown on the trader dashboard and stored for the day are undercounted whenever flattens collide or race the engine; the day's accounting silently diverges from actual fills. No crash, but corrupted financial display/record.

**Suggested fix.** Replace the select-then-write with an atomic upsert: INSERT INTO trader_daily_pnl (...) VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT (date, user_id) DO UPDATE SET realized_pnl = trader_daily_pnl.realized_pnl + EXCLUDED.realized_pnl, trades_count = trader_daily_pnl.trades_count + EXCLUDED.trades_count. Do not swallow the error silently — at minimum log it.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The cited code is present exactly as described. command/route.ts:180-192 reads today's trader_daily_pnl row by (date, userId), then either UPDATEs realizedPnl = existing.realizedPnl + realizedPnl (lost-update read-modify-write) or INSERTs a fresh row, with the whole block wrapped in `catch { /* best effort */ }` (line 192) so a unique-constraint rejection is silently swallowed. The UNIQUE index the finding relies on exists: trader_daily_pnl_date_user_idx on (date, userId) at schema/trader.ts:81. The concurrent-writer claim is not merely plausible — it is corroborated by the codebase itself: the engine's own daily-PnL writer upsertDailyPnl (trading-engine.ts:3062) was rewritten on 2026-06-09 to use Postgres ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE with arithmetic accumulation (realizedPnl = traderDailyPnl.realizedPnl + realizedDelta, tradesCount = + tradesCountDelta; lines 3094-3120), and its own comment (lines 3073-3079) states the pre-fix bug was that 'concurrent runScan + runExitCheck + flatten could BOTH see "no row" and BOTH attempt INSERT — one succeeds, the other's delta is dropped (caught at the unique constraint, then swallowed).' That fix was applied to the engine path but the flatten handler in command/route.ts was left on the old broken select-then-write pattern — an incomplete remediation. The flatten handler is explicitly designed to run while the engine is active (reserveManualFlatten, lines 122-126: 'Reserve these symbols in the running engine's pendingExits so its 15-min scan / 1-min exit poll won't also sell them mid-flatten ... No-op when the engine isn't running'), and the engine's 1-min exit poll (trading-engine.ts:2891) and 15-min scan (4593) write the same (getETDateString(), userId) row via the atomic upsert. So a flatten landing within an engine scan/exit write window, or two rapid flatten calls, race on the same accumulator row: the INSERT/INSERT collision drops one delta entirely and the UPDATE/UPDATE path loses the smaller delta. Impact is silent undercounting of the day's realized P&L and trade count shown on the dashboard and stored for tax/accounting — data integrity, no crash, conditional on a concurrency collision. This matches the project's named 'atomic upsert on accumulator rows' rule, and the correct fix already exists in-tree (call upsertDailyPnl, or use ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE). No mitigation refutes it; P2 (data corruption, requires concurrency) is appropriate.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:180-192 (select-then-(insert|update) with `realizedPnl: existing.realizedPnl + realizedPnl` at 187 and `catch { /* best effort */ }` at 192); src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:81 (uniqueIndex trader_daily_pnl_date_user_idx on (date, userId)); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3073-3079 (comment documenting the identical bug + that flatten is one of the racers); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3094-3120 (the atomic ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE fix the flatten handler does NOT use); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2891 and :4593 (engine exit/scan write the same daily-PnL row concurrently); src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:122-126 (reserveManualFlatten proves flatten runs while engine is active).

### 37. ✅ FIXED · [P2] /api/tax/report GET has no statement timeout and unbounded per-portfolio query loop

- **Lane:** tax · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:35-77`

**Description.** Every DB query in this route uses raw `db.select()` (portfolios line 35-38, per-portfolio portfolioTrades in a loop lines 44-54, all FILLED engine trades lines 69-77) with no withTimeout() wrapper and no isStatementTimeout handling. CLAUDE.md mandates `withTimeout(3000|5000, ...)` on all GET routes with DB queries and a 504 on statement timeout. The engine-trades query selects every FILLED row for the user across all time with no LIMIT, and the portfolio loop issues one unbounded query per portfolio. The sibling /api/export/tax-report route correctly uses `withTimeout(10_000, ...)` + isStatementTimeout (lines 53, 136), so this is an inconsistency, not an intentional exemption.

**Impact.** A user with a large trade history can hold a DB connection open indefinitely; under load this can exhaust the connection pool and degrade the whole app. No graceful 504 — the request hangs until the client or upstream proxy times out, then returns an opaque 500.

**Suggested fix.** Wrap the three queries in `withTimeout(5000, async (tx) => ...)` (5s for an export-style endpoint), catch isStatementTimeout(err) and return 504 with X-Query-Timeout, and replace the per-portfolio loop with a single `inArray(portfolioTrades.portfolioId, portfolioIds)` query.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> All technical claims are verified against the code. The GET route at src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts uses raw db.select() at all three query sites with no withTimeout() wrapper: portfolios (35-38), a per-portfolio portfolioTrades query inside a for-loop (44-54, an N+1 pattern), and an unbounded all-time FILLED traderTrades scan for the user with no LIMIT (69-77). The catch block (147-154) returns an opaque 500 with no isStatementTimeout() branch, so there is no graceful 504. This violates the explicit CLAUDE.md GET-route mandate ("All GET routes with DB queries use withTimeout()... 3s user-facing / 5s admin-export... Catch isStatementTimeout(err) → return 504"). withTimeout() (src/lib/db/index.ts:37-45) issues SET LOCAL statement_timeout inside a transaction, so its absence means PG never aborts a slow query on this route and the pooled connection (max:20, src/lib/db/index.ts:17-21) is held until client/proxy hangup. Critically, the sibling /api/export/tax-report route runs the identical FILLED-traderTrades query under withTimeout(10_000, ...) (line 53) with an isStatementTimeout(err) → 504 branch (lines 136-138), proving the correct pattern exists in the repo and this route is an oversight, not an intentional exemption. I attempted to refute on impact: the traderTrades query is indexed on userId (trader_trades_user_idx, src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:63), the route is session-authed and tier-gated to 'trader' (not anonymous), and a paper engine's row count is bounded — so "exhaust the pool / degrade the whole app" is somewhat aspirational rather than likely. But the contract violation and the missing graceful-degradation path are real and clearly within the documented invariant, and the proposed fix (wrap in withTimeout(5000), add 504 handling, collapse the loop to inArray) is correct. The reliability concern stands.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:35-38 (raw portfolios select, no withTimeout); :44-54 (N+1 per-portfolio portfolioTrades loop); :69-77 (unbounded all-time FILLED traderTrades scan, no LIMIT); :147-154 (catch returns opaque 500, no isStatementTimeout/504). src/lib/db/index.ts:37-45 (withTimeout sets SET LOCAL statement_timeout) and :50-52 (isStatementTimeout). src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts:53 + :136-138 (sibling route correctly uses withTimeout(10_000) + isStatementTimeout→504 on the same query). src/lib/db/index.ts:17-21 (pool max:20). src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:63 (trader_trades_user_idx backs the userId filter).

### 38. ✅ FIXED · [P2] Leap-year off-by-one: holdingDays > 365 misclassifies an exactly-one-year hold spanning a leap year as long-term

- **Lane:** tax · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/tax-report.ts:140-150`

**Description.** computeFifoClosedLots computes `holdingDays = (sellMs - buyMs) / MS_PER_DAY` and sets `isLongTerm: holdingDays > 365`. For a lot bought 2024-01-01 and sold 2025-01-01 (2024 is a leap year), the gap is 366 days, so holdingDays = 366 > 365 → long-term. Per IRS Pub 550 the holding period starts the day after purchase, so the one-year anniversary is 2025-01-01 and a sale on that date is NOT more than one year → it should be SHORT-term. tax-engine.ts gets this right (lines 293-295) by comparing against the calendar anniversary via setFullYear; tax-report.ts uses a fixed 365-day count, so the two tax modules disagree on the boundary in leap years. The test suite only covers the non-leap boundary (tests/unit/tax-report.test.ts lines 70-77: 2024-01-01→2024-12-31 = 365 days), so the leap-spanning case is untested.

**Impact.** An exactly-one-year hold that straddles Feb 29 is taxed at long-term capital-gains rates when it legally qualifies only for short-term (ordinary) rates, mis-stating the user's Form 8949 ST/LT split and tax estimate. Wrong direction for the IRS (under-taxes) in the gain case.

**Suggested fix.** Replace `holdingDays > 365` with the calendar-anniversary comparison used in tax-engine.ts: build `oneYearAfter = new Date(buyFillTime); oneYearAfter.setFullYear(+1)` and set `isLongTerm = sellFillTime.getTime() > oneYearAfter.getTime()`. Add a leap-year boundary test (2024-01-01 buy, 2025-01-01 sell → short-term).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P2.

> The finding is accurate on every checked point. tax-report.ts:94 defines MS_PER_DAY = 24*60*60*1000; line 140 computes holdingDays = (sellMs - buyMs)/MS_PER_DAY; line 150 sets isLongTerm: holdingDays > 365. For a lot bought 2024-01-01 (leap year) and sold 2025-01-01, the gap is 366 days, so holdingDays = 366 > 365 → isLongTerm = true (long-term). Per IRS Pub 550 the holding period starts the day after purchase, so the one-year anniversary of a 2024-01-01 buy falls on 2025-01-01 and a sale that day is exactly one year, NOT more than one year → it should be SHORT-term. tax-engine.ts:293-295 does this correctly with oneYearAfterPurchase = new Date(lot.date); setFullYear(+1); isLongTerm = sellDate > oneYearAfterPurchase — yielding false for the same dates — and its comment (lines 288-292) explicitly warns that a fixed 365-day count would mis-classify an exactly-one-year hold as long-term. So the two tax modules genuinely disagree at the leap-year boundary. The test suite (tax-report.test.ts) covers the 365-day non-leap boundary (lines 70-77, 2024-01-01→2024-12-31 → false) but has no test for the leap-straddling one-year-anniversary case. Direction of error is as claimed: long-term applied where short-term is legally correct, under-taxing a gain and mis-stating the Form 8949 ST/LT split. The trigger is narrow (hold must straddle Feb 29 and land at the exact 366-day mark, with matching intraday fill times for holdingDays to resolve to exactly 366), but it is a real, IRS-incorrect classification and an inter-module inconsistency with a ready-made correct template. P2 stands.

**Evidence:** src/lib/tax-report.ts:94 (const MS_PER_DAY = 24*60*60*1000); :140 (holdingDays = (event.fillTime.getTime() - head.fillTime.getTime())/MS_PER_DAY); :150 (isLongTerm: holdingDays > 365). Contrast: src/lib/tax-engine.ts:288-295 (comment + oneYearAfterPurchase.setFullYear(+1); isLongTerm = sellDate.getTime() > oneYearAfterPurchase.getTime()). Test gap: tests/unit/tax-report.test.ts:70-77 (only the 2024-01-01→2024-12-31 = 365-day non-leap boundary; no 2024→2025 leap-anniversary case).

### 39. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Dynamic-trail floor of 2% inverts and overrides sub-2% preset trailing stops

- **Lane:** backtester · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/backtester.ts:260-266`

**Description.** dynTrailPct = profitPct > 0 ? 0.02 + (activeCfg.trailingStopPct - 0.02) * Math.exp(-3*profitPct) : activeCfg.trailingStopPct (line 264-266). The 0.02 term is a hard floor. For presets whose trailingStopPct is below 2% — conservative (0.01), day_trade (0.008) — the (trailingStopPct - 0.02) factor is negative, so as profit rises exp(-3*profit) shrinks and dynTrailPct rises TOWARD 0.02. The trail therefore WIDENS with profit for these presets (opposite of the intended profit-based tightening) and can never be tighter than 2%, silently ignoring the configured 1% / 0.8% trail.

**Impact.** Conservative and day_trade backtests don't model their advertised tight trailing stops — every winner gives back at least 2% from the peak, and the trail loosens as the trade works, contradicting the preset's 'tight stops' description. Backtest results for these modes overstate give-back / understate the tightness users expect.

**Suggested fix.** Make the floor relative to the configured trail (e.g. floor = min(activeCfg.trailingStopPct, 0.02)) or clamp dynTrailPct to <= activeCfg.trailingStopPct so a tight base trail can't be widened by the formula. Align with the live engine's TRAIL_FLOOR handling.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and the math behaves exactly as described. backtester.ts:264-266 computes dynTrailPct = profitPct > 0 ? 0.02 + (activeCfg.trailingStopPct - 0.02) * Math.exp(-3*profitPct) : activeCfg.trailingStopPct. For any trailingStopPct < 0.02 the (trailingStopPct - 0.02) factor is negative, so as profit grows exp(-3*profitPct) shrinks toward 0 and dynTrailPct rises toward the 0.02 floor — the trail WIDENS with profit (inversion of intended tightening) and can never go below 2%. Verified concretely: conservative preset trailingStopPct=0.01 (strategy-presets.ts:17, labeled "Tight stops") gives trail 0.01 at profit 0 → 0.02 at large profit; day_trade=0.008 (line 41, "quick exits") gives 0.008 → 0.02. No clamp on dynTrailPct exists downstream (trailingStop = peakPrice*(1-dynTrailPct) at line 282; effectiveStop = Math.max(stopLoss, trailingStop) at line 283). The finding's claim that this diverges from the live engine is correct and load-bearing: trading-engine.ts:2341 computes range = Math.max(0, base - floor), which clamps the negative range to 0, so the live engine returns a flat 0.02 (no widening, no inversion) for sub-2% base trails. The backtester omits this Math.max(0,...) guard, so the two genuinely disagree and the proposed fix is accurate. Severity stays P3: this is a backtest/research-accuracy bug, not a live-trading or security issue — the live engine is unaffected, no money is at risk, and it only distorts offline backtest fidelity for sub-2% trails (overstating give-back, modeling a loosening rather than tightening trail). One framing nuance: the specific "conservative/day_trade preset" path is NOT reachable as a named mode arg in prod routes (mode-compare only runs optimized/tactical/adaptive at mode-compare/route.ts:46-50, and /api/backtest/[symbol] never passes mode), but the same inverted formula IS reachable via the single-symbol route's user-supplied trailingStop param clamped to min 0.005 (route.ts:34) and via the optimizer's portfolioBacktest GA range min 0.01 (optimizer.ts:98,459). So the underlying bug is real and reachable; only the preset-specific reachability is slightly overstated. P3 is appropriate.

**Evidence:** src/lib/backtester.ts:264-266 (inverted dynTrailPct formula with hardcoded 0.02 floor, no Math.max(0,...) clamp); src/lib/backtester.ts:282-283 (dynTrailPct consumed directly, no downstream clamp); src/lib/strategy-presets.ts:17 (conservative trailingStopPct=0.01, "Tight stops") and :41 (day_trade trailingStopPct=0.008, "quick exits"); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2341-2342 (live engine: range = Math.max(0, base - floor) clamps negative range → flat 0.02, no inversion); src/lib/backtester.ts:82 (configForMode propagates preset.trailingStopPct into cfg); src/app/api/backtest/[symbol]/route.ts:34 (user trailingStop clamped to min 0.005, no mode passed); src/app/api/backtest/mode-compare/route.ts:46-50 (only optimized/tactical/adaptive compared — conservative/day_trade not reachable here); src/lib/optimizer.ts:98,459 (portfolioBacktest GA trailingStopPct min 0.01 hits same formula)

### 40. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] toNumber() coerces unparseable Alpaca numerics to 0, feeding 0-priced fills into P&L delta math

- **Lane:** brokers · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** uncertain · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/brokers.ts:188-195, 205-222, 312-323`

**Description.** toNumber returns 0 for any value that isn't a number or parseable string (`isNaN(n) ? 0 : n`) and for null/objects. mapAlpacaOrder guards null explicitly (`filled_avg_price != null ? toNumber(...) : null`) so an absent price stays null, but if Alpaca ever returns the field present-but-non-numeric (empty string, "N/A", object), toNumber yields filledPrice=0 rather than null. reconcilePendingTrades (trading-engine.ts:3584-3605) does `delta = (newFillPrice - row.fillPrice) * row.quantity` and `newPnl = row.pnl + delta` using exactly `brokerOrder.filledPrice` whenever it is non-null. A spurious 0 fill price therefore produces delta = (0 - entry) * qty — a large fabricated loss written to trader_daily_pnl. Same coercion applies to qty/avgEntryPrice/currentPrice in getPositions (lines 313-322); a 0 currentPrice would distort downstream exposure/MTM math.

**Impact.** On malformed/edge broker data the silent 0 coercion converts a missing value into a concrete wrong number that flows directly into realized-P&L correction and position MTM — data corruption rather than a loud failure. Low likelihood (Alpaca normally returns clean numerics) but the failure mode is silent and money-facing.

**Suggested fix.** Have toNumber return NaN (or null) for unparseable input and have mapAlpacaOrder/getPositions propagate null for prices/qty rather than 0; guard money math with `!(x > 0)` before using a fill price, and skip the P&L delta when the broker price isn't a positive finite number.

**Verifier verdict:** `uncertain` — corrected severity P3.

> The code mechanics are exactly as described. brokers.ts:188-195 toNumber returns 0 for any non-numeric/unparseable input (isNaN(n)?0:n, and the trailing `return 0`). mapAlpacaOrder (214) guards only `!= null`: `filledPrice: o.filled_avg_price != null ? toNumber(o.filled_avg_price) : null` — so a present-but-non-numeric value (empty string, "N/A", object) yields filledPrice=0 rather than null. getPositions (313-322) coerces qty/avgEntryPrice/currentPrice the same way. The reconcile delta math is present and uses filledPrice directly: trading-engine.ts:3584 `newFillPrice = brokerOrder.filledPrice ?? row.fillPrice` then 3593 `const delta = (newFillPrice - row.fillPrice) * row.quantity; newPnl = row.pnl + delta`. So a spurious 0 fill price WOULD produce delta=(0-entry)*qty and write a fabricated loss. However, the impact is gated entirely on Alpaca violating its own response contract. For a `filled` order (the only state that triggers the delta math, line 3582), Alpaca contractually returns filled_avg_price as a numeric string; when not filled it is JSON null, which the != null guard already catches. The postulated empty-string/"N/A"/object case for a filled order is not part of Alpaca's documented contract, so the path requires malformed upstream data. The finding itself concedes "low likelihood." For the MTM claim, the engine's actual exit/stop math uses quote.price from the data provider (2760-2764), which IS already guarded with `if (!(currentPrice > 0)) continue;`; the broker currentPrice only feeds sector-exposure cap and peakPrice tracking (3356-3360), self-correcting on the next sync, not direct money math. This is a legitimate defensive-coding/hardening gap (the != null guard should also reject non-finite/non-positive numerics), but not a confirmed live data-corruption path. The finding's own P3 rating is correct and appropriate.

**Evidence:** src/lib/brokers.ts:188-195 (toNumber returns 0 on isNaN and on non-number/non-string); src/lib/brokers.ts:214 (filledPrice guard checks only `!= null`); src/lib/brokers.ts:313-316 (qty/avgEntryPrice/currentPrice via toNumber); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3582-3595 (delta = (newFillPrice - row.fillPrice) * row.quantity, only when bs==='filled' and action==='SELL'); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2760-2764 (exit/MTM uses quote.price guarded by `if (!(currentPrice > 0)) continue;`); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3356-3360 (broker currentPrice feeds peakPrice/marketValue, self-corrects next sync)

### 41. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Senate eFD response bodies parsed with res.json()/res.text() with no per-call guard against Akamai challenge pages

- **Lane:** data-ingestion · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:245-249`

**Description.** searchPtrs does `const data = (await res.json()) as {...}` immediately after checking res.ok. The module's own header comment notes the Senate site is Akamai-fronted and aggressive about non-browser traffic; Akamai frequently returns an HTML interstitial/challenge with HTTP 200, which makes res.json() throw a SyntaxError. That throw is not caught inside searchPtrs or ingestSenateYear — it only unwinds to refreshSenateRecent's per-year try/catch (lines 537-547), aborting the entire Senate year ingest (search + all PTR fetches) on a single transient challenge rather than retrying/backing off. The fetchAndParsePtr path is wrapped per-PTR (line 473) so it's resilient, but the search step is the single point that kills the whole year.

**Impact.** One Akamai challenge during the search step silently drops the full Senate ingest for that year on the daily cron; with no retry, congressional-trades data can go stale for a day on transient bot-detection.

**Suggested fix.** Wrap the body read in try/catch and validate Content-Type/JSON shape before parsing; on parse failure or detected challenge, retry with backoff (respecting any Retry-After) a few times before failing the year, rather than letting the SyntaxError abort everything.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code and control flow are exactly as described. At src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:245 `searchPtrs` does `const data = (await res.json()) as {...}` right after the `res.ok` check (line 240) with no try/catch anywhere in the function (192-271) or its paginated while-loop. An Akamai HTML challenge served with HTTP 200 — a real failure mode the module's own header comment acknowledges (lines 19-23: "fronted by Akamai, which is aggressive about flagging non-browser-shaped traffic") — passes `res.ok` and makes `res.json()` throw a SyntaxError. `ingestSenateYear` calls `searchPtrs` at line 453, OUTSIDE the per-PTR try/catch which only wraps the fetchAndParsePtr/DB-insert body (473-516). So the throw unwinds straight out of `ingestSenateYear` to `refreshSenateRecent`'s per-year catch (537-545), aborting the whole year's Senate ingest (search + all PTRs) on a single transient challenge. I confirmed there is no retry/backoff anywhere in the Senate path (grep found retry logic only in unrelated modules: Stripe, trading-engine boot, CSRF). This is the daily cron path (refresh-congress/route.ts:58). Two factors keep this correctly at P3, not higher: (a) the ingest is idempotent (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING) and the cron runs daily against a 45-day PTR disclosure window, so a one-day miss self-heals on the next run — the route comment itself says "even hourly polling is overkill"; (b) House ingest is fully independent (route.ts:49-54), so only Senate-for-that-year is lost. The finding's only overstatement is "silently drops" — it actually logs at log.error (line 541) and the cron returns HTTP 500 when Senate fails (route.ts:64,68), so it is observable. That nuance does not change the substance: the unguarded body parse is real, the single-point-of-failure analysis is accurate, and P3 (reliability/robustness, self-healing, no correctness/security/money impact) is the right severity.

**Evidence:** src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:240-249 (res.ok check then unguarded `await res.json()`); src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:192-271 (searchPtrs has no try/catch); src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:19-23 (header comment confirming Akamai bot-detection); src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:453 (searchPtrs called outside per-PTR try); src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:471-520 (per-PTR try/catch wraps only fetchAndParsePtr/insert); src/lib/congress-senate-ingester.ts:536-547 (refreshSenateRecent per-year try/catch logs at error and continues); src/app/api/cron/refresh-congress/route.ts:48-69 (daily cron path, House independent of Senate, 500 on Senate failure)

### 42. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Finnhub RateLimiter has a check-await-decrement TOCTOU that overshoots the 60/min cap under concurrency

- **Lane:** data-ingestion · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/finnhub.ts:208-237`

**Description.** RateLimiter.acquire() reads this.tokens, and if tokens < 1 computes waitMs = ((1 - tokens)/refillRate)*1000, awaits that sleep, calls refill(), then unconditionally does this.tokens -= 1. Node yields at the await, so when several Finnhub requests run concurrently (the hybrid pipeline fires sentiment + options + analyst layers in parallel via Promise.allSettled at src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:87, each calling request() -> rateLimiter.acquire()), they all observe tokens < 1, all compute the same waitMs, sleep, wake near-simultaneously, and each subtracts 1 after a single ~1-token refill. tokens is driven sharply negative and N requests fire in a burst. The acquire() also never re-checks after the single sleep+refill — one sleep then always decrements, even if still under 1 token.

**Impact.** Under concurrent load the limiter emits bursts that exceed Finnhub's free-tier 60 req/min, producing 429s; on a 429 the client (request(), lines 286-293) throws and the hybrid layer is dropped, degrading signal quality. The cap the limiter is meant to enforce is not actually enforced when calls overlap.

**Suggested fix.** Make the claim atomic: serialize acquire() through a single in-flight promise chain (each acquire awaits the previous), or claim a token synchronously before any await (decrement first, and if it goes below zero, schedule the wait based on the position in queue). Re-loop the refill/check after sleeping instead of decrementing unconditionally.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present exactly as described and the TOCTOU is real. src/lib/finnhub.ts:221-229: acquire() does refill() -> `if (this.tokens < 1)` -> compute `waitMs = ((1 - tokens)/refillRate)*1000` -> single `await setTimeout` -> refill() -> unconditional `this.tokens -= 1`. The `await` at line 225 is a yield point, and there is no serializing queue/mutex/in-flight chain anywhere in the file (only the token bucket synchronizes; grep for queue/mutex/inFlight returns nothing). So multiple callers that observe tokens<1 all compute the same waitMs, sleep, wake near-simultaneously, and each subtracts 1 after a single ~1-token refill, driving tokens negative and emitting a burst. The "never re-checks after the single sleep" claim is also literally true — there is no re-loop, so one sleep then always decrements even if still under 1 token. This violates the codebase's own "claim the slot synchronously before the first await" guidance. The concurrent call path is confirmed: src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:67-87 fires sentiment/options/analyst via Promise.allSettled, and sentiment-layer.ts:57 + analyst-layer.ts:71 both call getFinnhubClient() -> request() -> rateLimiter.acquire(). The client is a global singleton (finnhub.ts:458 `g.__finnhubClient ??= new FinnhubClient()`), so all symbols AND all multi-tenant users share one RateLimiter, amplifying overlap. HOWEVER the impact is narrower than the burst would suggest: (1) the bucket starts full at 60 tokens, so the first ~60 concurrent calls in a fresh window decrement synchronously with NO await and NO overshoot — the bug only manifests once tokens<1 under sustained load; (2) per CLAUDE.md and confirmed code, hybrid layers are NOT run in the high-volume screener scan, only on per-symbol analyze/engine-signal paths (~2-3 acquires each, far under 60); (3) both the request-level cache (finnhub.ts:271-273) and the sentiment layer cache (sentiment-layer.ts:51-55) skip acquire() on repeat symbols; (4) the downstream consequence is graceful — a 429 throws (finnhub.ts:292), is caught as a rejected Promise.allSettled outcome (pipeline.ts:91/105), and the layer is simply dropped, degrading signal quality with no state corruption, crash, or mis-sized order. So this is a genuine concurrency bug but a self-healing, transient soft-degradation that only bites under uncommon multi-user/multi-symbol simultaneity. P2 is mildly inflated for a non-corrupting, well-mitigated transient; P3 is proportionate.

**Evidence:** src/lib/finnhub.ts:221-229 (acquire: refill -> if tokens<1 -> waitMs -> await sleep -> refill -> unconditional tokens-=1, no re-loop); src/lib/finnhub.ts:231-236 (refill math allows tokens to be driven negative across wakers); src/lib/finnhub.ts:279 (request() awaits rateLimiter.acquire()); src/lib/finnhub.ts:286-293 (429/!ok throws); src/lib/finnhub.ts:458 (global singleton — one shared limiter across all users/symbols); src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:67-87 (parallel Promise.allSettled of the three layers); src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:57 and src/lib/hybrid/analyst-layer.ts:71 (both call getFinnhubClient -> request -> acquire); src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:90-110 (rejected/empty layer outcomes silently dropped); mitigations: src/lib/finnhub.ts:271-273 (request cache) and src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:51-55 (sentiment cache) bypass acquire on cache hit.

### 43. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] getSymbolSector buckets all off-list symbols (screener picks, ADRs) into 'Other' and all ETFs into 'ETF', distorting the sector cap

- **Lane:** engine-buy-gates · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/sectors.ts:196-198`

**Description.** getSymbolSector returns 'Other' for any symbol not in the static S&P-500 map (line 197), and every ETF (SPY, QQQ, GLD, XLK, XLF, ...) maps to a single 'ETF' bucket (lines 185-193). The engine's scan universe includes screener-fed external symbols (selectExternalSymbolsForTactical) which can be arbitrary tickers off the S&P-500 list. In the sector cap (canPlaceBuyOrder lines 933-941) all such symbols are treated as one sector. Two genuinely unrelated holdings (e.g. a small-cap screener pick + an ADR) count as the same 'Other' sector, and unrelated ETFs (GLD gold + XLF financials) count as the same 'ETF' sector.

**Impact.** Sector cap is both over-restrictive (unrelated 'Other' names block each other) and meaningless as a concentration measure (a portfolio of 5 unrelated ETFs reads as one sector at 5x the intended single-sector weight). The cap does not actually reflect economic sector concentration for off-list or ETF holdings.

**Suggested fix.** Resolve sector for off-list symbols via the broker/asset metadata or a provider sector lookup, and split ETFs by underlying exposure (or exclude ETFs from the concentration calc). At minimum, treat 'Other' symbols as their own per-symbol sector so they don't pool.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code behaves exactly as described and the pooling distortion is real, though the finding's causal story is partly inaccurate. CODE: src/lib/sectors.ts:196-198 returns "Other" for any symbol not in the static S&P-500 map, and lines 185-193 map every ETF (SPY/QQQ/DIA/IWM/VTI/VOO/GLD/SLV/USO/UNG/TLT/IEF/HYG/LQD/XLF/XLE/XLK/XLV/XLI/XLP/XLY/XLU/XLB/XLRE/XLC/UVXY) to a single "ETF" bucket — confirmed. SECTOR CAP: trading-engine.ts:928-956 computes newSector = getSymbolSector(symbol), then sums market value of every existing position where getSymbolSector(posSymbol)===newSector. So all "ETF" symbols sum together and all "Other" symbols sum together. The cap runs on all three scan paths (tactical 3750, tactical-smart 4067, optimized/runScan 4934 via buildSectorExposureContext, lines 849-863, which iterates the full broker-synced position map). ETF POOLING (fully real): The screener universe is built from getPopularSymbolsBySector() (screener.ts:207-213, 326-332), which is the same static map — so ETFs ARE scanned, can be pushed as BUY/STRONG_BUY via pushScreenerSignals/broadcastExternalSignal (trader-client.ts:67), and the engine can buy them. selectExternalSymbolsForTactical (6482-6496) dedups against SCAN_UNIVERSE = S&P-500 only (no ETFs, sp500.ts), so ETF signals survive the dedup. Thus a basket like SPY+GLD+TLT+XLF all pool into one "ETF" sector at multiples of the intended single-sector weight — both over-restrictive and economically meaningless as a concentration measure. This matches the claim. "OTHER" POOLING (reachable but mischaracterized cause): The finding claims the scan universe / selectExternalSymbolsForTactical introduces arbitrary off-S&P tickers that pool into "Other". That is inaccurate — the ONLY external-signal source is the screener, which scans only symbols already in the static map (S&P-500 + ETFs), so it can never produce an off-list "Other" symbol, and engine-initiated buys are confined to SCAN_UNIVERSE + screener picks. HOWEVER, the "Other" pooling is still reachable via a different path: syncPositionMapFromBroker (3248-3322) imports ALL long broker positions unfiltered, including manual broker-UI buys, transferred-in ADRs, and small-caps not in the static map. Those resolve to "Other" and pool in the sector sum. So the bug is real, just via broker-side positions rather than the engine's own universe. SEVERITY: P3 is appropriate and not inflated. The cap is opt-in (maxSectorExposurePct defaults to 0 = disabled, trading-engine.ts:779; gated at 929). The defect does not lose money, corrupt position sizing, or break a halt/stop invariant — it makes a conservative risk cap economically imprecise for ETF baskets and broker-side off-list holdings. The code itself flags the cap as "approximate anyway" (line 859). The engine's core S&P-500 universe resolves to correct GICS-style sectors.

**Evidence:** src/lib/sectors.ts:196-198 (off-list -> "Other"); src/lib/sectors.ts:185-193 (all ETFs -> "ETF"); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:933-941 (sector sum by getSymbolSector equality); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:779 (maxSectorExposurePct default 0 = disabled); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:849-863 (buildSectorExposureContext iterates full position map); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3750,4067,4934 (cap applied on all three scan paths); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6482-6496 (selectExternalSymbolsForTactical dedups against SCAN_UNIVERSE = S&P-500); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:24 (SCAN_UNIVERSE = SP500_SYMBOLS); src/lib/screener.ts:207-213,326-332 (screener universe = getPopularSymbolsBySector = static map incl. ETFs); src/lib/trader-client.ts:40-75 (only external-signal source is the screener); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3256-3311 (syncPositionMapFromBroker imports all long broker positions unfiltered)

### 44. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Portfolio exposure cap mixes cost basis (existing) with current price (new), understating exposure as positions appreciate

- **Lane:** engine-buy-gates · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5345-5353, 4516-4520, 5530-5538`

**Description.** The max-exposure check computes currentExposure as sum(p.entryPrice * p.qty) — i.e. cost basis — for existing positions (line 5348), but compares against effectiveMaxExposure = equity * multiplier where equity is live mark-to-market account equity, and adds the new order at currentPrice * qty (line 5349). Existing positions that have appreciated are counted at their lower entry cost, so the true market-value exposure can exceed the configured multiplier. The same cost-basis sum is used in the add path (4519) and swap-sell planner input (5537-5538).

**Impact.** In a rising book, actual market-value exposure can drift above the intended maxExposureMultiplier (default 1.5x equity) without the gate firing, because appreciated holdings are undercounted at entry cost. The leverage/exposure limit is looser than configured precisely when the book is up.

**Suggested fix.** Sum exposure using market value (p.marketValue ?? (p.currentPrice ?? p.entryPrice) * p.qty), consistent with how buildSectorExposureContext already values positions, rather than entryPrice * qty.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The finding is mechanically accurate at all three cited sites. The runScan exposure gate computes currentExposure = sum(p.entryPrice * p.qty) (cost basis) at src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5347-5348, compares it against effectiveMaxExposure derived from equity (the broker's live mark-to-market account equity: const equity = account.equity at line 3743/4056/4723), and adds the new order at currentPrice * qty (line 5349). The same cost-basis sum appears in the tactical-smart add path (4518-4519, compared to equity-based effectiveMaxExposure at 4517) and the swap-sell planner input (5537-5538, equity-based cap at 5530-5535). So the LHS is cost basis while the RHS and the marginal term are mark-to-market — a genuine basis mismatch that undercounts appreciated holdings and lets true market-value exposure drift above maxExposureMultiplier (default 1.5x) precisely when the book is up. The proposed fix is also validated: TrackedPosition already carries currentPrice and marketValue (interface at lines 3142-3143), populated every scan by syncPositionMapFromBroker (lines 3369-3370 set currentPrice: bp.currentPrice, marketValue: bp.marketValue). And buildSectorExposureContext at line 860 already uses exactly the proposed valuation: pos.marketValue ?? (pos.currentPrice ?? pos.entryPrice) * pos.qty. So the exposure cap diverges from an established market-value pattern in the same file, and the corrected data is readily in hand. Severity is correctly P3, not inflated. This is a soft guardrail (it skips a new BUY, it is not money-movement or position-sizing math). The mismatch only loosens the limit when positions are already profitable, and independent gates remain (buying-power check at line 5339, daily notional cap, position-count hard cap at 4507/5550, sector-exposure cap which itself uses market value). The bug is an internal consistency/under-enforcement defect in one approximate risk guardrail, not a leverage control of record — exactly a low-severity P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5346-5349 (effectiveMaxExposure from equity vs currentExposure = sum(entryPrice*qty), new order added at currentPrice*qty); :4517-4520 (add path, same pattern); :5530-5538 (swap-sell planner, equity cap vs entryPrice*qty exposure); :3743 const equity = account.equity (mark-to-market); :3142-3143 TrackedPosition.currentPrice/marketValue fields; :3369-3370 sync populates currentPrice/marketValue every scan; :860 buildSectorExposureContext uses pos.marketValue ?? (pos.currentPrice ?? pos.entryPrice) * pos.qty (the established market-value valuation the fix proposes)

### 45. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] runExitCheck computes realized P&L and consecutive-loss / daily-loss from the quote, not the actual market fill

- **Lane:** engine-exit-stops · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2867-2891`

**Description.** On a 1-min exit, runExitCheck places a market sell (placeEngineOrder, line 2867) then immediately computes `pnl = (currentPrice - pos.entryPrice) * pos.qty` (line 2869) from the pre-trade quote, before the order fills. This pnl feeds engine.dailyLoss (2870), recordTradeResult → consecutive-loss halt (2873-2879), and upsertDailyPnl (2891). A market sell on a fast-moving / illiquid name can fill materially away from the quote; the consecutive-loss counter and daily-loss halt threshold are therefore driven by an estimated, not realized, P&L. A trade that actually filled at a loss but was quoted flat would not increment consecutiveLosses, and vice-versa.

**Impact.** Auto-halt safeguards (consecutive-loss halt, daily-loss tracking) can mis-fire or fail to fire because they key off the quoted price at submission rather than the fill price returned by the broker, slightly mis-stating realized P&L and the loss-streak state that gates trading.

**Suggested fix.** After the sell order resolves, read the broker fill price (or reconcile via reconcilePendingTrades) and base pnl / dailyLoss / recordTradeResult on the actual filled price and qty, consistent with how the main-scan path uses broker-reported qty. At minimum document that this is an estimate and let reconcile correct the persisted PnL.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves as described. At src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2867-2891, runExitCheck places a market sell (2867), then computes `pnl = (currentPrice - pos.entryPrice) * pos.qty` (2869) from `currentPrice` — the pre-trade 1-min poll quote, not the broker fill. That estimated pnl feeds engine.dailyLoss (2870), recordTradeResult → consecutive-loss halt (2873-2879), and upsertDailyPnl (2891). recordTradeResult (1033-1040) only increments consecutiveLosses when `pnl < 0`, so a sign flip between quote and fill (flat-quoted trade that fills slightly negative, or vice versa) does mis-classify the loss-streak/daily-loss state. reconcilePendingTrades (3512-3663) DOES correct the persisted DB row's fillPrice/pnl via delta math (3593-3594) — but it never touches the in-memory engine.dailyLoss or engine.consecutiveLosses, so the halt-gate state is not reconciled to the actual fill. So the central mechanism is real and not mitigated for the in-memory safeguards. That said the impact is bounded and somewhat overstated: (1) the dominant main-scan exit path does the IDENTICAL thing — line 5187-5188 computes pnl from `currentPrice`, not the fill — so this is a deliberate system-wide design (estimate in-memory, reconcile the ledger), not a runExitCheck-specific bug. The finding's proposed-fix premise that the main scan 'uses broker-reported qty' is true only for QTY (a 2026-06-09 fix at 5175-5177), not for price. (2) Mis-firing only happens at the P&L sign boundary; genuine stop_loss/trailing_stop exits fire well below entry where quote-vs-fill slippage is dwarfed by the loss magnitude, so the sign is correct. The defect is a narrow numeric edge case in a defense-in-depth safeguard, with the authoritative trade ledger correctly reconciled. P3 (lowest) is appropriate.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:2867 placeEngineOrder (market sell); :2869 `const pnl = (currentPrice - pos.entryPrice) * pos.qty` (quote, not fill); :2870 `engine.dailyLoss += pnl < 0 ? pnl : 0`; :2873-2879 loadRiskLimits + recordTradeResult → tripSafeguardHalt('consecutive_losses'); :2882 logTrade(..., currentPrice, "PENDING", pnl, ...); :2891 upsertDailyPnl(..., pnl, ...). recordTradeResult sign gate at :1033-1040 (`if (pnl < 0) consecutiveLosses += 1`). Identical estimated-P&L in main scan at :5187-5188 (`pnl = (currentPrice - heldPosition.entryPrice) * sellQty`) and :5190/:5193. reconcilePendingTrades :3512-3663 updates only trader_trades.fillPrice/pnl/status (:3622-3630, delta math :3593-3594) and never mutates engine.dailyLoss/consecutiveLosses.

### 46. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Flatten command daily-P&L update is select-then-(insert|update), not an atomic upsert — concurrent writer drops a realized-P&L delta

- **Lane:** engine-lifecycle-regime · **Category:** data · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:180-192`

**Description.** The flatten path updates trader_daily_pnl with a read-modify-write: SELECT the today row (182-184), then if found UPDATE realizedPnl = existing.realizedPnl + delta (186-188) else INSERT (190). This is the exact accumulator TOCTOU the engine itself was fixed to avoid — upsertDailyPnl (trading-engine.ts:3093-3116) uses INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET realizedPnl = realizedPnl + delta specifically because concurrent runScan + runExitCheck + flatten could each see 'no row' and both INSERT (one rejected by the trader_daily_pnl_date_user unique index, its delta silently lost), or two UPDATEs could read the same baseline and lose one delta. The flatten route re-introduced the non-atomic pattern for the same table while the engine may be running and writing the same row via upsertDailyPnl.

**Impact.** A flatten that lands concurrently with an engine scan write (or another flatten in flight) for the same user/day can lose a realized-P&L delta or a tradesCount increment, corrupting the day's realized total and downstream daily-loss-halt math / tax-year reporting that reads trader_daily_pnl.

**Suggested fix.** Replace the SELECT-then-UPDATE/INSERT with the same atomic upsert the engine uses: INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (date,userId) DO UPDATE SET realizedPnl = trader_daily_pnl.realizedPnl + EXCLUDED.realizedPnl, tradesCount = trader_daily_pnl.tradesCount + EXCLUDED.tradesCount. Ideally call the shared upsertDailyPnl helper rather than open-coding it per route.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:180-192 does a non-atomic read-modify-write on trader_daily_pnl: it SELECTs today's row (182-184), then UPDATEs realizedPnl = existing.realizedPnl + realizedPnl using the stale read baseline (186-188), else INSERTs (190). The engine's own helper upsertDailyPnl (trading-engine.ts:3093-3120) was deliberately written as an atomic INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (date,userId) DO UPDATE SET realizedPnl = trader_daily_pnl.realizedPnl + delta — its line-3076 comment explicitly cites the unique-constraint catch on concurrent INSERTs, confirming this is the known accumulator-TOCTOU hazard. The impact is real and NOT mitigated, because flatten does not stop the engine. reserveManualFlatten (trading-engine.ts:7261-7283) only adds the flattened symbols to engine.pendingExits (no-op if not running) so the engine won't double-sell those specific symbols — but the engine keeps running and continues writing the SAME (today, userId) trader_daily_pnl row via the atomic upsertDailyPnl from runScan (4593), per-trade exit (2891), and the mark-to-market path (3952). Node is single-threaded but await yields: the flatten path has multiple awaits (placeOrder, writeAudit, insert traderTrades, and the await db.select at 182) before the await db.update at 186. An engine upsert running its atomic +delta during that window means flatten then writes existing.realizedPnl + realizedPnl off a pre-increment baseline, clobbering the engine's delta — or, on the INSERT branch, two INSERTs collide on the unique index trader_daily_pnl_date_user_idx (confirmed: drizzle/0003 line 23, schema/trader.ts:81) and the rejected one's delta is silently lost in the empty `catch {}` at line 192. Either way a realizedPnl delta or tradesCount increment can be lost, corrupting daily-loss-halt math and tax-year reporting that read this table. Severity P3 is correct, not inflated: it is a genuine data-integrity correctness bug matching the project's own "atomic upsert on accumulator rows" rule and the engine's own remediation, but it requires a narrow concurrent-interleave window with engine writes for the same user/day; flatten is an infrequent manual action and the corruption is a single accumulator value, not a cross-tenant leak or direct financial loss. The proposed fix (call the shared upsertDailyPnl helper) is exactly right.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:182-184 (SELECT today row), :186-188 (UPDATE realizedPnl = existing.realizedPnl + realizedPnl), :190 (INSERT branch), :192 (empty catch swallows constraint violation); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3093-3120 (atomic upsertDailyPnl with sql`${traderDailyPnl.realizedPnl} + ${realizedDelta}` ON CONFLICT), :3076 (comment on unique-constraint catch), :2891/:3952/:4593 (engine call sites writing same row), :7261-7283 (reserveManualFlatten only mutates pendingExits, does not stop engine); drizzle/0003_smart_human_fly.sql:23 and src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:81 (UNIQUE index on date,user_id)

### 47. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] globalThis engine map grows unbounded for halted engines and for position-less users created at boot (eviction only scheduled from stopEngine)

- **Lane:** engine-lifecycle-regime · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3204-3211, 6294-6310, 6805-6827, 6987-7071`

**Description.** evictEngineState() is scheduled in exactly one place: the deferred timer in stopEngine() (6299-6302). haltEngine() (6805-6914) sets engine.running=false but schedules no eviction, so a halted engine's EngineState (plus its position map and broker-position cache) stays in g.__tradingEngines / g2.__enginePositionMaps / g3.__brokerPositionCache forever. Separately, autoStartIfNeeded() calls getEngine(userId) at line 6988 — which lazily inserts a fresh EngineState into the map (getEngine 355-359) — then returns early at 7067 (no broker) or 7071 (positions.length===0) without ever evicting that just-created idle engine. bootEngines iterates EVERY user with an active broker connection (engine-boot.ts 25-47) and calls autoStartIfNeeded for each, so on boot the map gains a never-running entry for every broker-connected user with no open positions, none of which is ever evicted.

**Impact.** Slow, monotonic memory accumulation bounded by the broker-connected userbase plus any user who emergency-halts. Each leaked EngineState carries Sets/Maps (cooldowns, washSaleBlockedSymbols, exitRejectionCount/exitSuppressedUntil, unprotectedSymbols, recentOrderTimestamps) and a position map. On a long-lived container with a growing userbase this is a real heap leak, and stale entries can shadow a fresh boot's intended state.

**Suggested fix.** Have haltEngine schedule a guarded deferred evictEngineState() like stopEngine does (the existing running/starting re-check makes a later restart cancel it). In autoStartIfNeeded's early-return paths (no broker / no positions), evict the engine that getEngine just created when it isn't running. Consider a periodic sweep that deletes map entries where !running && !starting && !halted.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> All three code claims verified against the source. (1) evictEngineState (trading-engine.ts:3204-3211) is the only function that deletes from the three global maps, and it is scheduled in exactly ONE place: the deferred unref'd setTimeout in stopEngine (6299-6302). A grep across src finds no other call site and no periodic sweep. (2) haltEngine (6805-6914) sets engine.running=false and engine.halted=true (6826-6827) then returns at 6913 with no eviction scheduled — the EngineState, its position map (g2), and broker cache (g3) persist. Note evictEngineState only re-checks running||starting, not halted, but it is irrelevant here because the halt path never schedules it. (3) autoStartIfNeeded calls getEngine(userId) at 6988 which lazily inserts a fresh EngineState (getEngine 356-358), then has THREE early-return paths that leave the just-created idle entry behind: persisted-halt suppression (7046), no broker (7067), and positions.length===0 (7071). bootEngines (engine-boot.ts 25-47) iterates every user with an active broker connection and calls autoStartIfNeeded for each via Promise.allSettled, so on every container boot the map gains a never-running entry for every broker-connected user with no open positions. createDefaultEngine (301-348) allocates multiple Sets/Maps per entry, confirming a real (if small) per-entry footprint. The leak is real, monotonic, and bounded by the broker-connected userbase plus halt count. It is low-impact: each idle EngineState is a few empty collections (~KB), the broker-cache entry isn't even populated on the idle-boot path, and the cross-boot 'shadowing' concern is overstated since globals reset on a real process restart and getEngine returning an existing non-running entry is benign (startEngine mutates in place). Genuine reliability/heap-growth issue, correctly rated P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3204-3211 (evictEngineState — only deleter, re-checks running||starting); 6299-6302 (only setTimeout(evictEngineState) scheduling, in stopEngine); 6826-6827 + 6913 (haltEngine sets running=false/halted=true, returns with no eviction); 6988 (autoStartIfNeeded getEngine lazily creates); 356-358 (getEngine inserts createDefaultEngine on miss); 7046, 7067, 7071 (three early returns leaving the created entry); 301-348 (createDefaultEngine allocates pendingExits/cooldowns/exitRejectionCount/exitSuppressedUntil/unprotectedSymbols/washSaleBlockedSymbols/losingReentryBlockedSymbols Sets+Maps); src/lib/engine-boot.ts:25-47 (bootEngines selects every active broker connection and calls autoStartIfNeeded per user)
