### 48. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] reserveManualFlatten.release() deletes pendingExits entries the running engine may own — narrow double-sell window

- **Lane:** engine-lifecycle-regime · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** low
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7261-7283, 2864, 2901, 5153, 5251`

**Description.** reserveManualFlatten() (7261-7283) adds the flattened symbols to engine.pendingExits (7272) and the returned release() unconditionally deletes those same symbols from pendingExits (7275). pendingExits is a plain Set with no per-owner tracking, and the running engine's own exit paths independently add/delete the same set (runScan: add 2864 / delete 2901; runExitCheck: add 5153 / delete 5251) as their in-flight-sell guard. flatten is an engine COMMAND allowed while the engine is running (trader/command/route.ts:126). If the engine's 1-min exit poll has just added symbol X to pendingExits to guard its own market sell, and a concurrent flatten on X (or flatten-all) calls release(), release deletes X — clearing the engine's in-flight-exit guard while its sell is still pending. The next scan's pendingExits.has(X) check then passes and can place a second sell on the same position.

**Impact.** Rare but real: a position can be hit by two market sells (the engine's pending exit + a re-fire after the guard was cleared), oversell toward a short on a long-only engine, and double-count realized P&L. Requires precise interleaving of the 1-min poll and a manual flatten on the same symbol.

**Suggested fix.** Track reservation ownership instead of sharing the raw Set: either give the manual flatten its own reservation set/refcount that release only decrements (delete from pendingExits only if no engine-owned reservation remains), or have release() delete only symbols that were not already present when reserveManualFlatten was called (capture the pre-existing membership and skip those on release).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The structural defect is real and present. reserveManualFlatten (trading-engine.ts:7261-7283) adds every flattened symbol to engine.pendingExits (7272) and the returned release() does `for (const s of symbols) engine.pendingExits.delete(s)` (7275) — an unconditional delete of all reserved symbols. engine.pendingExits is a plain Set<string> with no per-owner tracking, and it is the explicit shared cross-path in-flight-sell guard: runScanInner adds at 5153 / deletes in finally at 5251, runExitCheck adds at 2864 / deletes in finally at 2901, and both gate on pendingExits.has(symbol) at loop top (4986, 2755) with synchronous re-check-and-claim (5143/5153, 2852/2864) precisely because the two paths can overlap. They CAN overlap: startEngine wires the main scan and the exit poll on SEPARATE re-entrancy guards (scanInFlight vs exitCheckInFlight, 6158/6237) plus separate setInterval timers (6220, 6239), so the set is the only thing serializing a sell across the two intervals. Flatten is an engine command allowed while running — the command route (trader/command/route.ts:24-126) has no engine-stop gate, and reserveManualFlatten exists specifically to coordinate with a running engine (doc 7250-7259). So if the engine has already claimed X (engine add precedes flatten reserve), the flatten's idempotent re-add is a no-op and its later release() deletes X out from under the engine's still-pending sell, clearing the guard before the engine's own finally runs. That is a genuine premature-guard-clear. Two qualifications that bound severity (and which the finding itself acknowledges): (1) The flatten loop (127-136) places its sells WITHOUT consulting pendingExits, so when the engine already has X in flight, the flatten double-sells X regardless of the ownership bug — the dominant double-sell contributor is the missing pendingExits check in the flatten loop, not the Set-ownership defect, so the proposed per-owner-tracking fix is incomplete. (2) The finding's specific re-fire path ("release clears guard → next scan's has(X) passes → second sell") additionally needs X to remain in the position map after release; on the success path release deletes X from the posMap (7278) and the engine's exit path deletes it too (5216/2883) before its finally, so a clean re-fire requires the narrow combination of engine-pending-sell + flatten-sell-on-X-throwing (so X is excluded from soldSymbols and not posMap-deleted) + an overlapping subsequent scan in the gap before the engine's in-flight sell resolves. This is exactly the "rare but real, requires precise interleaving" the finding claims. Net: the hazard is genuine, low-probability, and correctly rated P3 — not inflated. Cosmetic note: the finding swapped the function labels (2864/2901 are runExitCheck, 5153/5251 are runScanInner), which does not change the substance.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7272 (release adds all reserved symbols), :7275 (`for (const s of symbols) engine.pendingExits.delete(s)` — unconditional delete), :7264 (release signature, no owner discrimination); pendingExits as shared guard: :5143/:5153 (runScanInner claim), :5251 (runScanInner finally delete), :2852/:2864 (runExitCheck claim), :2901 (runExitCheck finally delete), :4986 and :2755 (top-of-loop has() skip gates); separate overlap-permitting guards :6158, :6220 (scan timer), :6237, :6239 (exit-check timer); flatten allowed while running and loop does not check pendingExits: src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:24-126 (no engine-stop gate), :127-136 (sells unconditionally), :126 (reserveManualFlatten call), :213 (flattenRelease(flattenSold)); posMap delete only for sold symbols on release: src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7276-7279.

### 49. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] reconcileBrokerSideExit matches the closing fill only by exact in-memory qty within a 1-hour window

- **Lane:** engine-scan-concurrency · **Category:** data · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3408-3432, 3446-3474`

**Description.** When a position vanishes from the broker, reconcileBrokerSideExit picks the SELL fill by `o.symbol === symbol && o.side==='sell' && status==='filled' && Number(o.filledQty) === expectedPos.qty && filledAt within last hour` (3415-3424). expectedPos.qty is the in-memory tracked qty, which syncPositionMapFromBroker may have already partially adjusted, and the broker-side stop could have filled in multiple child fills or a slightly different qty. If no order matches exactly (qty mismatch, fill older than 1h after a server outage, or multiple same-qty sells in the hour picking the wrong one), the function logs a warning and inserts NOTHING — the realized exit is silently never written to trader_trades, so realized P&L (and tax) permanently understates that closed loss/gain.

**Impact.** Broker-side exits whose fill qty doesn't exactly equal the stale in-memory qty, or that fill more than an hour before the next sync (e.g. after an overnight outage), are never logged as realized trades — realized P&L and tax reporting silently omit them.

**Suggested fix.** Match on symbol + side + a qty tolerance / sum of child fills, widen the window to cover realistic outage gaps (reuse RECONCILE_LOOKBACK_MS), and on no-match still insert a best-effort reconciled row using the broker's most recent matching sell rather than dropping it.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves as described. reconcileBrokerSideExit (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3401-3489) matches the closing SELL by symbol + side==='sell' + status==='filled' + Number(o.filledQty) === expectedPos.qty + filledAt within a hardcoded 1-hour window (lines 3415-3424), and on no match it logs a warning and returns without inserting anything (3426-3432), so the realized exit is never written to trader_trades — permanently understating realized P&L and tax. Crucially, this is the ONLY reconciliation path for broker-side exits. The engine places broker-resident GTC stop orders (placeDisasterStops 6749-6752, placeSafetyStops 6791-6794, syncBrokerStops 6574/6603) with type:'stop', timeInForce:'gtc' and NO logTrade call, so they create no PENDING trader_trades row. The robust 7-day reconcilePendingTrades (3512-3535, using RECONCILE_LOOKBACK_MS) only handles engine-placed orders that already have a PENDING row with a broker_order_id; it does not cover a GTC stop that fires while the engine is offline/halted. reconcileBrokerSideExit is invoked only when a symbol fully disappears from the broker (syncPositionMapFromBroker 3270-3293), and scan/sync paths bail when !isMarketOpen() (2722, 3679, 4000). So a GTC protective stop firing overnight/over a weekend/during a halt is reconciled at the next market-hours sync — which can easily be >1h after the broker fill timestamp, causing the no-match drop. The codebase already learned exactly this lesson for the PENDING reconciler: its window was widened 24h→7d (RECONCILE_LOOKBACK_MS, comment 3516-3522) after a real 2026-06-09 incident where a 50h halt aged rows out. The 1h window here is the un-fixed twin of that bug, and the proposed fix (reuse RECONCILE_LOOKBACK_MS, match on qty tolerance/child-fill sum) is concrete and idiomatic. Two sub-claims are overstated but do not refute the core defect: (1) the 'stale in-memory qty already partially adjusted by syncPositionMapFromBroker' mechanism is inaccurate — the disappearance loop (3270-3308) runs BEFORE the qty-update loop (3311-3360), and a fully-gone symbol never reaches the qty adjustment, so expectedPos.qty is the last-synced full qty which generally matches a full-position GTC stop; (2) 'multiple child fills / slightly different qty' is weak because Alpaca aggregates child fills into the parent order's filled_qty, and a genuine partial fill leaves the position partly on the broker (status partially_filled, not 'filled'), so this path would not even trigger. The dominant, real failure mode is the 1-hour window vs. an engine that only syncs during market hours. Impact is genuine financial-data integrity (realized P&L + tax permanently omit a closed trade), but the trigger is narrow (broker-side GTC stop firing >1h before the next market-hours sync), so P3 is appropriate.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3415-3424 (exact filledQty match + 1h window); 3426-3432 (no-match → warn + return, no insert); 3409 (oneHourAgo hardcoded); 3270-3293 (reconcileBrokerSideExit only fired on full disappearance, scoped by client present); 6749-6752 / 6791-6794 / 6574 (GTC protective stops placed with no logTrade → no PENDING row); 3512-3535 + 3516-3522 (reconcilePendingTrades covers only PENDING-row orders, uses 7-day RECONCILE_LOOKBACK_MS, comment documents the 24h→7d widening after the 2026-06-09 halt incident); 387 (RECONCILE_LOOKBACK_MS = 7 days, available to reuse); 2722/3679/4000 (scan paths return when !isMarketOpen, so sync only runs in market hours).

### 50. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] selectExternalSymbolsForTactical does not filter on signal freshness; relies solely on end-of-scan cleanup, admitting up-to-30-min-stale externals into the trade universe

- **Lane:** engine-swap-graduation · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6482-6496, 5671-5675, 4772-4773`

**Description.** selectExternalSymbolsForTactical filters externals only by direction (BUY/STRONG_BUY) and confidence rank, with no check on s.receivedAt. Freshness is enforced only by the end-of-scan cleanup at line 5673 (drops signals older than 30 min). Because cleanup runs at the END of each scan, the externals consumed at the START of a scan (line 4772) can be up to one full scan-interval older than 30 min (e.g. a long ~15-45 min hybrid scan means a signal received at T can still be selected at T+~45min on the following scan before cleanup fires). These feed both the scan universe and, indirectly, the deferred swap-sell candidate pool, so stale screener signals can drive buys.

**Impact.** The engine can analyze/act on screener signals well past their intended 30-min validity, including redeploying freed capital into symbols whose actionable window has closed. Magnitude bounded by scan duration but contradicts the documented 30-min expiry.

**Suggested fix.** Filter on `now - s.receivedAt < EXTERNAL_SIGNAL_TTL_MS` inside selectExternalSymbolsForTactical (pass `now`), so staleness is enforced at consumption time rather than only at the post-scan cleanup.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The finding is accurate on all structural points. (1) selectExternalSymbolsForTactical (src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6482-6496) filters externals only on direction (BUY/STRONG_BUY at line 6492), universe-dedup, and confidence rank — there is genuinely no receivedAt/freshness check, and no EXTERNAL_SIGNAL_TTL_MS constant exists (only inline `30 * 60 * 1000` literals). (2) It is invoked at the START of each scan (runScan: line 4772; tactical-smart: lines 4192, 4336), while the only staleness cleanup runs at the END of each scan (runScan: 5673-5674; tactical: 3970-3971; tactical-smart: 4611-4612), all dropping `now - s.receivedAt >= 30*60*1000`. With scans firing every SWING_SCAN_MS = 15 min (line 382), a signal that is just-under-30-min at scan N's end-cleanup survives and is consumed at scan N+1's start ~15 min later, i.e. at up to ~45 min of age — matching the finding's claimed magnitude. (3) A stale external signal can directly drive a buy: line 5262 computes `shouldBuy = (signal === BUY || signal === STRONG_BUY || !!extSignal) && marketHealthy`, so the `|| !!extSignal` term (extSignal found at 5259-5261, also without a freshness filter) makes a stale screener BUY sufficient even when the fresh per-symbol analyzeHybrid (4956) returns HOLD/SELL. The deferred swap-sell pool is populated from the same loop (5282), so stale externals indirectly reach the redeploy path too. The impact is real but bounded: per-symbol bars/analysis are re-fetched fresh every scan (4949, 4956) so pricing/sizing/stops use current data — only the entry direction-gate can be carried by the stale signal; passesSmartFilters (5312), marketHealthy, and canPlaceBuyOrder gates still apply; and pushExternalSignal dedup (6926) keeps still-actionable symbols refreshed, so staleness only bites for symbols the screener has stopped pushing. This is a genuine consistency gap (acts on signals modestly past their documented 30-min validity) but with limited magnitude and multiple downstream guards. P3 is appropriate — not inflated, not a false positive.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6491-6495 (filter on direction+confidence only, no receivedAt); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4772 (consume at runScan start); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4192,4336 (consume at tactical-smart start); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5673-5674, 3970-3971, 4611-4612 (cleanup only at scan end, `now - receivedAt < 30*60*1000`); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:382 (SWING_SCAN_MS = 15 min, so ~45-min worst-case consumption age); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5259-5262 (`shouldBuy = (... || !!extSignal) && marketHealthy` — stale ext signal alone can trigger buy); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4949,4956 (fresh per-symbol bars + analyzeHybrid mitigates pricing/sizing); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5282 (deferred swap-sell pool fed from same loop); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6926 (30-min push dedup keeps actionable symbols refreshed)

### 51. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Swap-sell redeploy sizes order and sets entry/stop from the stale per-symbol price captured earlier in the same scan loop

- **Lane:** engine-swap-graduation · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5282-5289, 5617-5652`

**Description.** Deferred candidates capture currentPrice at the moment their symbol was analyzed in the main loop (line 5285). The swap-sell block runs after the entire symbol loop, which can take minutes (per-symbol analyzeHybrid + 100ms sleeps + smart-filter I/O over up to ~100 symbols). It then uses candFull.currentPrice for the limit price (line 5617), entryPrice (5645), peakPrice (5646), stopLoss = candFull.currentPrice*(1-stopLossPct) (5647) and takeProfit (5648) with no re-quote. If the symbol moved up materially since capture, the limit at price*1.001 may not fill; if it fills, the in-memory entry/stop/take-profit are anchored to a price that no longer reflects the fill, so the first runExitCheck can mis-evaluate the stop (e.g. spurious stop-loss if the real fill was well above the stale entry).

**Impact.** Mispriced redeploy entries: unfillable limits (capital stays idle, defeating the swap-sell purpose) or positions whose in-memory stop/take-profit are anchored to a stale price, producing premature or delayed protective exits on the next poll.

**Suggested fix.** Re-fetch a fresh quote for each swap-sell attempt right before placing the order and recompute qty, limit, stop, take-profit, entry and peak from it (the in-loop entry path uses the just-analyzed price, so it is fresh; the deferred path must re-quote).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The mechanism the finding describes is real and present. Deferred swap-sell candidates capture currentPrice (= analysis.price) at the moment the symbol is analyzed inside the main loop (currentPrice assigned at line 4957, pushed into deferredCandidates at line 5285). The swap-sell block (gated by `swapMode === "enabled" && exitsThisScan > 0`) runs only after the full `for (const symbol of symbols)` loop completes — that loop iterates SCAN_UNIVERSE (~30) plus screener externals (~50, line 4773), each doing fetchBars + analyzeHybrid + async smart-filter I/O, so the captured price can be minutes stale by the time the block runs. The block never re-quotes: limitPrice (5617), entryPrice (5645), peakPrice (5646), stopLoss=candFull.currentPrice*(1-stopLossPct) (5647) and takeProfit (5648) all derive from candFull.currentPrice. I also verified the staleness is NOT self-corrected later: syncPositionMapFromBroker (lines 3311-3360) refreshes an existing position's qty/peakPrice/currentPrice/takeProfit/trailingStopPct but does NOT touch entryPrice or stopLoss (it only reads existing.entryPrice at 3348), so the stale anchor persists indefinitely rather than reconciling to the broker's avgEntryPrice. However, the finding's most severe claimed impact is mischaracterized. The order is a `day` LIMIT at candFull.currentPrice*1.001 (lines 5617, 5622-5623), so it can only fill AT OR BELOW that limit. The claim "spurious stop-loss if the real fill was well above the stale entry" is impossible for a limit buy — the fill price cannot exceed the limit. If the symbol moved up materially the order simply does not fill (benign idle-capital outcome; the PENDING row is later cleaned by reconcilePendingTrades/reconcileBrokerSideExit). If it does fill, the real entry is <= the stale entry, so the stale in-memory entryPrice is if anything too HIGH, which makes the stop slightly TIGHTER (a mild premature-exit risk), not a spurious immediate stop-out. Additionally, the in-loop main-scan entry path has the identical no-re-quote behavior (uses currentPrice from line 4957 for sizing at 5326, limit at 5375, and entry/peak at 5455-5456), so the swap-sell path is not categorically worse — only more stale because it is last. Net: confirmed real defect (no re-quote, entryPrice/stopLoss never reconciled), but the headline exit-mispricing scenario does not occur with a day limit; real impacts are missed redeploys and a possibly-too-tight stop. P3 is appropriate (arguably generous).

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4957 (currentPrice = analysis.price captured per-symbol); :5282-5289 (deferredCandidates.push with that currentPrice); :5528 (swap-sell block runs post-loop, gated on exitsThisScan>0); :5575-5577 (loop over plan.attempts after the symbol loop at :4937-5516); :5617 (limitPrice = candFull.currentPrice*1.001); :5622-5623 (type "limit", timeInForce "day"); :5642-5652 (positionMap.set with entryPrice/peakPrice/stopLoss/takeProfit all from candFull.currentPrice, no re-quote); :3311-3360 (syncPositionMapFromBroker updates qty/peak/currentPrice/takeProfit/trailingStopPct for existing positions but never entryPrice or stopLoss; :3348 only reads existing.entryPrice); :3811/4110/4858 (sync call sites — confirm runScan reconciles via this fn). In-loop entry parity (same staleness): :5326, :5375, :5455-5456.

### 52. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Wash-sale / losing-reentry refresh queries and loadTaxStatus run on the unbounded db pool with no statement timeout inside the per-BUY gate

- **Lane:** engine-wash-reentry · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** uncertain · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:1562-1577, 1587-1613, 1648-1665, 897-901`

**Description.** refreshWashSaleBlockedSymbols (1594-1611), refreshLosingReentryBlockedSymbols (1650-1663) and loadTaxStatus (1564-1568) all query via the raw `db` proxy, not via withTimeout(). The shared pool in src/lib/db/index.ts (lines 17-21) sets max/idle/connect_timeout but no statement_timeout, and there is no global statement_timeout (grep confirms statement_timeout only appears inside withTimeout's SET LOCAL). canPlaceBuyOrder awaits maybeRefreshWashSaleSet then maybeRefreshLosingReentrySet at the very top of the gate (lines 899-901), which sits in the hot path of every BUY inside the scan loop. A slow/locked trader_trades query therefore has no upper bound and stalls the scan's buy decisions indefinitely.

**Impact.** A degraded DB (lock contention, bloat, replica lag) can hang a scan inside the BUY gate with no timeout, delaying or blocking all order placement for that engine and the rest of the scan's risk/exit bookkeeping that follows. Reliability/availability hit on the live trading loop.

**Suggested fix.** Wrap these three queries in withTimeout(<= a few seconds) (or set a statement_timeout on the engine's DB usage) and treat a timeout the same as the existing catch in maybeRefresh* — keep the previous set, do not bump lastRefreshAt, retry next scan. This preserves the fail-soft semantics while bounding the stall.

**Verifier verdict:** `uncertain` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code exists and the core technical claim is accurate: loadTaxStatus (trading-engine.ts:1562-1577), refreshWashSaleBlockedSymbols (1587-1613) and refreshLosingReentryBlockedSymbols (1648-1665) all query via the raw `db` proxy, and there is no statement_timeout anywhere except inside withTimeout's SET LOCAL (verified by grep — only db/index.ts:42 and a comment in tax-report export). The pool (db/index.ts:17-21) sets max/idle_timeout/connect_timeout but no statement_timeout, and env.example's DATABASE_URL carries no statement_timeout option. canPlaceBuyOrder does await maybeRefreshWashSaleSet then maybeRefreshLosingReentrySet at the top (899-901) and is called per-symbol in scan loops (3912, 4292, 5405, 5605). So an unbounded query CAN hang a scan. But the finding's impact framing is overstated in two ways. (1) The "hot path of every BUY" claim is misleading: both maybeRefresh* helpers have a 5-minute age gate (WASH_SALE_REFRESH_MS / LOSING_REENTRY_REFRESH_MS = 5*60*1000, lines 722/737). On all but the first BUY in a 5-min window they return early after the cheap `age < REFRESH_MS` check (1626, 1675) WITHOUT touching the DB. The actual unbounded query fires at most once per 5 min per engine, and loadTaxStatus (called at 5906) runs once per scan setup, not per BUY. (2) Singling out these three queries is arbitrary: grep shows the ENTIRE engine uses raw `await db` (787, 2483, 2927, 2995, 3033, 5963, 5985, 7000, etc.) — withTimeout does not appear in trading-engine.ts at all. The CLAUDE.md withTimeout guidance is explicitly scoped to user-facing GET routes, not the periodic background engine loop. A degraded-DB stall here delays one background scan, not a user request; connect_timeout:5 bounds connection acquisition. This is a genuine-but-minor reliability gap that applies engine-wide, not the localized P2 the finding describes. Whether it merits a fix at all (vs. an engine-wide statement_timeout policy) is judgment-dependent, hence uncertain rather than a clean confirm or refute.

**Evidence:** src/lib/db/index.ts:17-21 (pool: max/idle_timeout/connect_timeout, no statement_timeout); src/lib/db/index.ts:42 (statement_timeout only via withTimeout SET LOCAL); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:1564,1594,1650 (raw `await db` in the three helpers); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:899-901 (canPlaceBuyOrder awaits both maybeRefresh* at top); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:1626,1675 (5-min age gate returns early before DB hit); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:722,737 (WASH_SALE_REFRESH_MS / LOSING_REENTRY_REFRESH_MS = 5 min); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:5906 (loadTaxStatus once per scan, not per BUY); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3912,4292,5405,5605 (canPlaceBuyOrder call sites in scan loops); .env.example:5 (DATABASE_URL has no statement_timeout option)

### 53. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Hybrid layers swallow all errors via empty catch, masking transient upstream failures as 'no data'

- **Lane:** hybrid-ai · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/hybrid/options-layer.ts:69-71, 126-128`

**Description.** applyOptionsFlowLayer catches with a bare `catch { return null; }` (lines 69-71) and fetchYahooOptions does the same (lines 126-128). sentiment-layer.ts (lines 96-98) and analyst-layer.ts (lines 109-111) follow the same pattern. There is no logging of err.message, so a Yahoo/Finnhub outage, parse error, or 429 is indistinguishable from a symbol legitimately having no options/news data. This violates the project rule against silently swallowing errors (log first, then degrade).

**Impact.** Operational blind spot: a provider outage silently disables the sentiment/options/analyst confidence adjustments for every symbol with no signal in logs, so the engine quietly runs on technicals-only while appearing healthy. Hard to diagnose post-incident.

**Suggested fix.** Log err.message (distinguishing abort/timeout, non-200, and parse failures) before returning null, matching ai-scoring-layer.ts which at least logs failures. Graceful degradation is correct; the silence is the defect.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> Every factual claim in the finding checks out against the code. options-layer.ts has bare `catch { return null; }` at lines 69-71 (applyOptionsFlowLayer) and 126-128 (fetchYahooOptions); sentiment-layer.ts at 96-98 (applySentimentLayer) and 154-156 (scoreSentimentWithAI); analyst-layer.ts at 109-111 (applyAnalystLayer). None of these catch blocks log err.message, so a Yahoo/Finnhub 429/timeout/parse error is indistinguishable from a symbol legitimately having no options/news/analyst data. The contrast with ai-scoring-layer.ts is accurate: that layer imports createRouteLogger and logs distinct failure modes — AbortError/timeout (line 214), generic failure with err.message (line 216), JSON parse failure (line 173), invalid signal (line 183), and direction-flip (line 190). The three sibling layers log nothing. Default config enables these layers (HYBRID_CONFIG.sentimentEnabled/optionsFlowEnabled/analystEnabled = `process.env.X !== "false"`, i.e. ON by default; only aiScoringEnabled defaults OFF), and the engine's analyzeHybrid path runs them, so the observability gap affects live operation, not just an unused code path. This does violate the project rule "Never silently swallow with empty .catch()... log first." The severity is correctly P3: these layers are bounded confidence *adjustments* (clamped to ±0.10 options, ±0.15 sentiment, ±0.08 analyst), not gating/sizing/safety decisions; pipeline.ts:87 already wraps them in Promise.allSettled and degrades gracefully to technicals-only on failure, so there is no correctness, financial-safety, or cross-tenant consequence — purely a diagnostic blind spot. The description and claimed impact never overstate this. Confirmed at the stated severity.

**Evidence:** src/lib/hybrid/options-layer.ts:69-71 (`} catch {\n    return null;\n  }`) and :126-128 (same in fetchYahooOptions); src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:96-98 and :154-156; src/lib/hybrid/analyst-layer.ts:109-111 — all bare catch, no logging. Contrast: src/lib/hybrid/ai-scoring-layer.ts:8 (`const log = createRouteLogger("ai-scoring")`), :173, :183, :190, :213-217 (logs abort vs generic err.message). Enabled by default: src/lib/config.ts:125-127 (`sentimentEnabled: process.env.HYBRID_SENTIMENT_ENABLED !== "false"`, etc.). Graceful-degradation mitigation: src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:87 (`Promise.allSettled`) and :92-110 (null/rejected → layer simply omitted, adjustment skipped at :116-124).

### 54. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Layer caches (sentiment/options/analyst) grow unbounded across the active-symbol set; size cap only evicts already-expired entries

- **Lane:** hybrid-ai · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:31-39`

**Description.** setCache only runs an eviction sweep when size > 200, and that sweep deletes ONLY entries whose expiry has already passed (`if (now > v.expiry)`). With the screener scanning hundreds of symbols within the 10-minute TTL window, the map can hold >200 simultaneously-live entries and the sweep removes none of them, so the 200 cap is not actually enforced — it is only soft-bounded by the TTL. The identical pattern exists in options-layer.ts (lines 29-37) and analyst-layer.ts (lines 42-50, 1-hour TTL, so it stays full longer).

**Impact.** Per-process memory for these globalThis maps is bounded by the live working set rather than the intended 200-entry cap; under a large universe the analyst cache (1h TTL) in particular can hold far more than 200 entries indefinitely. Slow growth / higher steady-state memory, not corruption.

**Suggested fix.** When size exceeds the cap after the expired-sweep, also evict the oldest/nearest-expiry live entries (LRU or expiry-sorted) down to the cap, so the bound holds regardless of TTL.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code behaves exactly as claimed. In all three layers, setCache only triggers a sweep when size > 200, and the sweep deletes ONLY already-expired entries (`if (now > v.expiry) delete`). There is no eviction of live entries down to the cap, so a working set of >200 simultaneously-fresh symbols is never trimmed — the 200 "cap" is only soft-bounded by the TTL, not enforced. This violates the project's own globalThis-bounding rule ("A TTL gates freshness, not memory"). The finding's stated trigger is partly WRONG but the mechanism is still real via a larger caller. The screener does NOT populate these caches: SCREENER_HYBRID_OPTIONS (screener.ts:22-24) sets enableSentiment/enableOptionsFlow/enableAnalyst:false, and the layers return before setCache when disabled. However, the engine runScan (trading-engine.ts:4772-4773) scans `[...SCAN_UNIVERSE, ...externals]` where SCAN_UNIVERSE = the S&P 500 (~470 fallback symbols, refreshed to the live >400-symbol list via getSP500Symbols). Each symbol runs analyzeHybrid → all three layers (default-ON for Trader+ tier; options uses free Yahoo unconditionally, sentiment/analyst when Finnhub configured, which it is in prod). So a single engine scan populates each map with ~500 fresh entries inside the 10-min (sentiment/options) and 1-hour (analyst) TTLs — the expired-only sweep removes none, confirming the cap is ineffective. Impact is real but minor and the finding itself characterizes it correctly ("Slow growth / higher steady-state memory, not corruption"). It is NOT truly unbounded: the keyspace is the finite universe of analyzed symbols (S&P 500 + a few externals + per-symbol /api/analyze hits), entries are small structs (a few numbers + short reason strings), and stale symbols do eventually get TTL-evicted on access/sweep. Worst case is low-hundreds-of-KB per process held at the working-set size instead of 200. The defect is genuine (documented cap not enforced) so this is confirmed, but the supporting detail naming the screener as populator is inaccurate. P3 is appropriate — no leak-forever, no correctness/security impact.

**Evidence:** src/lib/hybrid/sentiment-layer.ts:31-39 (setCache: `if (size>200)` then sweep `if (now > v.expiry) delete` — live entries never evicted); src/lib/hybrid/options-layer.ts:29-37 (identical); src/lib/hybrid/analyst-layer.ts:42-50 (identical, CACHE_TTL_MS=60min at line 25 keeps it full longer). Populated via src/lib/hybrid/pipeline.ts:71-85 (analyzeHybrid calls all three layers when enabled). Engine population path: src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4772-4773 (`symbols = [...SCAN_UNIVERSE, ...externalSymbols]`), line 24 (`SCAN_UNIVERSE = SP500_SYMBOLS`), src/lib/sp500.ts:9-71 (~470 fallback symbols), sp500.ts:84-106 (live refresh requires length>400). Defaults ON: src/lib/config.ts:125-127. Finding's screener premise refuted: src/lib/screener.ts:22-24 (SCREENER_HYBRID_OPTIONS disables all three layers).

### 55. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Fibonacci levels ignore swing direction — downtrend retracements computed as if retracing from the high

- **Lane:** indicators · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/indicators/fibonacci.ts:82-96`

**Description.** The code comment (lines 82-83) explicitly states direction matters: 'if swing high came after swing low, price is trending up (retrace from high); if swing low came after swing high … retrace from low.' But the level computation unconditionally uses `price: swingHigh.price - ratio * range` (line 87) regardless of which swing occurred later. In a downtrend (swingLow.index > swingHigh.index) the levels should project upward from the low (swingLow.price + ratio*range), not retrace down from the high. The stated direction-handling is never implemented, so downtrend fib levels are wrong.

**Impact.** Display-only at present: fibonacci is surfaced solely as a 'Fib Levels Active' badge / level list in signal-details.tsx (lines 483-486) and is NOT fed into trading scoring, so no money impact today. But the returned `levels[].price` values shown to users are incorrect in any downtrend, and the field is part of AnalysisResult that future code could consume for entries.

**Suggested fix.** Branch on swing order: when swingLow.index > swingHigh.index (downtrend), compute price = swingLow.price + ratio*range; otherwise keep swingHigh.price - ratio*range. Use the swing dates/indices already captured.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The code defect is real and present exactly as described. fibonacci.ts:82-83 documents direction-dependent handling ("if swing low came after swing high, price is trending down (retrace from low)"), but the level computation at line 87 unconditionally uses `price: swingHigh.price - ratio * range` with no branch on swing order (swingHigh.index vs swingLow.index). The captured indices/dates are never used to choose direction. So in a downtrend the ratio-to-price label mapping is inverted relative to convention: 23.6% is assigned to a price near the high rather than near the low. (Note the *set* of seven horizontal prices it returns — from swingHigh at ratio 0 down to swingLow at ratio 1 — is the same set of lines in either direction; what is actually wrong is the label assigned to each price in a downtrend, not arbitrary nonsense prices. The proposed fix recomputes the same prices in reverse order, which corrects the label mapping.) The claimed impact is accurate and, if anything, the finding understates how inert it is: calculateFibLevels is called once in analyzer.ts:361 and attached to AnalysisResult.fibonacci (analyzer.ts:375). The only consumer is signal-details.tsx:483-486, which renders a static 'Fib Levels Active' badge and does NOT display the level prices at all. No scoring, no trading, no optimizer (analyzeSignalOnly explicitly skips fibonacci, analyzer.ts:382-384) consumes levels[].price. So zero money impact today and not even a visible incorrect number — purely a latent contract/labeling bug that future code could consume. P3 (numeric/correctness, no impact) is the correct severity; not inflated.

**Evidence:** src/lib/indicators/fibonacci.ts:82-88 (comment describes direction handling; line 87 `price: swingHigh.price - ratio * range` is unconditional — swing indices captured at lines 36/58 are never used to branch); src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:361,375 (only producer, attaches to result); src/components/dashboard/signal-details.tsx:483-486 (sole consumer — renders 'Fib Levels Active' badge, does not show level prices); src/lib/indicators/analyzer.ts:382-384 (analyzeSignalOnly skips fibonacci entirely); Grep for `.levels`/`fibonacci.` across **/*.tsx returned no matches and across **/*.ts only types, tests, and the analyzer producer — confirming levels[].price is never read for scoring or trading.

### 56. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Pearson correlation returns 0 (not undefined) for a flat/constant price series — masks concentration risk

- **Lane:** momentum-rs · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/correlation.ts:42-59`

**Description.** pearsonCorrelation computes den = sqrt((n*sumX2 - sumX^2)*(n*sumY2 - sumY^2)) and returns `den === 0 ? 0 : num/den`. den is 0 whenever either series has zero variance (a constant/flat close series — a halted stock, a stale feed repeating the last print, or a stablecoin-like instrument). Correlation is mathematically undefined there, but the code reports 0.0 = 'perfectly uncorrelated'. computeCorrelationMatrix then surfaces that 0 on the risk-correlation page (src/app/dashboard/risk-correlation/page.tsx).

**Impact.** A halted or flatlined holding is displayed as a diversifier (0 correlation) when its true relationship is unknown, understating portfolio concentration to the user. Display-only (no automated trading gate consumes the matrix), hence P3.

**Suggested fix.** Return null/NaN for the zero-variance case and render it as 'n/a' in the matrix UI, distinguishing 'undefined (flat series)' from a genuine 0 correlation. Optionally also drop constant series from the matrix with a warning.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. src/lib/correlation.ts:56-58 computes den = Math.sqrt((n*sumX2 - sumX^2)*(n*sumY2 - sumY^2)) and returns `den === 0 ? 0 : num / den`. For a zero-variance (perfectly flat) close series, den === 0, so the function returns 0 — mathematically the correlation is undefined there, yet it is reported as 0.0 ("uncorrelated"). The value flows through computeCorrelationMatrix (line 34) into /api/correlation (route.ts:57) and is rendered on two display pages. On the risk-correlation page that 0 is folded into avgCorrelation (page.tsx:86), inflating diversificationScore (line 89) and lowering riskLevel (line 90), so a flatlined holding would read as a diversifier and understate concentration — confirming the claimed impact direction. This is a genuine numeric defect matching the CLAUDE.md rule against fabricating a basis / returning a wrong sentinel for an undefined quantity. However the impact is correctly bounded to P3, and severity is not inflated: (1) the matrix is purely display-only — the ONLY callers of computeCorrelationMatrix are the /api/correlation GET route feeding two dashboard pages (correlation, risk-correlation); no trading gate, position sizing, or halt logic consumes it (verified by grep). (2) The triggering condition is practically rare: route.ts:39-45 fetches 90 daily bars and requires >=20, so den===0 needs an entire ~90-bar daily-close series to be PERFECTLY constant. Real equities (symbols are restricted to /^[A-Z]{1,10}$/ tickers via the Yahoo provider) effectively never have 90 identical daily closes; the "stablecoin" example in the finding does not apply since these are equity tickers, not crypto. A merely near-flat series produces a valid (if noisy) correlation, not the den===0 branch. The finding itself acknowledges display-only and self-scopes to P3, which is the correct floor.

**Evidence:** src/lib/correlation.ts:56-58 (`const den = Math.sqrt((n * sumX2 - sumX * sumX) * (n * sumY2 - sumY * sumY)); return den === 0 ? 0 : num / den;`); src/lib/correlation.ts:34 (matrix populated from pearsonCorrelation); src/app/api/correlation/route.ts:10-16,39,45,57 (display-only GET, tier-gated, fetchBars 90 "1d", requires bars.length>=20); src/app/dashboard/risk-correlation/page.tsx:86,89-90 (0 dilutes avgCorrelation -> diversificationScore/riskLevel); src/app/dashboard/correlation/page.tsx:35-43 (second display-only consumer); grep confirms computeCorrelationMatrix/pearsonCorrelation have no other callers (no engine/sizing/halt consumer).

### 57. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Momentum analyzer treats the live, unclosed minute bar as a confirmed breakout (partial-bar / look-ahead)

- **Lane:** momentum-rs · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:90-145, 182-197`

**Description.** detectBullFlag uses bars[n-1] (the current bar) as the breakout bar: it requires current.close > consHigh (line 110) and computes volumeMultiple = current.volume / avgConsVol (line 129). The only live caller, src/app/api/momentum/analyze/route.ts:68-82, fetches bars via fetchMinuteBars(symbol, today, today), and Polygon's 1-minute aggregates for the current day include the in-progress (unclosed) minute. Early in a forming minute the bar's volume is only partially accumulated and its close is provisional, so volumeSurge flickers false→true→false and a 'confirmed breakout' close can revert before the minute closes. The analyzer also runs all indicators (EMA9/VWAP/RSI) including this partial bar (lines 182-197), so the precondition gates (price>VWAP, price>EMA9, RSI band) are evaluated on a mid-minute snapshot.

**Impact.** Signal/confidence shown to the trader (and any future engine consumer) is computed on a non-final bar; a STRONG_BUY can appear mid-minute on a breakout that fully reverses by bar close, leading a user to chase a fake-out. Not currently wired to auto-order placement, so impact is a misleading signal rather than a wrong live order.

**Suggested fix.** Evaluate the breakout on the last CLOSED bar: either drop the final bar when its timestamp is within the current minute, or have the route request bars only up to the prior completed minute. Document that detectBullFlag assumes bars[n-1] is closed, and have the live route enforce it.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The technical mechanism is real and the code behaves exactly as described. detectBullFlag uses bars[n-1] as the breakout bar: momentum-analyzer.ts:91 sets `current = bars[n-1]`, line 110 gates the breakout on `current.close <= consHigh`, and line 129 computes `volumeMultiple = current.volume / avgConsVol`. The indicator loop (lines 182-188) updates EMA9/VWAP/RSI/ATR over every bar including the last, and the preconditions (lines 202-232) are evaluated against `last = bars[bars.length-1]` (line 190). The look-ahead source is confirmed: fetchMinuteBars (polygon.ts:120-149) requests `/v2/aggs/.../range/1/minute/{toYmd(from)}/{toYmd(to)}` with from=to=today (route.ts:68-71) and maps every Polygon result straight to a Bar with NO trailing-bar drop, NO timestamp-vs-current-minute guard, and NO closed-bar filtering anywhere in the chain. Polygon's current-day minute aggregates include the in-progress minute, so during RTH bars[n-1] is a provisional, partially-accumulated bar — volumeSurge and the breakout-close gate are evaluated mid-minute and can flip before close. So the finding is real, not a false positive. However, the severity is inflated. I down-grade P2→P3 because the impact is purely a display-only, user-initiated signal: Grep shows analyzeMomentumBars/the route are consumed ONLY by the dashboard page, the route, and tests — fetchMinuteBars has exactly one caller. There is no engine or order-placement consumer (the finding concedes this). The page is explicitly framed 'Pure browsing — no orders are placed from this page' (page.tsx:217) with an 'Educational only ... does not place orders' disclaimer (page.tsx:485-491), and the user must manually click Analyze per symbol. So the worst case is a flickering/revertible signal shown on an educational, order-free page — a correctness/data-freshness glitch in a display, not a money-moving, cross-tenant, or auth defect. That is P3, not P2.

**Evidence:** src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:91 (const current = bars[n-1]); :110 (if (current.close <= consHigh) continue); :129 (volumeMultiple = current.volume / avgConsVol); :140 (breakoutPrice: current.close); :182-188 (indicator loop over all bars incl. last); :190 (last = bars[bars.length-1]); :202-232 (VWAP/EMA9/RSI preconditions on `price`/`rsiVal` derived from last bar). src/app/api/momentum/analyze/route.ts:68-71 (const today = new Date(); fetchMinuteBars(symbol, today, today)); :82 (analyzeMomentumBars). src/lib/providers/polygon.ts:120-149 (fetchMinuteBars: range/1/minute/{toYmd(from)}/{toYmd(to)}, maps b→Bar with no trailing-bar/timestamp filter). Grep: fetchMinuteBars has one caller (route.ts:71); analyzeMomentumBars/MomentumResult consumed only by src/app/dashboard/momentum/page.tsx (UI), the route, and tests/unit/momentum-analyzer.test.ts — no engine/order path. src/app/dashboard/momentum/page.tsx:217 ('no orders are placed from this page'), :485-491 (Educational only / does not place orders).

### 58. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Stop/take-profit exits fill at the exact trigger level, ignoring gaps (optimistic fills)

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/optimizer.ts:492-502,516-522`

**Description.** When a stop is hit, exitPrice is set to the trigger level itself: line 493 `exitPrice = Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)` and line 501 the hard take-profit `exitPrice = pos.takeProfitPrice`. Only a fixed per-side slippage (SLIP = 5bps, line 520 `exitFill = exitPrice * (1 - SLIP)`) is applied. A bar that gaps far below the stop (bar.low << stopLoss) still fills at the stop level rather than near bar.open/bar.low, and a gap-up through the take-profit fills at the TP rather than higher. Real overnight gaps on daily bars routinely blow through stops; the backtester assumes a fill that wasn't available.

**Impact.** Downside (stop) exits are systematically overstated and gap risk is invisible, inflating backtest totalReturn and Sharpe. The GA is selected on these inflated numbers, so the persisted live params (stopLossPct, trailingStopPct) are tuned to a rosier risk picture than live trading delivers.

**Suggested fix.** Fill stop exits at min(stop, bar.open) when bar.open < stop (gap down) and TP exits at max(tp, bar.open) on gap-ups, before applying SLIP. This models gap-through fills and removes the systematic optimism.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described in portfolioBacktest. optimizer.ts:492-493 fills a stop hit at the exact trigger level (exitPrice = Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop)) whenever bar.low crosses it — it never consults bar.open, so a gap-down bar (bar.open << stopLoss) still fills at the stop, not near the open/low. Line 520 then applies only a fixed 5bps SLIP (SLIP = BACKTEST_COSTS.slippageBps/10000, slippageBps=5). The Bar type (src/types/index.ts:13-20) carries open/high/low/close, so the gap data needed for the proposed fix is present and deliberately unused. These are daily bars (CLAUDE.md: multi-year backtests are daily-only), which gap overnight routinely, so for a long-only system the gap-down-through-stop fills are systematically optimistic, biasing totalReturn/Sharpe upward, and the GA selects persisted live params (stopLossPct, trailingStopPct) on those inflated numbers — the claimed impact is real and directional. Two qualifications lower the severity from P2 to P3: (1) the hard take-profit path the finding cites at lines 500-501 is effectively dead — graduationEnabled is hardcoded true (line 470) and the graduation branch at line 471 intercepts every bar.high >= takeProfitPrice crossing first, exiting at bar.close (line 482), so the `!graduationEnabled` guard on line 500 never passes in portfolioBacktest; only the stop side (492-493) is live; (2) this is a known, documented modeling simplification, not an oversight — config.ts:114-117 explicitly states the fill is modeled as "a bar close or an exact stop/TP level" plus fixed slippage. It is an offline research-tooling fidelity gap (it biases GA param selection optimistically) rather than a live-execution correctness or security defect — live trades use real broker fills, not this code. Real and worth fixing, but confined to backtest fidelity with one cited path dormant, so P3 rather than P2.

**Evidence:** src/lib/optimizer.ts:492-493 (stop fills at exact Math.max(pos.stopLoss, trailStop) when bar.low crosses, ignoring bar.open); src/lib/optimizer.ts:500-501 (hard TP fills at pos.takeProfitPrice — but guarded by !graduationEnabled which is always false); src/lib/optimizer.ts:470-487 (graduationEnabled=true always intercepts the TP crossing first, exiting at bar.close, making the 500-501 branch dead for the GA); src/lib/optimizer.ts:520 (exitFill = exitPrice * (1 - SLIP), only fixed slippage); src/lib/optimizer.ts:108 (SLIP = BACKTEST_COSTS.slippageBps/10000); src/lib/config.ts:113-118 (slippageBps:5; comment documents fill modeled as "a bar close or an exact stop/TP level" plus fixed slippage); src/types/index.ts:13-20 (Bar has open/high/low/close — gap data available but unused for fills)

### 59. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Fetch-timeout race timers are never cleared (orphan timers per symbol fetch)

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/optimizer.ts:228-231,256-259`

**Description.** Both incremental and full fetch paths use Promise.race against a setTimeout-based reject timer (lines 229-230 and 257-258) but never capture or clearTimeout the handle. When fetchBars resolves first (the common case), the 10s reject timer keeps running and holds the rejecting promise alive; for a 500-symbol sp500 universe that is hundreds of live timers and unhandled rejections being scheduled per run. Per CLAUDE.md's Timer & Resource Cleanup rule, every setTimeout paired with await should clearTimeout in finally.

**Impact.** Minor resource leak and a burst of orphan timers / potential unhandled-rejection noise during each optimization run; not a correctness bug but a reliability/cleanliness gap in a CPU- and memory-sensitive worker.

**Suggested fix.** Capture the timer id and clearTimeout it once the race settles: `const t = setTimeout(...); try { return await Promise.race([provider.fetchBars(...), timeoutPromise]); } finally { clearTimeout(t); }` (or use AbortController on the fetch).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present exactly as described. src/lib/optimizer.ts lines 228-231 and 256-259 both build `Promise.race([provider.fetchBars(...), new Promise((_, reject) => setTimeout(() => reject(new Error("timeout")), 10000))])` and the setTimeout handle is never captured or cleared — when fetchBars wins (the common case) the 10s reject timer keeps running. This is a genuine violation of CLAUDE.md's "Timer & Resource Cleanup" rule (every setTimeout paired with await should clearTimeout in finally), and the proposed try/finally clearTimeout fix is correct. So it is a real, fixable cleanliness gap, not a non-issue — false_positive would be wrong. However, the claimed *impact* is materially overstated on two points: (1) "unhandled rejections / unhandled-rejection noise" is incorrect — Promise.race attaches an internal reaction handler to every input promise, and that handler persists after the race settles; the late timeout rejection is therefore handled (ignored) by the race, not surfaced as an unhandledRejection. (2) "hundreds of live timers per run" is overstated: FETCH_CONCURRENCY = 3 (line 132) and fetchAllBars processes symbols in batches of 3 via Promise.allSettled (line 277), so at most ~3 orphan timers are pending simultaneously, each self-clearing within 10s with no accumulation. Over a 500-symbol sp500 run hundreds of timers are *created* sequentially, but they do not co-exist or leak unboundedly. Net effect is a small, self-resolving resource/cleanliness inefficiency in a CPU/memory-sensitive worker. P3 is the correct severity; the defect is real but the impact narrative (unhandled rejections + hundreds of concurrent live timers) is inflated.

**Evidence:** src/lib/optimizer.ts:228-231 (incremental-fetch Promise.race with uncaptured setTimeout reject timer); src/lib/optimizer.ts:256-259 (full-fetch Promise.race with uncaptured setTimeout reject timer); src/lib/optimizer.ts:132 (FETCH_CONCURRENCY = 3 — bounds concurrent orphan timers to ~3, refuting "hundreds of live timers"); src/lib/optimizer.ts:275-277 (fetchAllBars batches by FETCH_CONCURRENCY via Promise.allSettled); src/lib/optimizer.ts:248-250 and 262-265 (catch blocks swallow the timeout rejection of the race itself; the late timer rejection is absorbed by Promise.race's retained reaction handler, so no unhandledRejection is emitted)

### 60. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] rsThreshold momentum filter is a 59-bar lookback labeled 60-day (off-by-one)

- **Lane:** optimizer-ga · **Category:** logic · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/optimizer.ts:566-568`

**Description.** The relative-strength entry filter computes `rs60 = (w[w.length-1].close - w[w.length-60].close)/w[w.length-60].close` after gating on `w.length >= 60`. With w.length exactly 60, w[length-60] = w[0] and w[length-1] = w[59], i.e. a 59-interval return, not 60. The same window is shift-trimmed to WINDOW_SIZE=90, so on longer windows it is a true 60-bar span, making the lookback inconsistent depending on how long the position window has been warming.

**Impact.** The rsThreshold gate (a tuned GA parameter, PARAM_RANGES line 104) is evaluated against a slightly different lookback than its name/intent implies and varies with window warm-up, marginally shifting which symbols pass the entry filter and thus the tuned threshold. Low magnitude.

**Suggested fix.** Use `w[w.length-61]` for a true 60-bar return (and require w.length>=61), or rename/redocument as a 59-bar window; keep it constant regardless of window length.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The mechanical claim is accurate. At src/lib/optimizer.ts:566-568 the RS filter computes rs60 = (w[last].close - w[last-60].close)/w[last-60].close after gating on w.length >= 60. When w.length === 60, w[w.length-60] is w[0] and w[w.length-1] is w[59] — index distance 59, i.e. a 59-interval return, not 60. The window is a rolling Bar[] grown by w.push(bar) and trimmed only when w.length > WINDOW_SIZE (90) via w.shift() (lines 438-440), so once the window passes length 60 the same offset spans a true 60 intervals. rsThreshold is indeed a GA-tuned param (PARAM_RANGES line 104, range -0.10..0.10). So both the off-by-one and the window-length dependence exist exactly as described.\n\nHowever the impact is overstated, not understated. The window never resets mid-segment (only push + top-trim), so each symbol crosses w.length === 60 exactly ONCE per segment evaluation (and the test segment re-warms from empty at startIdx = data.trainEnd, line 408, so it crosses once more there). That means the 59-interval computation happens on exactly one bar per symbol per segment — every subsequent bar uses a true 60-interval span. It is a single-bar warm-up boundary artifact, not a sustained or pervasive inconsistency. The marginal difference between a 59- and 60-day daily return on one gating decision per symbol-segment is negligible, and the GA tunes rsThreshold against whatever the filter computes, so the threshold stays self-consistent within the optimizer. The same pattern appears in the compare route (compare/route.ts:141) and the live engine getRelativeStrength (trading-engine.ts:614), but the live array is typically already 90 bars so it is a true 60-interval span there. The far larger semantic divergence (optimizer/compare compute RAW return while the live engine subtracts SPY for true relative strength) is a separate issue this finding does not raise. Net: the bug is real but the practical effect is cosmetic. P3 is correct; it is at the bottom of P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/optimizer.ts:561 (if (!w || w.length < 60) continue); src/lib/optimizer.ts:566-568 (rs60 computation gated on w.length >= 60); src/lib/optimizer.ts:438-440 (rolling window: w.push(bar); if (w.length > WINDOW_SIZE) w.shift()); src/lib/optimizer.ts:123 (WINDOW_SIZE = 90); src/lib/optimizer.ts:104 (rsThreshold PARAM_RANGES -0.10..0.10); src/lib/optimizer.ts:408-409 (test segment startIdx = data.trainEnd, windows re-warm from empty); identical pattern at src/app/api/optimize/compare/route.ts:140-142 and src/lib/trading-engine.ts:614 (live, but raw-return-vs-SPY semantic differs)

### 61. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Daily and intraday scans share one untagged cache.results; consumers cannot tell 1d-bar from 5m-bar signals and POST can overwrite intraday with daily mid-session

- **Lane:** screener-sectors · **Category:** data · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/screener.ts:265-266, 384-385, 49-64 (ScreenerCache) vs src/app/api/screener/route.ts:42-60,106-114`

**Description.** Both runScanInternal (daily 90×1d) and runIntradayScanInternal (intraday 2d×5m) write the SAME `cache.results` and `cache.scannedAt` (screener.ts:265-266, 384-385). ScreenerResult (screener.ts:30-41) and ScreenerCache (49-64) carry NO timeframe field — only scanInFlightType marks the in-flight timeframe, which is cleared on completion (198, 317). The GET/POST routes (route.ts:44-54, 119-128) return cache.results + scannedAt with no timeframe indication, so a caller cannot tell whether the RSI/signal/ATR it received came from daily or 5m bars (which differ materially). Worse, the POST handler always runs scanAllSymbols() (DAILY) when stale (route.ts:111) even during market hours when the scheduler has populated cache.results with intraday data — overwriting intraday results with daily-bar results and resetting scannedAt, deferring the scheduler's next intraday refresh by up to 5 min.

**Impact.** Screener UI / downstream consumers can display or act on 5m-bar signals labeled identically to daily signals (and vice-versa). User-triggered POST during the session silently flips the shared results to a different timeframe. Confusing/incorrect screening output; not a direct money-loss path since the engine re-analyzes per symbol at trade time.

**Suggested fix.** Add a `resultsTimeframe: "daily" | "intraday"` field to ScreenerCache, set it alongside cache.results in both scan functions, and surface it in the API response. Have the POST stale-refresh choose the timeframe based on market hours (intraday during RTH) instead of always running the daily scan.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The described mechanism is present and behaves exactly as claimed. Both scan paths write the same untagged cache: runScanInternal (daily, 90×1d) at screener.ts:265-266 and runIntradayScanInternal (intraday, 2d×5m) at 384-385 set cache.results and cache.scannedAt identically. ScreenerResult (30-41) and ScreenerCache (49-64) carry no timeframe/resolution field; the only timeframe marker is scanInFlightType (62), which is reset to null on completion (198, 317), so it conveys nothing once a scan finishes. The GET (route.ts:42-60) and POST (119-128) responses return results + scannedAt with no timeframe indicator. The scheduler genuinely alternates the shared cache between timeframes during RTH: a daily scan at open, then scanAllSymbolsIntraday() every 5 min (screener.ts:499-503), so cache.results is 5m-bar data for most of the session. The POST stale path (cacheTtlSeconds=300) always calls scanAllSymbols() = daily (route.ts:110-111), so a user POST mid-session overwrites intraday results with daily-bar results and resets scannedAt, which pushes back the scheduler's next intraday fire (gated on ageMs >= intradayIntervalMs = 5 min). The downstream impact is real but low: the screener UI explicitly fetches its per-symbol detail at "90 days, daily bars … for consistency" (page.tsx:238-239), i.e. it assumes the table is daily data, yet renders RSI/ATR/signal with no timeframe label — so intraday-populated rows silently mismatch. The engine path is mitigated as the finding admits: pushScreenerSignals only forms a candidate universe (ACTIONABLE_SIGNALS + MIN_CONFIDENCE gate, trader-client.ts:40-42) and the engine re-analyzes each symbol at trade time, so no direct money loss. The finding self-rates P3 and explicitly disclaims a money path; the claim is neither fabricated nor materially overstated. I could not refute it.

**Evidence:** src/lib/screener.ts:265-266 (daily writes cache.results/scannedAt); 384-385 (intraday writes same); 30-41 ScreenerResult has no timeframe field; 49-64 ScreenerCache has no timeframe field, only scanInFlightType:62; 198,317 scanInFlightType reset to null on completion; 499-503 scheduler runs scanAllSymbolsIntraday every 5 min during RTH; 282 & 400 both push results to engine via pushScreenerSignals. src/app/api/screener/route.ts:44-54 GET returns results/scannedAt untagged; 110-111 POST stale-refresh always runs scanAllSymbols() (daily); 119-128 POST response untagged. src/app/dashboard/screener/page.tsx:238-239 UI per-symbol fetch hardcodes days=90&resolution=1d "for consistency", assuming the table is daily. src/lib/config.ts:58 cacheTtlSeconds=300, :60 intradayIntervalMs=5min. src/lib/trader-client.ts:40-42 candidate-only gate (engine re-analyzes at trade time, so no money path).

### 62. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Missed-daily-recovery date comparison relies on re-parsing an ET-localized string in the server's local TZ (round-trip via new Date(localeString))

- **Lane:** screener-sectors · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/screener.ts:489-497`

**Description.** The scheduler's missed-daily recovery builds `et = new Date(now.toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: "America/New_York" }))` (489-490), then derives `todayStr = et.toLocaleDateString("en-US")` WITHOUT a timeZone option (492), comparing it against `scanDateStr = cache.scannedAt.toLocaleDateString("en-US", { timeZone: "America/New_York" })` (491). This is the new Date(localeString) anti-pattern: `et` is the ET wall-clock numerals reinterpreted as server-local time, and todayStr formats it back in server-local — it round-trips the date only as long as Node's Date parser accepts the exact `M/D/YYYY, h:mm:ss AM` locale format. The two operands are also produced by inconsistent methods (one timezone-aware, one timezone-naive on a re-parsed Date). If the locale-string parse ever fails or the format shifts (Node/ICU version, locale data), `et` becomes Invalid Date and the comparison mis-fires — either re-running a full daily scan every 60s (quota burn) or never recovering a missed daily.

**Impact.** Fragile correctness for the once-per-day recovery path. Worst case: repeated full-universe daily rescans every minute (Yahoo/Finnhub quota burn) or a permanently-missed daily scan after a mid-session restart. Not money-loss; reliability/quota.

**Suggested fix.** Compute both date keys with a single timezone-aware formatter and compare strings directly, e.g. `const etDay = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-CA", { timeZone: "America/New_York", year:"numeric", month:"2-digit", day:"2-digit" });` then compare `etDay.format(now)` to `etDay.format(cache.scannedAt)`. Never re-parse a locale string with new Date().

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present verbatim at src/lib/screener.ts:489-492 and is exactly the "new Date(localeString)" anti-pattern the finding describes. `now.toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: "America/New_York" })` yields an ET wall-clock string (e.g. "6/17/2026, 9:45:00 AM"); `new Date(etStr)` reinterprets those numerals as server-local time; then `et.toLocaleDateString("en-US")` (NO timeZone option, line 492) reads them back, while the other operand `scanDateStr` (line 491) is timezone-aware directly on the real instant. The two operands are genuinely produced by inconsistent methods, and re-parsing a locale string with new Date() is explicitly a banned pattern (cf. the project's own date-based-cron-key timezone guidance). The proposed fix (single Intl.DateTimeFormat with timeZone, compare formatted strings) is correct and idiomatic. However, the *runtime impact* is latent, not active. Under current V8/ICU, the en-US toLocaleString output ("M/D/YYYY, h:mm:ss AM") parses reliably, so `et` carries the correct ET date numerals and the round-trip yields the same correct ET calendar date string as `scanDateStr` — the comparison works today. The "rescan every 60s" / "permanently missed daily" outcomes require `et` to become Invalid Date, which only happens on a hypothetical future Node/ICU/locale-data change, not on the present stack. Additional mitigations bound even that worst case: the interval guards `if (cache.scanning) return` (line 480) and awaits runDailyScan (line 495); scanAllSymbols sets cache.scannedAt = new Date() on completion (line 266), so a successful recovery is naturally one-shot per day (scanDateStr === todayStr on the next tick). Quota-burn would require the comparison to be *permanently* unequal, which the working parser prevents. Verdict: real, accurately-described latent fragility / banned anti-pattern with a correct fix, but no demonstrable present-day misbehavior. Reporter already rated it P3 reliability and stated "Not money-loss"; that severity is appropriate and not inflated. Confirming at P3 because the pattern and operand inconsistency are genuinely present and worth the trivial fix, while noting the claimed impact is conditioned on a future regression rather than current behavior.

**Evidence:** src/lib/screener.ts:489-490 (etStr via toLocaleString TZ + new Date(etStr) round-trip); :491 (scanDateStr timezone-aware) vs :492 (todayStr toLocaleDateString WITHOUT timeZone on re-parsed Date) — inconsistent operands; :493-497 comparison drives runDailyScan; :480 `if (cache.scanning) return` guard; :495 awaited recovery; :266 `cache.scannedAt = new Date()` set on scan completion makes recovery one-shot per day; :188 cache.scanning=true in scanAllSymbols.

### 63. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] DM thread GET performs a state-mutating write (clears unread) with no CSRF protection

- **Lane:** security-auth-tenant · **Category:** security · **Verifier:** uncertain · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/app/api/dm/threads/[id]/route.ts:82-86`

**Description.** GET /api/dm/threads/[id] unconditionally writes to the DB — it updates dmThreads.aLastSeenAt/bLastSeenAt to now() for the caller's side on every fetch (lines 83-86). GET handlers use only getSession() with no CSRF token check (by design, since GETs are normally side-effect-free). Because this GET mutates state and a simple cross-site GET (img/script/fetch) carries the session cookie without a CORS preflight, a malicious page can force a logged-in victim's unread badge for a known thread id to clear (mark messages 'seen') without consent.

**Impact.** Cross-site forgery of the 'mark thread as seen' action: an attacker who knows a thread id the victim participates in can silently clear the victim's unread indicator, potentially causing a missed message. Low blast radius (no data exfil, requires guessing a UUID thread id), but it is a CSRF-able state change on a GET.

**Suggested fix.** Move the last-seen write to a dedicated POST/PATCH guarded by requireAuthWithCsrf, or have the GET return the thread read-only and let the client mark-seen via the existing CSRF-protected POST. Side effects do not belong on GET.

**Verifier verdict:** `uncertain` — corrected severity P3.

> The code claim is accurate: GET /api/dm/threads/[id] unconditionally performs an UPDATE on every fetch (route.ts:82-86), setting the caller's aLastSeenAt/bLastSeenAt to now(), and GET uses only getSession() (route.ts:38) with no CSRF check. So "side effect on a GET" is a real code smell, and the proposed fix (move the last-seen write behind a CSRF-protected POST/PATCH) is reasonable defense-in-depth that matches the project's own "side effects do not belong on GET" principle. However, the finding's concrete attack model is materially wrong, which undercuts the claimed impact. The session cookie is set with sameSite: "lax" (auth.ts:119) and getSession() reads that same cookie (auth.ts:61, AUTH_CONFIG.cookieName). Under SameSite=Lax, the cookie is NOT attached to cross-site subresource requests — exactly the vectors the finding names: <img src>, <script src>, and cross-site fetch/XHR all omit the Lax cookie. Without the cookie, getSession() returns null → 401 at route.ts:39-40, and no write occurs. So the "silent" CSRF described (img/script/fetch carrying the session cookie) does not actually work. The only surviving vector is a cross-site top-level GET navigation (clicked link / window.location), which Lax does permit. But that (a) navigates the victim to a raw JSON page (visibly disruptive, not silent), (b) requires knowing a valid thread UUID the victim participates in — the UUID_RE check (route.ts:43) plus the ownership-scoped loadThread (route.ts:48, WHERE id = ? AND (userAId = me OR userBId = me)) means a guessed/random UUID returns 404 with no mutation, and a non-participant can never trigger any write, and (c) the entire effect is clearing the victim's own unread badge on one known thread — no data exfiltration, no cross-tenant write. P3 is an appropriate ceiling; the issue is a low-value side-effect-on-GET, not the exploitable CSRF the description implies. Verdict is uncertain rather than false_positive because the underlying design concern (mutation on GET with no CSRF token) is genuinely present even though the stated exploit vectors are refuted by SameSite=Lax.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/dm/threads/[id]/route.ts:38 (GET uses getSession() only); :43 (UUID_RE validation); :48 (loadThread ownership scoping: id AND userAId/userBId = caller); :82-86 (unconditional UPDATE of aLastSeenAt/bLastSeenAt on every GET). src/lib/auth.ts:59-63 (getSession reads AUTH_CONFIG.cookieName, no CSRF); :119 and :137 (sameSite: "lax" on the session cookie — refutes the img/script/fetch CSRF vectors). For contrast, the POST path is CSRF-guarded at route.ts:106 via requireAuthWithCsrf.

### 64. ⬜ PENDING · [P3] Engine trades with no fillPrice/limitPrice are skipped, orphaning their disposition and corrupting FIFO matching

- **Lane:** tax · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:80-95`

**Description.** In the engine-trades loop, `const price = t.fillPrice ?? t.limitPrice ?? 0; if (price <= 0) continue;` (lines 80-81) drops any FILLED row lacking both a fill price and a limit price (e.g. a market BUY whose fillPrice wasn't backfilled by the reconciler). Because this is a blanket skip applied to BUYs and SELLs alike, a skipped BUY removes a lot that a later SELL of the same symbol needs: that SELL then FIFO-matches against an older/wrong lot or, if no lot remains, emits no line — silently altering reported cost basis and gains. The same pattern exists in /api/tax/form8949 (lines 100-101). The export route avoids this by requiring isNotNull(fillPrice) at the SQL layer for the whole FIFO set, keeping the lot queue self-consistent.

**Impact.** Sporadic cost-basis errors and dropped/mis-matched lots whenever a FILLED row is missing price data (reconciler lag, partial backfill), producing a tax report that doesn't reconcile against the broker's own 1099-B.

**Suggested fix.** Either exclude price-less rows at the SQL layer consistently (so BUYs and SELLs are filtered as a coherent set) and surface a warning when rows are dropped, or backfill a price before FIFO so a missing price never desynchronizes the lot queue. Log when rows are skipped so the discrepancy is observable rather than silent.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. In src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:80-81, `const price = t.fillPrice ?? t.limitPrice ?? 0; if (price <= 0) continue;` is a blanket skip applied to FILLED rows regardless of action. The identical pattern exists in src/app/api/tax/form8949/route.ts:100-101. Both routes feed allTrades into the FIFO matcher in src/lib/tax-engine.ts (generateForm8949, lines 257-339): a BUY pushes a lot onto symbolLots (261-269); a SELL drains lots FIFO (273-338). If a BUY is skipped, its lot never enters the queue, so a later SELL of the same symbol matches an older/wrong lot or, with no lot remaining, the `while (remaining > 0 && symbolLots.length > 0)` loop body never runs and emits no line — a real cost-basis/lot desync, not a fabricated one. The cross-route inconsistency is also real: src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts:70 filters `isNotNull(traderTrades.fillPrice)` at the SQL layer, dropping BUYs and SELLs as one coherent set into a separate FIFO engine (tax-report.ts), so its lot queue stays self-consistent. However, the claimed impact is overstated. The precondition — a FILLED row with both fillPrice and limitPrice null/<=0 — is a degenerate edge, not the routine 'reconciler lag / partial backfill' the finding implies: (1) engine market orders are inserted via logTrade with orderType='market' and a NON-NULL quote placeholder for fillPrice (trading-engine.ts:5482 passes currentPrice, :3926/:4306/etc. pass quote.price), and limitPrice is never set for them (defaults null, line 43 schema), so the `?? limitPrice` fallback is effectively dead and the only price is the seeded placeholder; (2) the reconciler at :3582-3584 sets newFillPrice = brokerOrder.filledPrice ?? row.fillPrice (preserving the non-null placeholder) and only writes it when non-null (:3626), so a FILLED row almost always carries a non-null price; (3) the only null-fillPrice logTrade insert is the FAILED-order catch path (:5500), which does not reconcile to FILLED. The finding's specific trigger ('a market BUY whose fillPrice wasn't backfilled') therefore does NOT produce a skip, because that BUY already has a non-null placeholder. The tax engine is also explicitly a disclaimed estimator (tax-engine.ts:198-228 documents known §1091 limitations whose net direction overstates the user's liability). So the bug is a genuine latent inconsistency worth fixing (consistent SQL-layer filtering + a dropped-row warning), but it fires only on degenerate data. P3 is correct.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/tax/report/route.ts:80-81 (blanket `price = fillPrice ?? limitPrice ?? 0; if (price <= 0) continue`); src/app/api/tax/form8949/route.ts:100-101 (same pattern); src/lib/tax-engine.ts:261-269 (BUY pushes lot), :273-338 (SELL FIFO-drains; no line emitted when symbolLots empty); src/app/api/export/tax-report/route.ts:70 (coherent SQL-layer `isNotNull(traderTrades.fillPrice)`); src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:43,45 (limitPrice and fillPrice both nullable); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:3003 (engine orders orderType='market', limitPrice unset), :5482 (BUY logged with non-null currentPrice placeholder), :3584+:3626 (reconciler preserves non-null fillPrice, writes only when non-null), :5500 (only null-fillPrice insert is the failed-order catch path); src/lib/tax-engine.ts:198-228 (documented disclaimed-estimator stance).
