### 65. ✅ FIXED · [P3] No concurrency guard on the alert cron: overlapping runs double-fire and bypass the cooldown (TOCTOU)

- **Lane:** alerts · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:23-77`

**Description.** The cron has no in-flight lock or advisory lock. evaluateAlertRules reads rule.lastConditionMet and rule.lastTriggered, then awaits multiple DB writes (alert-engine.ts lines 124, 131-138) before persisting lastTriggered. Two overlapping invocations (cron retry, manual trigger, or a slow run still executing when the next */5 fires) both read lastConditionMet=false / a stale lastTriggered, both compute risingEdge=true, both pass the cooldown check, and both insert alertHistory + send a notification before either write lands. There is also a read-modify-write gap between reading lastTriggered and writing it.

**Impact.** Duplicate alert notifications and duplicate alert_history rows; the 1h cooldown is bypassed during the overlap window. Spammy/noisy alerts and a corrupted edge-trigger state if the two runs disagree on conditionMet.

**Suggested fix.** Wrap the cron body in a pg advisory lock (pg_try_advisory_lock on a fixed key) or an in-flight guard so overlapping runs skip. Make the lastTriggered/lastConditionMet update conditional (UPDATE ... WHERE last_triggered = <observed> or WHERE last_condition_met = false) so only one writer wins the edge.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The described code and TOCTOU mechanism are real and accurately characterized. The cron route (src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:23-77) has no in-flight lock or pg advisory lock — confirmed by reading the full handler; the codebase has the guard pattern elsewhere (screener.ts scanInFlight, trading-engine.ts:6158) but it is absent here. In src/lib/alert-engine.ts, evaluateAlertRules reads rule.lastConditionMet and rule.lastTriggered into decideAlert (lines 114-119), then awaits db.insert(alertHistory) (131), db.update lastTriggered (132), and sendNotification (133) BEFORE the lastTriggered write lands. Two overlapping invocations both observing lastConditionMet=false / a stale lastTriggered both compute risingEdge=true (decideAlert line 41), both pass the cooldown check (lines 43-45), and both fire — yielding duplicate alert_history rows and duplicate notifications and a one-window cooldown bypass. The read-modify-write gap is also real: line 132 does an unconditional `set({ lastTriggered })` with no `WHERE last_triggered = <observed>` predicate, and line 124 likewise overwrites lastConditionMet unconditionally. The schema (alerts.ts:22,26) confirms nullable last_triggered and boolean last_condition_met default false, matching the finding. evaluateAlertRules is called only from this cron (analyze-route trigger was removed per analyze/[symbol]/route.ts:125), so the only overlap sources are a slow run straddling the */5 boundary, a cron retry, or a manual GET — all plausible once scheduled. Severity downgraded to P3 because: (1) the impact is duplicate/spammy notifications and history rows plus a transient one-window cooldown bypass — noise, not money movement, cross-tenant leak, or durable corruption (the lastConditionMet disagreement self-heals on the next evaluation); (2) per CLAUDE.md line 263 the cron is 'Not yet wired into prod cron' and there is no vercel.json or crontab in the repo, so no scheduled or concurrent invocation is active today — the defect is latent until the operator adds the crontab entry; (3) actual overlap even then requires a slow run or coincident manual retry, and each rule's await chain is short, making the race window small.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:36-73 (no lock around the rows fetch + per-symbol loop calling evaluateAlertRules); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:114-119 (read lastConditionMet/lastTriggered), :41-45 (risingEdge + cooldown decision), :123-124 (unconditional lastConditionMet write), :131-133 (insert alertHistory, unconditional lastTriggered write, then sendNotification — write lands after the notification); src/lib/db/schema/alerts.ts:22,26 (last_triggered nullable, last_condition_met boolean default false); src/app/api/analyze/[symbol]/route.ts:125 (inline trigger removed — cron is sole caller); CLAUDE.md:263 ('Not yet wired into prod cron — add the crontab entry'); contrast guard pattern at src/lib/screener.ts:180-198 and src/lib/trading-engine.ts:6158-6200 (scanInFlight) showing the codebase has the missing pattern available.

### 66. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Rising edge suppressed by the 1h cooldown is permanently consumed — alert is lost, never re-fires while condition stays true

- **Lane:** alerts · **Category:** logic · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/alert-engine.ts:40-47, 114-127`

**Description.** When a rising edge lands inside the cooldown window, decideAlert returns { fire: false, persistState: true } (lines 43-44, confirmed by the test at tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts lines 34-41). evaluateAlertRules then writes lastConditionMet=true (line 124) but does NOT fire. On the next cron run the condition is still true → true→true → risingEdge is false → never fires, even after the cooldown elapses. The edge is swallowed: the only way to ever get the alert is for the condition to fully clear (re-arm) and then rise again. For a slow-moving threshold (e.g. price crossing above $X and staying there), the alert that was merely rate-limited is silently dropped forever.

**Impact.** Legitimate alerts are lost. The cooldown is documented as a 'secondary anti-spam guard' but here it converts a transient rate-limit into permanent suppression, so a user can miss the only notification they would ever get for a sustained condition.

**Suggested fix.** When a rising edge is suppressed purely by cooldown, do NOT mark the edge as consumed — keep lastConditionMet such that the rule re-evaluates and fires once the cooldown expires while the condition is still met. E.g. only persist lastConditionMet=true after a successful fire, or carry a separate 'pending fire' flag so the post-cooldown evaluation can deliver it.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The described mechanism is real and present exactly as claimed. In src/lib/alert-engine.ts, decideAlert (lines 40-47) returns { fire: false, persistState: true } when a rising edge lands inside the 1h cooldown (lines 43-44), and the test at tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts:34-41 explicitly confirms this ("suppresses a rising edge inside the cooldown but still consumes the edge"). evaluateAlertRules then writes lastConditionMet=conditionMet (=true) at line 124 and continues without firing (line 127) or touching lastTriggered (line 132 only runs on fire). On the next cron run with the condition still true, decideAlert(true, true, ...) yields risingEdge=false → { fire: false, persistState: false }, so the alert never fires even after the cooldown elapses. The only path to re-fire is a full false→true after a complete clear (re-arm). The cron (src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:47-64) runs ~5min with fresh per-symbol data and the per-rule state machine is the only gate, so nothing mitigates this upstream. HOWEVER, the finding's severity framing is inflated and partly inverted: the "slow-moving threshold that crosses above $X and stays there" example does NOT trigger the bug — a sustained single cross produces exactly one rising edge that fires immediately (no recent lastTriggered to block it). The bug only bites an oscillating condition that produces a SECOND rising edge within the 1h cooldown of a prior fire and then settles true — i.e. clear-then-re-rise inside an hour. That is precisely the oscillation scenario the cooldown exists to suppress (line 25 comment: "secondary anti-spam guard"), and the consumed-edge behavior is deliberately documented in the code comment and the unit test. It is also not "forever" in the sense the description implies: the rule re-arms and fires on the next genuine clear/rise. So it is a real, narrow, by-design rate-limiting trade-off that can drop one alert during an intra-hour oscillation, not a broad silent loss of the only notification a user would get.

**Evidence:** src/lib/alert-engine.ts:40-47 (decideAlert: risingEdge logic + cooldown returns fire:false,persistState:true); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:121-132 (writes lastConditionMet=true on persistState, continue on !fire, lastTriggered only updated on fire); src/lib/alert-engine.ts:25 (COOLDOWN_MS = 1h "secondary anti-spam guard"); tests/unit/alert-engine.test.ts:34-41 (explicitly asserts the edge is consumed inside cooldown — intended behavior); src/app/api/cron/evaluate-alerts/route.ts:47-64 (~5min cron, fresh data, no upstream mitigation)

### 67. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Bracket order forwards a one-sided bracket Alpaca rejects, and never passes qty validation as a guard

- **Lane:** brokers · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/brokers.ts:411-421`

**Description.** When orderClass==="bracket", placeOrder sends `order_class: bracket` but only attaches take_profit if takeProfitPrice is set and stop_loss if stopLossPrice is set (lines 413-418). The validator (validators.ts:339-346) allows a bracket with only ONE of the two legs. Alpaca's `bracket` order class requires BOTH a take_profit and a stop_loss leg — a single-leg request is rejected (single-leg is `oto`, not `bracket`). So a manual bracket order with only a stop-loss (or only a take-profit) is constructed here, sent, and bounced by Alpaca with a 422. Additionally, for a bracket the code still applies `limit_price` (line 402) and omits any qty/notional safety check at the client layer — it relies entirely on the validator to have rejected notional brackets.

**Impact.** User-submitted single-leg bracket orders are accepted by the API/validator, built here, and fail at the broker with an opaque 422 — a confusing dead-end rather than a clean client-side rejection. No money loss (broker rejects), but a correctness/UX defect on the manual order path.

**Suggested fix.** Either require both legs at the validator for orderClass bracket, or in placeOrder downgrade a single-leg bracket to order_class 'oto' (with the present leg) so the broker accepts it; don't send an order_class the broker will reject given the legs provided.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The finding is accurate and reproducible through the manual order path. (1) brokers.ts:411-418 sets order_class="bracket" and conditionally attaches take_profit only if takeProfitPrice is set and stop_loss only if stopLossPrice is set — exactly as described, so a single-leg bracket payload is constructible. (2) The validator at validators.ts:339-346 explicitly permits a bracket with only ONE leg: its refine requires (stopLossPrice OR takeProfitPrice), not both. (3) The frontend ticket at trade/[symbol]/page.tsx:204-208 enforces the same weak rule ("Bracket needs at least a take-profit or stop-loss"), presents two independent optional inputs (lines 543-565), and at lines 242-243 sends only the leg(s) the user filled — so a user can submit a one-sided bracket from the UI. (4) The route (api/broker/orders/route.ts:119-171) parses with the permissive schema and forwards parsed.data straight to placeOrder with no leg-count guard. (5) Alpaca's documented contract: the bracket order class requires BOTH a take_profit and a stop_loss leg; a single-leg request is an oto, not a bracket, and Alpaca returns a 422 for a one-sided bracket. The PlaceOrderParams type already lists "oto" (brokers.ts:100), confirming the proposed downgrade fix is feasible. The defect is confined to the manual path — the engine never constructs bracket orders (no matches in trading-engine.ts), so there is no money-loss exposure: Alpaca rejects pre-fill, and the route does surface err.userMessage (route.ts:231-235), so it is a confusing dead-end rather than a silent failure. The secondary limit_price point (line 402) is harmless (undefined for market brackets, valid for limit brackets) and not a bug. Impact is a genuine correctness/UX defect with no security or financial impact, matching P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/brokers.ts:411-418 (conditional single-leg bracket payload); src/lib/brokers.ts:100 (PlaceOrderParams orderClass includes "oto"); src/lib/validators.ts:339-346 (bracket refine allows stopLossPrice OR takeProfitPrice, not both); src/app/dashboard/trade/[symbol]/page.tsx:204-208 (validate() requires only one leg) and 240-243 (sends only present legs); src/app/api/broker/orders/route.ts:119-171 (schema parse then direct forward to placeOrder, no leg-count guard); trading-engine.ts has no bracket usage (grep for orderClass:"bracket"/takeProfitPrice/stopLossPrice returned no matches), confining the defect to the manual path

### 68. ✅ FIXED · [P3] AlpacaClient.cancelAllOrders swallows failures and treats 207 Multi-Status as success

- **Lane:** brokers · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** uncertain · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/brokers.ts:499-508`

**Description.** cancelAllOrders does `if (!res.ok) log.error(...)` and then returns void unconditionally — it never throws and never inspects the body. Two problems: (1) On a real failure (429/500/network) it logs and returns as if it succeeded; callers like cancelAllAndWait (trading-engine.ts:6699-6714) and flatten-all (6837-6840) `await client.cancelAllOrders()` and assume best-effort cancel happened. (2) Alpaca's DELETE /v2/orders returns HTTP 207 Multi-Status when SOME orders cannot be canceled, each with a per-order status in the body; 207 satisfies `res.ok` (200-299), so a partial-cancel failure passes completely silently — the per-order failure array is never read. cancelAllAndWait's polling loop partially compensates (it re-polls getOrders until clean or 5s timeout), but the other call sites do not, and a stuck order that never cancels just yields a silent 5s timeout with no signal.

**Impact.** During an emergency flatten / daily-loss halt, orders the broker refused to cancel (e.g. partially filled, held) stay live with no error surfaced. Pending protective/entry orders can fill after the engine believes it has flattened, leaving an unmanaged live position — a wrong-state-on-halt hazard on real money.

**Suggested fix.** Parse the response: on non-2xx throw a BrokerError; on 207 inspect each entry and throw (or return a structured partial-failure result) listing the order ids that failed to cancel so callers can retry those specifically rather than assuming a clean flatten.

**Verifier verdict:** `uncertain` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present exactly as described: cancelAllOrders (brokers.ts:499-508) does `if (!res.ok) { log.error(...) }` then returns void unconditionally — never throws, never inspects the body. The API-contract inconsistency the finding identifies is REAL: the sibling cancelOrder (488-497) throws BrokerError on non-2xx, while cancelAllOrders swallows it; and 207 Multi-Status satisfies res.ok (200-299) so a partial-cancel body is never read. That is a genuine reliability/observability weakness worth fixing. However the finding's framing is partly wrong and its headline impact is mischaracterized, which is why I cannot confirm it as the described P2: 1. FALSE sub-claim on network/timeout. brokerFetch (160-186) THROWS a BrokerError on abort/network failure BEFORE control reaches line 504. So 'on a real failure (429/500/network) it logs and returns as if it succeeded' is only true for HTTP error statuses (429/500) — network/timeout failures DO propagate and are caught+logged by every caller's try/catch. The finding overstates the swallow surface. 2. Liquidation correctness does NOT depend on the cancel return at any call site, so the catastrophic 'unmanaged live position after the engine believes it flattened' is overstated. (a) haltEngine flatten (6831-6890) re-reads broker positions via getPositions() and places a market sell per position; a market sell either fills (position closed) or errors on held_for_orders qty and is logged at 6757/6797. An uncancelled PROTECTIVE stop firing = the position closing, which is the flatten goal, not a hazard. (b) cancelAllAndWait (6699-6716) has a polling loop that re-verifies getOrders('open') and times out at 5s; the subsequent stop placement surfaces any held-qty error. (c) command-route flatten-all (113-139) re-reads toClose and market-sells each with per-order logging. (d) enforceDailyLossHalt (1404-1414) is explicitly documented best-effort (1335-1336) and its primary effect is engine.halted=true blocking new BUYs — it intentionally does not flatten. The only genuine residual hazard is narrow: a resting limit-BUY that fails to cancel on the daily-loss halt path (which does not flatten) could later fill into a halted engine that won't manage it — but engine BUYs are predominantly market orders that fill immediately, and engine.halted blocks new entries. That is a P3-level edge case, not a P2 wrong-state-on-real-money hazard. Verdict is uncertain rather than false_positive because the underlying defect (swallow on HTTP-error + unread 207) is real and the proposed fix (throw on non-2xx, parse 207 per-order) is a legitimate improvement — just not at the claimed severity or with the claimed impact.

**Evidence:** brokers.ts:499-508 (cancelAllOrders swallows !res.ok, returns void, never inspects body; 207 passes res.ok); brokers.ts:488-497 (sibling cancelOrder throws BrokerError on non-2xx — the inconsistency); brokers.ts:160-186 (brokerFetch THROWS BrokerError on network/abort before line 504 — refutes the network sub-claim); trading-engine.ts:1335-1336 + 1404-1414 (daily-loss halt cancel is documented best-effort, logged+continued, does not flatten); trading-engine.ts:1446-1450 (halt blocks BUYs but doesn't flatten — confirms cancel is not the flatten mechanism); trading-engine.ts:6699-6716 (cancelAllAndWait re-polls getOrders('open') and times out — independent verification); trading-engine.ts:6748-6759 / 6790-6798 (stop placement after cancel catches+logs held-qty errors); trading-engine.ts:6851-6890 (haltEngine re-reads broker positions and market-sells each — flatten correctness independent of cancel return); command/route.ts:113-139 (flatten-all re-reads toClose, per-order market sell + logging)

### 69. ✅ FIXED · [P3] fetchSectorMovers computes changePct without a prior-close > 0 guard (Infinity/NaN leak)

- **Lane:** data-ingestion · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/market-context.ts:163-181`

**Description.** fetchSectorMovers maps each symbol to changePct: ((curr - prev) / prev) * 100 with no guard on prev. Its sibling fetchQuoteWithChange (lines 129-141) explicitly guards `if (!(prev > 0)) return null;` before the same division, but fetchSectorMovers omits it. If a daily bar's close is 0 or otherwise non-positive (bad upstream tick that wasn't null-filtered in market-data), prev=0 yields Infinity/-Infinity (or NaN for 0/0). That value flows into sectorPerformance.avgChange (reduce/length) and the topGainers/topLosers sort in gatherMarketContext, and into topMovers for gatherChatContext, where it is JSON-serialized into the LLM/UI context.

**Impact.** A single zero/garbage close corrupts a sector's average change and the gainers/losers ranking shown to users and fed to the chat LLM; JSON.stringify(Infinity) serializes as null, silently dropping the number rather than surfacing an error.

**Suggested fix.** Mirror the sibling guard: after computing prev/curr, `if (!(prev > 0)) throw new Error('bad prior close')` (so allSettled drops it) or skip the symbol. Apply the same !(x>0) guard anywhere a prior close becomes a divisor.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> All three claims hold up against the actual code. (1) The cited code is present exactly as described: fetchSectorMovers computes changePct ((curr-prev)/prev)*100 at market-context.ts:167-171 with no positivity guard on prev, while the sibling fetchQuoteWithChange guards with if (!(prev > 0)) return null at line 139. The asymmetry between two functions doing the identical division, one guarded, one not, is concrete evidence the guard was intended and simply omitted. (2) Impact is real, not mitigated upstream: the only divisor source is provider.fetchBars in market-data.ts. The Yahoo primary provider filters bars only on == null (line 128), so a close of 0 or negative survives, becomes parseFloat 0, and becomes prev. The Finnhub provider copies close: data.c[i] (line 211) with no filter at all. So a non-positive prior close can reach the unguarded division, producing Infinity/-Infinity or NaN for 0/0. That value flows into sectorPerformance.avgChange via reduce/length (line 239), into the topGainers/topLosers sort (lines 244-246), and into gatherChatContext topMovers items (lines 313-318), all JSON-serialized into LLM/UI context. JSON.stringify(Infinity) serializes to null, silently dropping the value as claimed. (3) Severity is correctly low, not inflated. The affected symbols are large-cap liquid tickers (top-3 per sector plus SPY/QQQ/DIA), for which a 0 or negative daily close is very improbable, so the trigger is rare. When it fires, the corruption is confined to advisory context (a sector average and gainers/losers ranking shown to the user and fed to the chat LLM); it never touches money math, position sizing, or stop-loss, and Promise.allSettled isolates per-symbol throws. This matches the project guideline to guard external numbers with !(x>0) before money math. Real defect, low blast radius, P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/market-context.ts:167-171 unguarded division prev=bars[len-2].close, curr=bars[len-1].close, changePct: ((curr-prev)/prev)*100; src/lib/market-context.ts:139 sibling guard if (!(prev > 0)) return null; src/lib/market-data.ts:128 Yahoo filters only == null, not 0 or negative; src/lib/market-data.ts:211 Finnhub copies close with no filter; src/lib/market-context.ts:239 avgChange reduce/length consumes changePct; src/lib/market-context.ts:244-246 topGainers/topLosers sort on changePct; src/lib/market-context.ts:313-318 topMovers items JSON-serialized for chat LLM context

### 70. ✅ FIXED · [P3] SSRF: getFilingContent host allowlist uses endsWith("sec.gov") on a user-supplied URL

- **Lane:** data-ingestion · **Category:** security · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/sec-filings.ts:152-164`

**Description.** getFilingContent() fetches an arbitrary URL with only two guards: url.startsWith("https://") and parsed.hostname.endsWith("sec.gov"). The URL comes straight from the /api/filings/chat POST body (chatBodySchema.filingUrl: z.string().url().optional() — any well-formed https URL passes Zod, then is handed to getFilingContent at filings/chat/route.ts line 74). endsWith("sec.gov") is a substring/suffix allowlist, not an exact-host check: hostnames like 'evilsec.gov' or 'foosec.gov' satisfy it, and any future *.sec.gov subdomain takeover would too. There is no rejection of credentials-in-URL, non-standard ports, or redirect following (default fetch follows redirects, so a real sec.gov URL that 30x-redirects off-domain would still be followed and returned). The fetched body is then embedded into the LLM prompt and returned to the caller.

**Impact.** Authenticated premium user can coerce the server to issue outbound GETs to non-sec.gov hosts that merely suffix-match 'sec.gov', or to be redirected off-domain, and have the response body reflected back through the LLM answer — an SSRF / blind-fetch primitive. .gov registration restrictions narrow but do not eliminate the misconfigured-allowlist exposure (subdomain/redirect cases).

**Suggested fix.** Replace the suffix test with an exact-host allowlist: const h = parsed.hostname.toLowerCase(); if (h !== "www.sec.gov" && h !== "data.sec.gov" && !h.endsWith(".sec.gov")) return "";. Also set redirect:"manual" (or re-validate the final URL after redirect), reject any port other than 443, and reject URLs containing userinfo (parsed.username/parsed.password).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The described code is present and behaves exactly as claimed. In src/lib/sec-filings.ts:152-164, getFilingContent() guards a user-supplied URL with only two checks: url.startsWith("https://") (line 153) and parsed.hostname.endsWith("sec.gov") (line 156). The endsWith test has NO leading-dot boundary, so it is a suffix/substring allowlist: hostnames like "evilsec.gov" or "notsec.gov" satisfy it, as the finding states. There is no rejection of userinfo (username/password), no port restriction, and no redirect control — default fetch (lines 161-164) follows redirects, so a real sec.gov URL that 30x-redirects off-domain would be followed. The URL flows straight from the POST body: chatBodySchema.filingUrl is z.string().url().optional() (route.ts:16), so any well-formed https URL passes Zod, and it is handed to getFilingContent at route.ts:74 with no intermediate host validation. The fetched body is embedded into the LLM prompt (route.ts:79) and the model answer is returned to the caller (route.ts:107,111), confirming the response-reflection / blind-fetch primitive. So the vulnerability class (misconfigured suffix allowlist + no redirect/port/userinfo hardening) is REAL, not a false positive, and the proposed fix is sound. However the claimed impact is somewhat overstated for severity purposes: (1) the allowlist constrains the registrable name to end in literal "sec.gov", and .gov registration is gated by US-government policy (CISA/GSA) — an attacker cannot trivially register evilsec.gov, so this is NOT a general SSRF primitive against internal infrastructure (no 169.254.169.254, localhost, RFC1918, or arbitrary-external reach). (2) The endpoint requires an authenticated PREMIUM user (route.ts:30-35) plus a 10 req/min rate limit (route.ts:38). The exposure is thus a genuine hardening gap (suffix-vs-exact-host, redirect following, no userinfo/port rejection) but the practical blast radius is .gov-suffix-constrained and privilege-gated, so it is best rated P3 rather than P2.

**Evidence:** src/lib/sec-filings.ts:153 (`if (!url.startsWith("https://")) return "";`); src/lib/sec-filings.ts:156 (`if (!parsed.hostname.endsWith("sec.gov")) return "";` — no leading dot, suffix match); src/lib/sec-filings.ts:161-164 (fetch with no redirect:"manual"); src/app/api/filings/chat/route.ts:16 (`filingUrl: z.string().url().optional()`); route.ts:74 (`filingContext = await getFilingContent(filingUrl);`); route.ts:79 (filing content embedded in LLM prompt); route.ts:107,111 (model answer returned to caller); route.ts:30-35 (requireAuthWithCsrf + premium tier gate); route.ts:38 (rateLimit 10/60s).

### 71. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Earnings-blackout gate uses hardcoded 5-day window in canPlaceBuyOrder, ignoring the user's configured earningsBlackoutDays

- **Lane:** engine-buy-gates · **Category:** trading · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:888-923, 5308-5314, 3886-3923, 4267-4317, 4470-4502, 4509-4558`

**Description.** canPlaceBuyOrder gates entry on earnings blackout only when riskLimits.earningsBlackoutDays > 0 (line 907) but then calls isInEarningsBlackout(symbol) WITHOUT passing the days argument (line 909), so it always uses the function default of 5 (signature `blackoutDays: number = 5`, line 533). Only the main-scan path's passesSmartFilters (line 5312) passes the user's real value. The tactical, tactical-smart initial buy-in, swap-buy, and add paths do NOT call passesSmartFilters at all — they rely solely on canPlaceBuyOrder for earnings protection. Net effect: a user who configures a 10-day earnings blackout gets only 5 days of protection in those modes; a user who configures a 2-day blackout gets an over-wide 5-day blackout that suppresses valid trades.

**Impact.** Buys can be placed 6-10 calendar days into an earnings event for a user who asked for a 10-day buffer (engine trades into the exact volatility the setting was meant to avoid), or valid entries get blocked for a user who asked for a narrow 2-day window. The gate width silently diverges from the user's risk profile in every mode except the main optimized scan.

**Suggested fix.** Pass the configured value: `await isInEarningsBlackout(symbol, riskLimits.earningsBlackoutDays)` at line 909. Also make tactical/tactical-smart entry paths run passesSmartFilters (or document that canPlaceBuyOrder is the sole earnings gate for those modes and wire the value through).

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The code mechanic is exactly as described. In canPlaceBuyOrder, the earnings gate is entered only when riskLimits.earningsBlackoutDays > 0 (trading-engine.ts:907) but then calls isInEarningsBlackout(symbol) with NO days argument (line 909), so it always uses the function's default of 5 (signature `blackoutDays: number = 5`, line 533; window check `daysUntil <= blackoutDays`, line 589). The audit detail then reports the *configured* value (details.blackoutDays = riskLimits.earningsBlackoutDays, line 916), so the logged width diverges from the enforced width. By contrast, passesSmartFilters threads the real value through (line 678/680) and is called with it only on the main optimized scan (5312-5314) and the optimized swap-sell planner path (5598-5600). The tactical entry (3912), tactical-smart initial buy-in (4292), swap-buy (4477) and add (4547) paths rely solely on canPlaceBuyOrder and never call passesSmartFilters — confirmed by reading each path. So in those modes the gate width is hardcoded to 5, contradicting the function's own comment (522-532) that says it is 'now driven by the parameter.' That is a genuine latent correctness bug and the proposed one-line fix (pass riskLimits.earningsBlackoutDays at 909) is correct. HOWEVER the finding's user-facing impact is mischaracterized. earningsBlackoutDays is NOT user-configurable through any reachable path: it is absent from updateRiskProfileSchema (validators.ts:182-199, which only lists trailActivation*, maxDailyNotionalPct, maxConsecutiveLosses, etc.), and Zod .object() strips unknown keys, so the /api/risk-profile PATCH route silently drops any earningsBlackoutDays a client sends. There is no UI/component/API reference to it anywhere (grep of src/components and src/app returned zero matches; only the engine, schema, migration, and docs mention it). The DB column has no default (migration 0029 line 19: `earnings_blackout_days integer`, NULL), and loadRiskLimits resolves NULL to the RiskLimits default of 0 = disabled (lines 781, 806). With value 0 the gate at line 907 never fires. The scenario 'a user configures a 10-day blackout and gets only 5' therefore cannot happen for ordinary users — the gate only activates if someone sets a non-zero value via a direct/admin DB write, and only then does the hardcoded-5 bug bite. Real bug, real inconsistency, trivial fix — but no reachable user-facing blast radius today, so P2 is inflated; P3 fits a latent correctness defect.

**Evidence:** trading-engine.ts:909 `const inBlackout = await isInEarningsBlackout(symbol);` (no days arg) inside the `if (riskLimits.earningsBlackoutDays > 0)` guard at line 907; trading-engine.ts:533 `async function isInEarningsBlackout(symbol: string, blackoutDays: number = 5)`; trading-engine.ts:916 audit detail reports configured value while enforcement uses 5; trading-engine.ts:589 `if (daysUntil >= -1 && daysUntil <= blackoutDays) return true`. Main scan passes the value: trading-engine.ts:5312-5314; optimized swap-sell planner: 5598-5600. Tactical/smart paths use only canPlaceBuyOrder with NO passesSmartFilters: 3912, 4292, 4477, 4547. Mitigation evidence: validators.ts:182-199 (updateRiskProfileSchema omits earningsBlackoutDays); trading-engine.ts:781 `earningsBlackoutDays: 0, // 0 = disabled`; trading-engine.ts:806 `profile.earningsBlackoutDays ?? defaults.earningsBlackoutDays`; drizzle/0029_engine_intelligence.sql:19 `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS earnings_blackout_days integer;` (NULL default); zero matches for earningsBlackout in src/components and src/app.

### 72. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Risk-profile *Pct fields use inconsistent units (percent vs fraction) in the same table, a unit-confusion landmine for sizing/caps

- **Lane:** engine-buy-gates · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:795-804, 940-941, 988-989`

**Description.** loadRiskLimits treats maxPositionPct and maxDailyLossPct as percents (divides by 100 at lines 795, 797), but maxDailyNotionalPct and maxSectorExposurePct are consumed as raw fractions (line 988 uses maxDailyNotionalPct directly as a multiplier of equity; line 940-941 compares sectorPct, a fraction, directly against maxSectorExposurePct). The DB schema comments (strategies.ts lines 59, 62-63) confirm the split: maxDailyNotionalPct '0.5 = 50%', maxSectorExposurePct '0.25 -> 25%', while maxPositionPct is a whole-number percent. A direct DB edit (the only way to set the sector cap, per the wiring gap above) of maxSectorExposurePct=25 intending 25% yields a 2500% cap (disabled); maxDailyNotionalPct=50 intending 50% yields a 50x-equity cap.

**Impact.** Operators editing the risk profile by hand (currently the only path for the sector cap) will silently mis-scale caps by 100x, disabling protections they think they enabled, or over-tightening notional. The mixed convention also makes any future UI wiring error-prone.

**Suggested fix.** Standardize the stored unit for all *Pct fields (preferably fraction 0..1) and update loadRiskLimits accordingly, or rename the fraction-valued fields (maxDailyNotionalFrac, maxSectorExposureFrac) so the convention is unambiguous; add validator entries with the correct range for each.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The finding is accurate on every load-bearing point. (1) The cited code behaves exactly as described: loadRiskLimits divides maxPositionPct and maxDailyLossPct by 100 (trading-engine.ts:795, :797), treating them as whole-number percents, while maxDailyNotionalPct is passed through raw (:800) and consumed directly as an equity multiplier (notionalCap = bootEquity * maxDailyNotionalPct, :988) and maxSectorExposurePct is compared directly against a fraction (sectorPct = totalAfter/equity at :940, then sectorPct > maxSectorExposurePct at :941). (2) The schema/validator/UI all corroborate the split convention rather than contradict it: strategies.ts:59 comments maxDailyNotionalPct 'fraction (0.5 = 50% of equity)' and :62-63 'maxSectorExposurePct ... 0.25 -> 25%', whereas validators.ts:184/:187 bound maxDailyLossPct/maxPositionPct at 0.1-100 and 0.5-100 (whole percent) but maxDailyNotionalPct at 0-10 (fraction). The Trader UI labels confirm percent entry: 'Max Daily Loss (%)' default 2%, 'Max Position (%)' default 15% (page.tsx:1297-1315), sent raw. (3) The 'wiring gap' is real and stronger than claimed: maxSectorExposurePct is absent from updateRiskProfileSchema entirely (validators.ts:182-199) and appears in zero .tsx files (no UI anywhere), so the validated API path cannot set it — a direct DB edit is the only route, where entering 25 (meaning 25%) yields a 2500% cap that sectorPct can never exceed, silently disabling the protection (fail-open). maxDailyNotionalPct=50 (meaning 50%) yields a 50x-equity cap. The inconsistency is independently evidenced by the UI itself special-casing trailActivationProfitPct with an ad-hoc num/100 (page.tsx:1389) and an explanatory comment about the percent-vs-fraction mismatch, while leaving the fraction fields without any such surface. This is a genuine latent unit-confusion landmine, not an actively-firing bug — the running code is internally consistent; harm requires a hand DB edit with a wrong-unit guess. P3 is correct: maintainability/footgun severity, not a P2 mis-execution in normal operation.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:795 (maxPositionPct/100), :797 (maxDailyLossPct/100), :800 (maxDailyNotionalPct raw), :940-941 (sectorPct fraction compared to maxSectorExposurePct), :988 (bootEquity * maxDailyNotionalPct); src/lib/db/schema/strategies.ts:59 ('fraction (0.5 = 50%)'), :62-63 ('0.25 -> 25%'); src/lib/validators.ts:184/:187 (percent ranges) vs :192 (maxDailyNotionalPct 0-10 fraction) and absence of maxSectorExposurePct in updateRiskProfileSchema :182-199; src/app/dashboard/trader/page.tsx:1297-1315 (percent-labeled fields), :1389 (ad-hoc /100 for trailActivationProfitPct); Grep of maxSectorExposurePct across **/*.tsx returned no matches (no UI).

### 73. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Equity-collapse tripwire divides by boot.equity with no positive guard (NaN/Infinity on zero-equity boot)

- **Lane:** engine-risk-halts · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4836-4845, 5893-5896, 3729-3740`

**Description.** `const equityDrop = (engine.boot.equity - currentAccount.equity) / engine.boot.equity;` (4836) then `if (equityDrop > ACCOUNT_SWITCH_EQUITY_DROP_PCT)`. `engine.boot.equity` is set directly from `bootAccount.equity` at startEngine (5894) and re-snapshotted at each new trading day (3729-3740) with no `> 0` validation. If the broker reports equity 0 at boot (empty/fresh account, or a transient glitch), the divide yields NaN (current also 0) or -Infinity (current > 0); `NaN > 0.5` and `-Infinity > 0.5` are both false, so the equity-collapse safeguard silently fails to evaluate. It is a fail-open (no false halt) only in the degenerate zero-equity case, but the tripwire is then disabled until the next valid re-snapshot.

**Impact.** Account-integrity safeguard (50% equity-drop detection) is silently inert whenever the boot snapshot captured equity 0, including after a transient broker glitch at start. Low real-world frequency, but it's a safety control that can be disabled by bad upstream data with no log/alert.

**Suggested fix.** Guard the boot capture: reject/refuse to boot (or defer the tripwire) when `bootAccount.equity <= 0`, and in the check compute `equityDrop` only when `engine.boot.equity > 0`, else skip with a warn log rather than evaluating NaN/Infinity.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The code is present and behaves exactly as claimed. At src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4836 `const equityDrop = (engine.boot.equity - currentAccount.equity) / engine.boot.equity;` divides by `engine.boot.equity` with no positive guard, then 4837 gates `if (equityDrop > ACCOUNT_SWITCH_EQUITY_DROP_PCT)` where the constant is 0.5 (line 718). `engine.boot.equity` is captured unconditionally from `bootAccount.equity` at startEngine (5894), and `bootAccount = await resolved.client.getAccount()` (5843) has no `> 0` validation — the only boot rejections are getAccount() throwing (5844) and non-Alpaca brokers (5820). The re-snapshot at each new trading day (3739) likewise assigns `account.equity` with no guard. Crucially, the equity field is reachable as 0: `toNumber()` in brokers.ts (188-195) returns 0 for a missing/unparseable `data.equity`, and Alpaca account equity flows through it (brokers.ts:276). So an empty/fresh account or a transient parse glitch yields `engine.boot.equity = 0`. The arithmetic claim holds: 0/0 = NaN (`NaN > 0.5` is false) and (0 - X)/0 = -Infinity for X>0 (`-Infinity > 0.5` is false), so the equity-collapse safeguard silently fails to evaluate. My refutation attempts all failed: no upstream guard rejects zero-equity boot, the scenario is reachable, and the NaN is not handled anywhere. The finding is also honest about scope (fail-open, no false halt). P3 is correct: low real-world frequency (requires equity exactly 0 at boot/snapshot), a zero-equity account can't size positions anyway, and the next trading-day re-snapshot re-arms it — but the transient-glitch case leaves a safety control silently inert with no log/alert until the next valid snapshot, a genuine narrow gap.

**Evidence:** src/lib/trading-engine.ts:4836-4837 (divide by engine.boot.equity, no guard; threshold check); 718 (ACCOUNT_SWITCH_EQUITY_DROP_PCT = 0.5); 5841-5848 (bootAccount fetched, only throw is caught, no equity>0 check); 5893-5896 (engine.boot.equity = bootAccount.equity, no validation); 3729-3740 (new-day re-snapshot, no validation); src/lib/brokers.ts:188-195 (toNumber returns 0 on missing/NaN) and 276 (equity: toNumber(data.equity))

### 74. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Watchdog runs unguarded setInterval cycles that can overlap and double-process every engine

- **Lane:** engine-snapshot-watchdog · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:86-108, 173-181`

**Description.** startWatchdog() schedules runWatchdog() via raw setInterval every 60s with no in-flight guard. runWatchdog() loops over all engines and for each performs serial awaited DB queries (recentlyAlerted SELECT, writeAlert INSERT) and possibly an awaited sendPushToUser network call. With many engines or a slow DB/push provider, one cycle can exceed 60s, so the next interval fires while the prior cycle is still mid-loop. Two concurrent cycles both call recentlyAlerted() (which only finds rows committed by a PRIOR cycle, not the in-flight sibling) → both pass the dedup check for the same (userId, kind) → both INSERT a duplicate alert and both fire a duplicate push. The dedup is a classic check-then-act TOCTOU across the INSERT.

**Impact.** Duplicate engine alerts and duplicate push notifications during slow cycles; under sustained overlap, alert spam defeats the 15-min dedup window the module is designed around, and the DB INSERT/push load compounds (each overlap adds a full pass).

**Suggested fix.** Add an in-flight boolean guard on globalThis set synchronously at the top of the interval callback and cleared in finally (skip the tick if already running), as is standard for the project's schedulers. Same pattern should be applied to stop-sync-scheduler.ts.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The code matches the finding exactly. startWatchdog() (src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:173-181) schedules runWatchdog() via a raw setInterval at 60s with no in-flight guard; the globalThis __engineWatchdogId guard only prevents double-REGISTRATION, not overlapping ticks. runWatchdog() (86-165) iterates all engine snapshots and, when a condition fires, performs serially-awaited recentlyAlerted() SELECT (37-50), writeAlert() INSERT (60-66), and for severity 'error' an awaited sendPushToUser() (75) which itself does a DB SELECT + serial per-subscription network sends (push.ts:54-66). recentlyAlerted() is a textbook check-then-act: it only matches rows already COMMITTED (gt createdAt cutoff), so two overlapping cycles both pass the check for the same (userId, kind) before either INSERT lands. There is no DB-level backstop: migration drizzle/0012_engine_watchdog.sql:1-18 deliberately omits a unique constraint ("we don't need a unique constraint here" — dedup is code-only), and schema/trader.ts:123-135 confirms only two non-unique btree indexes. So the duplicate-INSERT + duplicate-push TOCTOU is mechanically real. However the impact is mischaracterized as P2. (1) This is a single-node, single-process deployment (instrumentation.ts boots one process; one interval registered) so overlap requires a single cycle to exceed 60s, not multiple schedulers. (2) The loop is cheap in steady state: getAllEngineSnapshots() (trading-engine.ts:7161-7190) is pure in-memory iteration; DB/push I/O runs ONLY when a condition is actively true AND not yet within the 15-min dedup window. Once an alert is written, the dedup suppresses re-fires for 15 min, capping DB-touching passes to ~1 per engine per 15 min. A >60s cycle therefore needs many engines simultaneously in a fresh firing-but-undeduped state, or a pathologically slow DB/push provider. (3) The blast radius when it does occur is a DUPLICATE alert row + duplicate push notification (UX annoyance), not money movement or state corruption. The "alert spam defeats the dedup window / load compounds per pass" claim is overstated — the overlap window is bounded (the subsequent cycle sees the now-committed rows), yielding a ~2× multiplier, not unbounded spam. The proposed in-flight guard is sound hardening and the same gap exists verbatim in the sibling stop-sync-scheduler.ts (which the finding notes), but the consequence is cosmetic, so P3 not P2.

**Evidence:** src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:175-179 (raw setInterval, no in-flight guard; __engineWatchdogId guards registration only at 174); src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:37-50 (recentlyAlerted check-then-act on committed rows only); src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:60-66,73-83 (awaited INSERT then awaited sendPushToUser per error alert); src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:93-164 (serial per-engine awaited loop); src/lib/push.ts:54-66 (sendPushToUser DB SELECT + serial per-sub network sends); drizzle/0012_engine_watchdog.sql:4-18 (no unique constraint — code-only dedup, explicit comment); src/lib/db/schema/trader.ts:123-135 (only non-unique indexes on engine_alerts); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7161-7190 (getAllEngineSnapshots is pure in-memory, cheap); src/instrumentation.ts:46-48 (single registration at boot, single process); src/lib/stop-sync-scheduler.ts:90-101 (sibling scheduler shares the identical no-guard pattern, refuting "guard is standard for the project's schedulers")

### 75. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Watchdog daily-loss warning uses a fabricated $50k basis, so it mis-fires on both large and small accounts

- **Lane:** engine-snapshot-watchdog · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:123-149`

**Description.** The daily_loss_warn condition compares the absolute realized loss against conservativeHaltDollars = s.dailyLossLimit * 50_000 (line 137), warning at 80% of that. dailyLossLimit is a fraction of equity (default 0.02). The real engine halts on dailyLossLimit × actual equity at scan time, not × $50k. getAllEngineSnapshots does not expose equity, so the watchdog invents a $50k denominator. Consequences: a $500k account (real halt ≈ $10k) gets warned at 0.8 × 0.02 × 50k = $800 — it will alert on essentially every losing day, far below any real halt risk; a $5k account (real halt ≈ $100) is not warned until an $800 loss — by then the engine has long since auto-halted, so the 'warn before halt' purpose never triggers. The denominator is a fabricated basis disconnected from the user's actual equity.

**Impact.** False/early daily-loss alerts (push notifications, severity warn) for large books, and effectively no pre-halt warning for small books — the feature's stated goal ('warn so the user can react before auto-halt') is not met across the realistic account-size range.

**Suggested fix.** Expose the per-engine equity (or the engine's last computed dailyLossThreshold in dollars) on getAllEngineSnapshots and compare lossMag against the real threshold (dailyLossLimit × equity), or have the engine record the actual halt-dollar line on state and read it here. Drop the hardcoded $50k floor.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The finding is accurate in every load-bearing detail. (1) The cited code is present exactly as described: engine-watchdog.ts:137 computes `conservativeHaltDollars = s.dailyLossLimit * 50_000` and line 138 warns at `DAILY_LOSS_WARN_FRAC (0.8) * conservativeHaltDollars`, behind a `!s.halted && s.dailyLossLimit > 0 && s.dailyLoss < 0` guard (line 128). (2) `dailyLossLimit` is confirmed to be a fraction, not dollars: it is initialized to 0.02 (trading-engine.ts:314), DEFAULT_DAILY_LOSS_PCT = 0.02 (line 713), and is assigned directly from `riskLimits.dailyLossPct` (lines 1364, 4739), which itself is `profile.maxDailyLossPct / 100` (line 797) — a unitless fraction. (3) The real halt uses live equity, not $50k: trading-engine.ts:1365 computes `dailyLossThreshold = equity * riskLimits.dailyLossPct` and halts when `dailyLoss <= -dailyLossThreshold` (line 1366), setting `engine.halted = true` (line 1378). (4) getAllEngineSnapshots (lines 7161-7190) exposes dailyLoss and dailyLossLimit but NOT equity, so the watchdog genuinely has no real basis and substitutes the hardcoded $50k floor. The math checks out in both directions: a $500k book (real halt ~$10k) is warned at $800 (0.8 × 0.02 × 50k), firing on nearly every losing day far below real halt risk; a $5k book (real halt ~$100) would need an $800 loss to warn, but by then the engine has already set halted=true at $100, so the `!s.halted` guard (line 128) suppresses the warn entirely — the pre-halt warning never fires for small books. The feature's stated goal ('warn before auto-halt', per comment line 123) is not met across the realistic account-size range. The impact is real but confined to an advisory push-notification path; it does not affect trade execution, position sizing, or the real halt control (which correctly uses live equity). P3 is the correct severity — a correctness defect in a non-critical alerting feature, not a money/core-safety bug. The author even acknowledges the approximation in comments (lines 125-136), but the chosen constant produces wrong behavior. The proposed fix (expose equity or the engine's dollar threshold on the snapshot and compare against the real line) is sound.

**Evidence:** src/lib/engine-watchdog.ts:128 (guard `!s.halted && s.dailyLossLimit > 0 && s.dailyLoss < 0`), :137 (`const conservativeHaltDollars = s.dailyLossLimit * 50_000;`), :138 (`if (lossMag >= DAILY_LOSS_WARN_FRAC * conservativeHaltDollars)`); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:314 (`dailyLossLimit: 0.02`), :713 (`DEFAULT_DAILY_LOSS_PCT = 0.02`), :797 (`dailyLossPct = profile.maxDailyLossPct / 100`), :1364-1366 (`engine.dailyLossLimit = riskLimits.dailyLossPct; const dailyLossThreshold = equity * riskLimits.dailyLossPct; if (engine.dailyLoss > -dailyLossThreshold) return false;`), :1378 (`engine.halted = true;`), :7161-7190 (getAllEngineSnapshots exposes dailyLoss + dailyLossLimit, no equity field)

### 76. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Stop-sync scheduler can overlap with itself / the boot cycle, racing concurrent replaceOrder on the same broker stop

- **Lane:** engine-snapshot-watchdog · **Category:** concurrency · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/stop-sync-scheduler.ts:56-101`

**Description.** startStopSyncScheduler() uses raw setInterval (5 min) with no in-flight guard, and instrumentation.ts (lines 60-64) fires an immediate runStopSyncCycle() at boot in addition to starting the interval. runStopSyncCycle iterates every engine and awaits syncBrokerStopsForUser → syncBrokerStops (trading-engine.ts 6506+), which has no in-flight guard either: it reads open orders, computes a replacement stop, and calls client.replaceOrder(orderId, ...). If a cycle runs long (many engines × per-symbol replaceOrder over Finnhub/Alpaca latency) the next 5-min tick can start before it finishes, or the boot immediate-run overlaps the first scheduled tick. Two concurrent syncBrokerStops for the same user both fetch the same open stop order id and both issue replaceOrder against it. Alpaca's replaceOrder cancels-and-replaces, so the second call references an order id the first already replaced → rejected/erroring call (logged & swallowed in syncBrokerStops), and in the worst interleaving a stop can be left momentarily without a resting order while two replacements churn.

**Impact.** Wasted replaceOrder calls counting against Alpaca's order rate limit, error noise, and a transient window where a protective stop order may be in flux (cancelled-not-yet-replaced) on a live position. The scan-tail race is mitigated by the scanStartedAt gate, but the scheduler-vs-scheduler and boot-vs-scheduler races are not.

**Suggested fix.** Add an in-flight guard on globalThis in the setInterval callback (skip if a cycle is still running). Additionally guard syncBrokerStops per-userId (a per-user in-flight Set claimed synchronously before the first await) so the in-scan tail call, the bootstrap run, and the scheduled tick can never double-replace the same order.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The described code exists exactly as claimed. (1) startStopSyncScheduler (stop-sync-scheduler.ts:90-101) uses a raw setInterval with no in-flight guard — the callback just fires runStopSyncCycle().catch(...). (2) instrumentation.ts:60-64 starts that interval AND fires an immediate `void runStopSyncCycle()` at boot, so two independent cycle triggers exist. (3) runStopSyncCycle (56-88) iterates engines sequentially with `await syncBrokerStopsForUser`, so a single cycle never self-races, but nothing prevents a second cycle from starting while a slow one is mid-flight. (4) syncBrokerStopsForUser (trading-engine.ts:7404-7446) gates only on no_engine / engine_stopped / no_positions / scan_in_flight(scanStartedAt). The scanStartedAt gate defends only against the in-scan tail call; it does NOT defend scheduler-vs-scheduler or boot-vs-scheduler, exactly as the finding states. (5) syncBrokerStops (6506+) itself has no per-user in-flight guard: it reads open stop orders (getOrders, 6524), maps by symbol (6529-6534), computes targetStop, and calls client.replaceOrder!(existing.id, ...) (6653). (6) Confirmed no `__stopSyncInFlight`/`stopSyncRunning` global exists anywhere (grep: no matches). (7) Alpaca replaceOrder (brokers.ts:510-543) is a PATCH that cancel-and-replaces; a non-ok response throws BrokerError (523), which syncBrokerStops swallows in its catch — so a doubled replace produces a swallowed error, not a crash. So the mechanism is real. BUT: the race only materializes if one cycle runs longer than the 5-min interval (or longer than the 5-min boot→first-tick gap). Stop-sync does NOT run the Finnhub-paced hybrid analyzer loop that caused the documented scan hangs — resolveStrategy is a param lookup, not a market-data fetch — so a >5-min stop-sync cycle requires either large engine counts on one process or severe broker latency, neither of which the current single-process/few-engine deployment makes likely. And the worst-case impact is self-healing: Alpaca's replace is atomic server-side, so the first replacement leaves a resting stop; the losing second call just errors and is swallowed. No demonstrated lost-stop, no rate-limit accounting impact in-process (syncBrokerStops never calls recordOrderPlacement, so these calls don't even touch the 30/min engine limiter — they only hit Alpaca's generous server-side limit). This is a genuine but low-likelihood, low-blast-radius hardening gap (add a cheap globalThis in-flight skip + per-user claim), which is P3, not P2.

**Evidence:** src/lib/stop-sync-scheduler.ts:90-101 (setInterval, no in-flight guard); :56-88 (sequential per-cycle iteration); src/instrumentation.ts:60-64 (startStopSyncScheduler() + immediate void runStopSyncCycle() at boot); src/lib/trading-engine.ts:7404-7446 (syncBrokerStopsForUser gates only running/positions/scanStartedAt — scanStartedAt comment at 7421-7443 explicitly only covers the in-scan tail race); :6506-6534,6649-6660 (syncBrokerStops: no in-flight guard, getOrders→map→replaceOrder!(existing.id)); src/lib/brokers.ts:510-543 (Alpaca replaceOrder PATCH cancel-and-replace, throws BrokerError on non-ok at 523); grep for __stopSyncInFlight/stopSyncRunning = no matches; trading-engine.ts:716,1004-1013,1022 (ORDER_RATE_LIMIT 30/min via recentOrderTimestamps) — but syncBrokerStops never calls recordOrderPlacement (recordOrderPlacement call sites at 2868/3924/4304/4489/4559/5191/5440/5626 are all in scan/exit paths, none in syncBrokerStops), so these replaceOrder calls bypass the in-process limiter entirely.

### 77. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Momentum confidence scoring hardcodes denominators (0.05 and 3) — NaN/Infinity/negative on tuned params

- **Lane:** momentum-rs · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:319-330`

**Description.** compositeScore is built from tightnessScore = 1 - tightness/params.maxTightness, impulseScore = (impulsePct - params.minImpulsePct)/(0.05 - params.minImpulsePct), and volumeScore = (volumeMultiple - params.minVolumeSurge)/(3 - params.minVolumeSurge). The literals 0.05 and 3 are fixed score ceilings while minImpulsePct/minVolumeSurge are MomentumParams. If a tuned config sets minImpulsePct >= 0.05 the impulse denominator is 0 (→ Infinity) or negative (→ inverted score); minVolumeSurge >= 3 does the same for volume; maxTightness = 0 makes tightnessScore divide by zero. Any of these poisons compositeScore and the confidence math (lines 334-341), producing NaN/Infinity confidence or a STRONG_BUY/BUY chosen from garbage. MomentumParams is explicitly described as 'exported for tests + tuning', so this is reachable the moment an optimizer feeds non-default params.

**Impact.** A future tuning/optimizer path passing aggressive thresholds yields NaN/Infinity confidence and wrong signal classification. Currently latent (route only uses DEFAULT_MOMENTUM_PARAMS), but it is a correctness landmine on the documented tuning surface.

**Suggested fix.** Derive the score ceilings from params (e.g. impulse range = max(epsilon, impulseCeiling - minImpulsePct) with an explicit impulseCeiling param > minImpulsePct), clamp each sub-score to [0,1] with Math.max(0, Math.min(1, ...)), and guard every denominator with a positive-floor before dividing.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present and behaves exactly as described. momentum-analyzer.ts:319 divides by params.maxTightness (0 → divide-by-zero); :320-323 divides by the hardcoded ceiling expression (0.05 - params.minImpulsePct), which is 0 when minImpulsePct===0.05 and negative when minImpulsePct>0.05; :324-328 divides by (3 - params.minVolumeSurge), 0/negative when minVolumeSurge>=3. The Math.min(1, ...) caps only the upper bound — there is no lower clamp — so a negative or NaN sub-score flows into compositeScore (:329-330) and the confidence math (:334-341). The fixed ceilings (0.05, 3) contradicting the parameterized floors (minImpulsePct, minVolumeSurge) is a genuine defensive-coding defect on the documented tuning surface.\n\nHowever, the production impact is mischaracterized. The bug is purely latent: the only non-test caller is the momentum route (route.ts:82), which calls analyzeMomentumBars(symbol, bars) with no params arg, so it defaults to DEFAULT_MOMENTUM_PARAMS (analyzer line 152). Under defaults the denominators are all safely positive (0.05-0.02=0.03, 3-1.5=1.5, maxTightness=0.5) — no NaN/Infinity/negative score is reachable today. A grep for analyzeMomentumBars and MomentumParams shows NO optimizer or trading-engine consumer that passes tuned params; the engine signal pipeline uses analyzeBars/analyzeHybrid, not this analyzer. The 'optimizer feeds aggressive thresholds' scenario is hypothetical future code with no current call site. The finding itself concedes it is 'currently latent.' Real defect, but the claimed impact (NaN/Infinity confidence and wrong STRONG_BUY/BUY classification in a live path) does not occur in the current codebase, and the denominators are data-independent so any future tuning consumer would hit it deterministically and immediately. This is a defensive-correctness landmine, not a live numeric bug — P2 overstates it; P3 fits a latent code-quality issue with no reachable trigger.

**Evidence:** src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:319 (tightnessScore = 1 - pattern.tightness / params.maxTightness); :320-323 (impulseScore = Math.min(1, (impulsePct - minImpulsePct) / (0.05 - minImpulsePct))); :324-328 (volumeScore = Math.min(1, (volumeMultiple - minVolumeSurge) / (3 - minVolumeSurge))); :329-330 (compositeScore weighted sum, no lower clamp); :334-341 (confidence derived from compositeScore). DEFAULT_MOMENTUM_PARAMS at :31-40 (minImpulsePct 0.02, maxTightness 0.5, minVolumeSurge 1.5 — all denominators positive). Default-param signature at :152 (params: MomentumParams = DEFAULT_MOMENTUM_PARAMS). Sole non-test caller src/app/api/momentum/analyze/route.ts:82 passes no params. Grep confirms no optimizer/engine caller passes non-default MomentumParams (only route + tests).

### 78. ✅ FIXED · [P3] avgConsVol guard `/ k || 1` fabricates a volume surge on zero-volume consolidation

- **Lane:** momentum-rs · **Category:** numeric · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:127-130`

**Description.** avgConsVol = consolidation.reduce((s,b)=>s+b.volume,0) / k || 1 parses as (sum/k) || 1. When every consolidation bar has volume 0, sum/k = 0, and 0 || 1 substitutes 1 share. volumeMultiple then = current.volume / 1 = the breakout bar's full volume (e.g. 50,000), which trivially clears minVolumeSurge, so volumeSurge = true and the setup is scored as volume-confirmed. The intended guard ('treat zero avg as 1') is wrong here: a flat/halted consolidation with no volume should NOT auto-confirm the breakout.

**Impact.** A consolidation with zero recorded volume (illiquid/halted small-cap, sparse premarket minute bars) yields a fabricated volume surge and an unwarranted BUY/STRONG_BUY. Bounded to the analyzer's displayed signal, but it is exactly the thin-liquidity regime this small-cap scanner targets.

**Suggested fix.** Reject the setup when avgConsVol is not > 0 (continue/skip) rather than substituting 1, or compute volumeSurge = avgConsVol > 0 && current.volume/avgConsVol >= minVolumeSurge so a zero-volume base never confirms.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present verbatim and behaves exactly as the finding claims. At src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:127-128 the expression `consolidation.reduce((s, b) => s + b.volume, 0) / k || 1` parses (JS precedence: `/` binds tighter than `||`) as `(sum / k) || 1`. When every consolidation bar has volume 0, `sum/k = 0`, and `0 || 1` substitutes 1 share. Then line 129 `volumeMultiple = current.volume / 1 = current.volume`, which for any non-trivial breakout volume trivially clears `params.minVolumeSurge` (default 1.5, line 34), so line 130 sets `volumeSurge = true`. That flag is the decisive gate: line 299 short-circuits to HOLD (confidence 0.4) when `!volumeSurge`, while `volumeSurge === true` falls through to the scoring block (lines 318-341) that emits BUY (line 339) or STRONG_BUY (line 335). So a flat/halted zero-volume base does fabricate a "volume-confirmed" breakout and an unwarranted BUY/STRONG_BUY signal — the exact thin-liquidity small-cap regime this analyzer targets (docstring lines 9-12). The proposed fix (`volumeSurge = avgConsVol > 0 && current.volume/avgConsVol >= minVolumeSurge`) is correct. Severity stays P3, not higher, because impact is genuinely bounded: the only non-test caller is POST /api/momentum/analyze (route.ts:82), an explicitly display-only, trader-tier-gated endpoint whose own docstring states "Caller decides whether to act — the route never places orders" (route.ts:9-10). It is NOT wired into the live trading engine, the screener push-feed, or any order-placement path (grep for analyzeMomentumBars/momentum-analyzer returns only the test file and this one route). The consequence is therefore a misleading "(confirmed)" badge / inflated confidence on a manual analyze screen, not a fabricated order. Real numeric-correctness flaw, low blast radius — confirmed at P3.

**Evidence:** src/lib/indicators/momentum-analyzer.ts:127-130 (`/ k || 1` guard, volumeMultiple, volumeSurge); :286-296 (reasons text "(confirmed)" vs "(unconfirmed)"); :299-316 (HOLD short-circuit when !volumeSurge); :334-341 (STRONG_BUY/BUY scoring reached only when volumeSurge true); :31-40 (minVolumeSurge default 1.5). Caller: src/app/api/momentum/analyze/route.ts:9-10 ("never places orders"), :82 (sole non-test invocation). Grep confirms no engine/screener/order-path caller. Existing tests cover surge/no-surge (tests/unit/momentum-analyzer.test.ts:130-165) but not the zero-volume base case.

### 79. ✅ FIXED · [P3] RSResult.rsScore JSDoc contradicts the implemented growth-factor formula

- **Lane:** momentum-rs · **Category:** correctness · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/lib/relative-strength.ts:7-14, 127-129`

**Description.** The RSResult interface comments rsScore as 'symbol return / benchmark return' (line 9) — the naive signed ratio that the code's own block comment (lines 119-126) explicitly rejects because it inverts through a negative benchmark. The actual computation is the correct growth-factor ratio rsScore = (1+returnPct/100)/(1+benchmarkReturnPct/100) (lines 127-129), and the UI badge/stat thresholds (>=1 outperform) are written against the growth-factor semantics. The stale interface doc is duplicated verbatim in the page component (src/app/dashboard/relative-strength/page.tsx:21-28).

**Impact.** No runtime defect — the math and UI are consistent and correct. Risk is maintenance: a future edit trusting the interface comment could 'fix' rsScore back to the inverting return/return ratio (smaller losses ranked as outperformance in a down market). Documentation-only.

**Suggested fix.** Update both JSDoc comments to describe the growth-factor ratio ((1+r)/(1+b): >1 outperformed, <1 underperformed, 1 parity) so the contract matches the implementation.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The core finding is real and matches the code. src/lib/relative-strength.ts:10 comments rsScore as `// symbol return / benchmark return` — the naive signed ratio. The block comment at lines 119-126 explicitly rejects that formula because it inverts through a negative benchmark (with SPY at -10%, a +5% stock gives 5/-10=-0.5 looking like underperformance, while a -20% stock gives -20/-10=+2.0 looking like outperformance). Lines 127-129 implement the correct growth-factor ratio rsScore = (1+returnPct/100)/(1+benchmarkReturnPct/100). The UI is written against growth-factor semantics, confirming the parity point is 1.0: page.tsx:164 (`if (score >= 1.0) return "bullish"`), :197 ("Outperforming" = rsScore >= 1), :198 ("Underperforming" = rsScore < 1). So the interface JSDoc contradicts both the implementation and the UI contract. Impact is documentation-only, exactly as the finding states — no runtime defect; the math and UI are consistent and correct. The maintenance risk (a future edit "fixing" rsScore back to the inverting return/return ratio per the stale comment) is genuine. One factual error in the finding: it claims the stale doc is "duplicated verbatim in the page component (page.tsx:21-28)" — this is FALSE. The page's RSResult interface (lines 21-28) has no inline comment on rsScore or any field. The stale comment exists only at the single line src/lib/relative-strength.ts:10. This narrows the defect's scope and makes the proposed "update both JSDoc comments" fix half-spurious (the page has nothing to update), but does not negate the confirmed core issue. P3 is the correct severity for a cosmetic/doc-only inconsistency.

**Evidence:** src/lib/relative-strength.ts:10 (`rsScore: number;          // symbol return / benchmark return`); src/lib/relative-strength.ts:119-126 (block comment rejecting the naive ratio); src/lib/relative-strength.ts:127-129 (growth-factor formula `benchFactor !== 0 ? (1 + returnPct / 100) / benchFactor : 1`); src/app/dashboard/relative-strength/page.tsx:164 (`if (score >= 1.0) return "bullish"`); src/app/dashboard/relative-strength/page.tsx:197-198 (Outperforming = rsScore>=1, Underperforming = rsScore<1); src/app/dashboard/relative-strength/page.tsx:21-28 (page RSResult interface with NO comment on rsScore — contradicts finding's "duplicated verbatim" claim)

### 80. ✅ FIXED · [P3] Admin tier-change route uses a loose id regex instead of strict UUID validation

- **Lane:** security-auth-tenant · **Category:** correctness · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** high
- **Location:** `src/app/api/admin/users/[id]/tier/route.ts:42-45`

**Description.** PATCH /api/admin/users/[id]/tier validates the path id with /^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/i, which accepts any 36-char string of hex digits and dashes in any arrangement (e.g. 36 dashes, or hyphens in wrong positions) rather than a well-formed UUID. Every other [id] route in this scope uses the canonical UUID_RE (8-4-4-4-12). The loose pattern relies on a malformed-but-36-char value simply not matching any users.id row (returning 404). It is admin-gated, so not a privilege issue, but the inconsistency is a latent correctness hazard: a malformed value that still happens to be a valid Postgres uuid cast could reach the query, and the route diverges from the codebase's UUID validation convention.

**Impact.** Low: admin-only route, malformed ids 404 rather than mutate. The risk is reduced input hygiene and divergence from the strict-UUID convention used by the broker/layout/DM [id] routes, which could mask future bugs if the query path changes.

**Suggested fix.** Use the same strict UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i used by the other [id] routes.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The cited code is present exactly as described. src\app\api\admin\users\[id]\tier\route.ts:43 validates the path id with /^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/i, which matches any 36-char string of hex digits and dashes in any arrangement (36 dashes, hyphens in wrong positions) rather than the 8-4-4-4-12 UUID structure. The dominant codebase convention IS the strict UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i, used by dashboard/layouts/[id], broker/connections/[id]/activate, dm/threads/[id], watchlists/[id] (+ sub-routes), and support/tickets/[id] — so this route does diverge from convention, confirming the finding's core claim. One factual overstatement in the finding: it claims "Every other [id] route in this scope uses the canonical UUID_RE." That is false — admin/reddit-subreddits/route.ts:224 and trader/trades/[id]/post-mortem/route.ts:59 also use the same loose /^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/i. So the loose pattern is a small shared sub-convention, not a one-off. This does not change the substance: the route still diverges from the dominant strict pattern. Impact is low and correctly scoped. The route is admin-gated (requireAuthWithCsrf(request,["admin"]) at line 39) and the query is parameterized Drizzle (eq(users.id,id)) — no injection, no cross-tenant or privilege risk. users.id is a Postgres uuid column (schema/users.ts:12). The finding's speculation that "a malformed value that still happens to be a valid Postgres uuid cast could reach the query" is essentially moot: a malformed-but-36-char value (e.g. 36 dashes) is NOT a valid uuid cast, so the SELECT at lines 71-75 (which, unlike the UPDATE, is NOT wrapped in try/catch) would throw and yield a 500 rather than a clean 400. The worst realistic outcome is therefore a 500 instead of a 400/404 for an admin who hand-crafts a malformed id — no mutation occurs (the throw is on the read, before the UPDATE). This is a genuine input-hygiene/convention-divergence correctness nit, exactly as the finding frames it, at the lowest severity tier. Not inflated; P3 is correct.

**Evidence:** src\app\api\admin\users\[id]\tier\route.ts:43 (`if (!/^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/i.test(id))`); strict convention: src\app\api\dashboard\layouts\[id]\route.ts:17, src\app\api\broker\connections\[id]\activate\route.ts:24, src\app\api\dm\threads\[id]\route.ts:14, src\app\api\watchlists\[id]\route.ts:21, src\app\api\support\tickets\[id]\route.ts:16 (all `/^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i`); same-loose-pattern counterexamples to the "every other" claim: src\app\api\admin\reddit-subreddits\route.ts:224 and src\app\api\trader\trades\[id]\post-mortem\route.ts:59; admin gate: tier\route.ts:39; parameterized query + unguarded read: tier\route.ts:71-75 (no try/catch) vs UPDATE try/catch at 88-125; uuid column type: src\lib\db\schema\users.ts:12 (`id: uuid("id").primaryKey().defaultRandom()`).

### 81. ✅ FIXED · [P3] trader/command runs tier DB lookup and body parse before rate-limit gate

- **Lane:** security-auth-tenant · **Category:** reliability · **Verifier:** confirmed · **Finder confidence:** medium
- **Location:** `src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:24-43`

**Description.** POST /api/trader/command executes checkTier(auth.userId, 'trader') — a DB query — and request.json() BEFORE the rateLimit('trader-cmd:'+userId, 10, 60) check (rateLimit is invoked at line 40, after checkTier at line 31). An authenticated user (including a free-tier user who always fails the tier check) can hammer the endpoint and incur an unbounded number of checkTier DB round-trips per second, since the per-user rate limiter only short-circuits after those queries already ran. The cheap in-memory rate-limit gate should precede the DB-touching tier check.

**Impact.** A single authenticated account can drive unbounded DB load (one users-table SELECT per request) against /api/trader/command, bypassing the intended 10/60s throttle for the expensive work. Minor DoS / cost amplification.

**Suggested fix.** Reorder so rateLimit() runs immediately after auth (before checkTier and before request.json()). The rate-limit gate is the cheapest check and should be first among the per-user gates.

**Verifier verdict:** `confirmed` — corrected severity P3.

> The finding is factually accurate. In src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts the POST handler runs requireAuthWithCsrf (line 25, in-memory CSRF + JWT verify, no DB), then checkTier(auth.userId, "trader") at line 30, then request.json() at line 33-38, and only reaches rateLimit("trader-cmd:"+userId, 10, 60) at line 40. checkTier (src/lib/tiers-server.ts:74) calls getUserTier, which runs a real DB SELECT on the users table (lines 50-56) — albeit an indexed primary-key lookup wrapped in a 3s statement timeout. rateLimit (src/lib/rate-limiter.ts:13-32) is a pure in-memory globalThis Map operation with no I/O, so it is unambiguously the cheaper gate and the global "cheapest predicate first" guidance supports running it before the tier DB query. Since auth performs no rate-limiting, an authenticated (and CSRF-valid) account — including a free-tier user who always fails the tier check — can issue requests faster than 10/60s and incur one users-table PK SELECT per request before the throttle short-circuits. The claim that the per-user rate limiter only fires after the DB query is correct. The "unbounded DB load" framing is mildly overstated: the request still requires a valid session cookie and CSRF token (not anonymous), the query is a single indexed lookup with a statement timeout, and this same checkTier-before-rateLimit ordering is the established convention across ~6 other routes (chat, screener, trader/dashboard, insights, filings/chat), so it is a systemic hardening nit rather than a unique defect. The behavior is real and the proposed reorder is a legitimate defense-in-depth improvement, but the practical impact (one cheap indexed SELECT per request from an authenticated account) is minor. P3 is appropriate and not inflated.

**Evidence:** src/app/api/trader/command/route.ts:25 (requireAuthWithCsrf, no DB/rate-limit), :30-31 (checkTier — DB query), :33-38 (request.json), :40-43 (rateLimit gate runs last); src/lib/tiers-server.ts:48-64 (getUserTier runs users-table SELECT) and :74-84 (checkTier delegates to getUserTier); src/lib/rate-limiter.ts:13-32 (in-memory Map, no I/O); src/lib/auth.ts:153-175 (requireAuthWithCsrf with no roles arg does only CSRF + JWT verify, no DB, no throttle); convention confirmed across src/app/api/chat/route.ts:22,26 / screener/route.ts:74,77 / trader/dashboard/route.ts:21,25 / insights/[symbol]/route.ts:18,22 / filings/chat/route.ts:34,38 (all checkTier before rateLimit).
